\displaystyle+\kappa[Z^{I},\Gamma\beta]\Delta\theta+\kappa\Gamma\beta[Z^{I},{\rm div\,}]\nabla\theta+\kappa Z^{I}(\Gamma\nabla\beta\cdot\nabla\theta)-\kappa\Gamma\nabla\beta\cdot Z^{I}\nabla\theta.

Multiplying the equation (
2.5
) by
\kappa(\gamma^{2}\varrho^{I},u_{\varepsilon}^{I},\frac{(\gamma-1)C_{v}}{R}\theta^{I})
and integrating in space and time, one finds the identity:

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\big(\gamma^{2}|\varrho^{I}|^{2}+\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta)}|u_{\varepsilon}^{I}|^{2}+\frac{(\gamma-1)C_{v}^{2}}{R^{2}}|\theta^{I}|^{2}\big)(t)\mathrm{d}x+\frac{(\gamma-1)C_{v}}{R}\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\beta\Gamma|Z^{I}\nabla\theta|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mu\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\lambda_{1}\Gamma|Z^{I}\nabla u|^{2}+(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma|Z^{I}{\rm div\,}u|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(2.9)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{K}}_{0}+{\mathcal{K}}_{1}+\cdots+{\mathcal{K}}_{6},

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\big(\gamma^{2}|\varrho^{I}|^{2}+\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta)}|u_{\varepsilon}^{I}|^{2}+\frac{(\gamma-1)C_{v}^{2}}{R^{2}}|\theta^{I}|^{2}\big)(0)\,\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\big(\partial_{t}(\frac{1}{R\beta})+{\rm div\,}(\frac{1}{R\beta}u)\big)|u_{\varepsilon}^{I}|^{2}+{\rm div\,}u\big(\gamma^{2}|\varrho^{I}|^{2}+\frac{(\gamma-1)C_{v}^{2}}{R^{2}}|\theta^{I}|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma Z^{I}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}(u_{\varepsilon}^{I}\cdot\textbf{n})+\lambda_{1}\Gamma(Z^{I}\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\textbf{n})\cdot u_{\varepsilon}^{I}\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s=\colon{\mathcal{K}}_{21}+{\mathcal{K}}_{22},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{3}

\displaystyle=\kappa^{2}\frac{(\gamma-1)C_{v}}{R}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\beta\Gamma(Z^{I}\nabla\theta\cdot\textbf{n})\theta^{I}\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{4}

\displaystyle=-\kappa^{2}\frac{(\gamma-1)C_{v}}{R}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\beta\theta^{I}\nabla\Gamma\cdot Z^{I}\nabla\theta+\beta\Gamma Z^{I}\nabla\theta\cdot[\nabla,Z^{I}]\theta\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{5}

\displaystyle=-\kappa\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})(u_{\varepsilon}^{I}\cdot\nabla\Gamma+\Gamma[{\rm div\,},Z^{I}]u_{\varepsilon})Z^{I}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\lambda_{1}\big((\nabla\Gamma\cdot Z^{I}\nabla u_{\varepsilon})\cdot u_{\varepsilon}^{I}+\Gamma Z^{I}\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\cdot[\nabla,Z^{I}]u_{\varepsilon}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

(2.10)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{6}

\displaystyle=\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\gamma^{2}{\mathcal{C}}_{\varrho}^{I}\varrho^{I}+\frac{(\gamma-1)C_{v}}{R}{\mathcal{C}}_{\theta}^{I}\theta^{I}+{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon}}^{I}\cdot u_{\varepsilon}^{I}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

Note that hereafter,
\mathrm{d}S_{y}
denotes the surface measure of
\Omega.

We will control
{\mathcal{K}}_{0}-{\mathcal{K}}_{6}
term by term. First of all,
it follows from the assumption (
1.34
) and the property (
1.33
) that:

(2.11)

\displaystyle c_{0}\leq\beta(\theta)(t,x),\,\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma)(t,x)\leq{1}/{c_{0}},\quad\,\forall(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\Omega.

Consequently, we have by the definition of
Y_{m}(0)
in (
1.25
) and Remark
1.5
that, for any
(\mu,\kappa)\in A,\varepsilon\in(0,1],

(2.12)

{\mathcal{K}}_{0}\lesssim\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\varepsilon u,\varepsilon\sigma,\theta)(0)\|_{H_{co}^{m}}^{2}\lesssim Y^{2}_{m}(0).

Thanks to (
2.11
),
it holds also that

|\!|\!|(\partial_{t}(\frac{1}{R\beta}),{\rm div\,}(\frac{1}{R\beta}u),{\rm div\,}u)|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,u,\partial_{t}\theta,\nabla\theta,{\rm div\,}u)|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

The term
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}
can thus be controlled as:

(2.13)

{\mathcal{K}}_{1}\lesssim T\kappa\|(\varrho^{I},u_{\varepsilon}^{I},\theta^{I})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

In order to control the term
{\mathcal{K}}_{2},
we estimate successively
{\mathcal{K}}_{21},{\mathcal{K}}_{22}.
First, for
{\mathcal{K}}_{21},
in light of the fact
(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{k}(u\cdot\textbf{n})|_{\partial\Omega}=0
for
k\leq m-1,
we may assume

Z^{I}
contains at least one spatial tangential derivative, say
Z^{I}=\partial_{y}Z^{\tilde{I}}.
By using the duality between
H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)
and
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)
and the trace inequality (
15.21
),
one controls
{\mathcal{K}}_{21}
as:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{21}

\displaystyle=\kappa\mu(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Gamma Z^{I}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}[Z^{I},\textbf{n}\cdot]u_{\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s

(2.14)

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\mu|{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}|[Z^{I},\textbf{n}\cdot]u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon},{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla u,u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\varepsilon{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

We refer to (
1.12
) for the definition of the norm
L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}.

Second, for
{\mathcal{K}}_{22},
we split it further into two terms:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{22}

\displaystyle=\kappa\mu\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Gamma(Z^{I}\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\textbf{n})\cdot u_{\varepsilon}^{I}\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\kappa\mu\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Gamma\big([\textbf{n}\cdot,Z^{I}]\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\cdot u_{\varepsilon}^{I}+Z^{I}\Pi\partial_{\textbf{n}}u_{\varepsilon}\Pi u_{\varepsilon}^{I}+[\Pi,Z^{I}]\partial_{\textbf{n}}u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\Pi u_{\varepsilon}^{I}\big)\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\mu\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Gamma Z^{I}\partial_{\textbf{n}}u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\textbf{n}(u_{\varepsilon}^{I}\cdot\textbf{n})\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s=\colon{\mathcal{K}}_{221}+{\mathcal{K}}_{222}.

Thanks to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the term
{\mathcal{K}}_{221}
can be bounded as:

{\mathcal{K}}_{221}\lesssim\kappa\mu|u_{\varepsilon}^{I}|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{y}^{2}}\big(|\nabla u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}+|Z^{I}\Pi\partial_{\textbf{n}}u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{y}^{2}}\big).

By the virtue of the identity

(2.15)

\partial_{\textbf{n}}u\cdot\textbf{n}={\rm div\,}u-(\Pi\partial_{y^{1}}u)^{1}-(\Pi\partial_{y^{2}}u)^{2},

and the boundary condition (
1.16
), one finds that:

|\nabla u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}+|Z^{I}\Pi\partial_{\textbf{n}}u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim|(u_{\varepsilon},\partial_{y}u_{\varepsilon})|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}+|{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}.

Application of the trace inequality (
15.20
) then yields:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{221}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\mu|u_{\varepsilon}^{I}|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{y}^{2}}\big(|(u_{\varepsilon},\partial_{y}u_{\varepsilon})|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}+|{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}+\|\nabla({\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon},u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}+\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2},

where
\|\cdot\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}
is defined in (
1.9
) that precludes the highest time derivatives
\|(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{m}\cdot\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}.

By using the identity (
2.15
), the boundary condition (
1.16
) and the
trace inequality (
15.21
),
the term
{\mathcal{K}}_{222}
can be treated in the similar way as
{\mathcal{K}}_{21}:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{222}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\mu|\partial_{\textbf{n}}u_{\varepsilon}|_{{}_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}}|[Z^{I},\textbf{n}\cdot]u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}(u_{\varepsilon},\partial_{y}u_{\varepsilon},{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon})|_{{}_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}}|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}u|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\big(\|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}^{2}+\|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon},\nabla u,u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\big)\lesssim\varepsilon{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

The above two inequalities lead to that

{\mathcal{K}}_{22}\lesssim\varepsilon{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2},

which, combined with (
2.1
), yields:

(2.16)

{\mathcal{K}}_{2}\lesssim\varepsilon{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Let us proceed to estimate
{\mathcal{K}}_{3}.
In view of the Neumann boundary condition
\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta|_{\partial\Omega}=0
, we can assume that
Z^{I}
contains at least one spatial tangential derivative
\partial_{y}
(say
Z^{I}=\partial_{y}Z^{\tilde{I}},|\tilde{I}|\lesssim|I|-1
), otherwise it vanishes. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2}\big|[Z^{I},\textbf{n}]\nabla\theta\big|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}|\partial_{y}\theta|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}\lesssim|\kappa\nabla\theta|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}|\kappa\partial_{y}\theta|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}.

Applying again the trace inequality (
15.20
), one can control
{\mathcal{K}}_{3}
in the following way:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\|\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\kappa\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\kappa\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\big(\|\kappa\nabla\partial_{y}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\kappa\partial_{y}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\kappa\partial_{y}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Note that in the last inequality, we have used the fact:

\|\kappa\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\kappa\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Next, for the term
{\mathcal{K}}_{4},
we use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to find:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\kappa\theta^{I}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|\kappa[\nabla,Z^{I}]\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}})\|\kappa Z^{I}\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\varepsilon\sigma|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}\big)

(2.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\kappa\theta^{I}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|\kappa\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}})\|\kappa Z^{I}\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\varepsilon\sigma|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Let us remark that when controlling the term

[\nabla,Z^{I}]\theta,
we have used the following identity
which can be shown by induction:

(2.18)

[Z^{I},\partial_{i}]=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{|{J}|\leq|I|-1}c_{I,{J}}Z^{{J}}\partial_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{|{{J}}|\leq|I|-1}d_{I,{J}}\partial_{j}Z^{{J}}

where
J
is a
(M+1)
multi-index and
c_{I,J},d_{I,J}
are smooth functions that depend on
I
,
J
,
i
and the derivatives (up to order
|I|
) of
\nabla\phi
,
\partial_{i}
is the derivation in the standard Euclidean coordinates.

Following the similar arguments as in the estimate of
{\mathcal{K}}_{5}
, we can control the next term
{\mathcal{K}}_{5}
as:

(2.19)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{5}\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}\|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Finally, for the term
{\mathcal{K}}_{6}
which is defined in (
2.10
), we use again the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to get:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{6}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\varrho^{I},u_{\varepsilon}^{I},\theta^{I})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}\|({\mathcal{C}}_{\varrho}^{I},{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon}}^{I},{\mathcal{C}}_{\theta}^{I})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}.

It will be shown very soon in the next lemma that:

\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}({\mathcal{C}}_{\varrho}^{I},{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon}}^{I},{\mathcal{C}}_{\theta}^{I})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which, together with the previous inequality, yields:

(2.20)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{6}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Collecting the estimates (
2.12
)-(
2.13
), (
2.16
)-(
2.1
) and (
2.19
), (
2.20
),
we find (
2.4
) by using the energy identity (
2.1
).
∎

Lemma 2.3
.

Under the same assumptions as in the the Proposition
2.1

and given
Z^{I}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{k},k\leq m-1
or
Z^{I}=Z_{j}Z^{\tilde{I}},|I|\leq m-1,j=1,\cdots M,
the following estimate holds:

\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|({\mathcal{C}}_{\varrho}^{I},{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon}}^{I},{\mathcal{C}}_{\theta}^{I})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{\varrho}^{I},{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon}}^{I},{\mathcal{C}}_{\theta}^{I}
are defined in (
2.6
)-(
2.8
).

Proof.

We begin with the estimate
{\mathcal{C}}_{\varrho}^{I}.
For the first term
[Z^{I},u\cdot\nabla]\varrho,

we write:

[Z^{I},u\cdot\nabla]\varrho=[Z^{I},u,\nabla\varrho]+u\cdot[Z^{I},\nabla]\varrho+\nabla\varrho\cdot Z^{I}u,

where

[Z^{I},u,\nabla\varrho]=Z^{I}(u\cdot\nabla\varrho)-Z^{I}u\cdot\nabla\varrho+u\cdot Z^{I}\nabla\varrho.

One can thus use the identities (
2.18
) and (
15.6
) to obtain that:

(2.21)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|[Z^{I},u,\nabla\varrho]+u\cdot[Z^{I},\nabla]\varrho\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla\varrho\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}})\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\nabla\varrho|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m+1}{2}],\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Note that in view of the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}={\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u,\theta)
in (
1.21
), it holds by the assumption
m\geq 7
that:

(2.22)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|(\nabla(\theta,u),\nabla^{2}(\kappa\theta,\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}u))|\!|\!|

\displaystyle{}_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla(\sigma,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\theta,\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}u)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\sigma,u,\theta,\kappa\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m+1}{2}],\infty,t}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, it is direct to see that
\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\varrho\cdot Z^{I}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}|\!|\!|\nabla\varrho|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t},
which, combined with (
2.21
),
yields that:

\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}[Z^{I},u\cdot\nabla]\varrho\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Next, by using again the identity (
2.18
), we find that:

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}[Z^{I},{\rm div\,}]u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Combining the above two inequalities, we finish the estimate of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\varrho}^{I}
and find that:

(2.23)

\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\varrho}^{I}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We now proceed to control the commutator
{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon}}^{I}={\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon,1}}^{I}+{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon,2}}^{I}+{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon,3}}^{I}
defined in (
2.7
).
Simply replacing
\varrho
by
u_{\varepsilon}
, the term
{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon,1}}^{I}
can be controlled in a similar way as the first term of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\varrho}^{I}:

(2.24)

\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon,1}}^{I}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

For
{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon,1}}^{I},
we use the commutator estimate (
15.3
) and the identity (
2.18
)
to get that:

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon,2}}^{I}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|(Z\beta,Z(\Gamma\beta))\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}+\|(\nabla\sigma,\mu{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u_{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(Z\beta,Z(\Gamma\beta))|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\nabla\sigma,\mu{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u_{\varepsilon})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big).

Applying the estimates (
15.10
), (
15.11
) and using (
2.22
), we then derive that:

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon,2}}^{I}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}+\|(\nabla\sigma,\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Next, in view of the identity (
2.18
), the term
{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon,3}}^{I}
can be controlled as:

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon,3}}^{I}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The previous two inequalities, together with (
2.24
), yield:

(2.25)

\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{C}}_{u_{\varepsilon}}^{I}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

It remains to deal with the term
{\mathcal{C}}_{\theta}^{I}
defined in (
2.8
). The first two terms can be controlled in a similar way as
{\mathcal{C}}_{\varrho}^{I},
the other terms can be handled with the help of product estimate (
15.1
), commutator estimate (
15.3
) as well as the properties for
\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma),\beta(\theta)
in Corollary
15.3
. For instance, the term

\kappa[Z^{I},\Gamma\beta]\Delta\theta
can be bounded in the following way:

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\kappa[Z^{I},\Gamma\beta]\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|Z(\Gamma\beta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|Z(\Gamma\beta)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\kappa\Delta\theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

After some (slightly lengthy) further computations, we would find:

(2.26)

\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\theta}^{I}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The proof is thus complete in view of estimates (
2.23
), (
2.25
), (
2.26
).
∎

2.2.
\varepsilon-
dependent estimate-II

In this subsection, we aim to control
\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\varepsilon\nabla\sigma,\varepsilon{\rm div\,}u,\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}.
More precisely, the following result will be shown:

Proposition 2.4
.

Under the same assumption as in Proposition
2.1
, we have that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

(2.27)

\displaystyle\kappa\|(\nabla(\varrho,\theta),{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\kappa\mu\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim Y^{2}_{m}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Proof.

Denote
h_{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{R\beta}u\cdot\nabla u_{\varepsilon}+\nabla\sigma-\mu\Gamma{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u_{\varepsilon},
and rewrite the equation of
u_{\varepsilon}
in (
2.3
) as:

\frac{1}{R}\partial_{t}u_{\varepsilon}+\beta(\theta)h_{\varepsilon}=0.

Taking a vector field
Z^{J}(|J|\leq m-1),
applying
{\rm div\,}Z^{J}
on the above equations,
multiplying by
\frac{\kappa}{\beta(\theta)}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon},
and integrating in space and time, we find that:

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{R\beta}|Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}|^{2}(t)\mathrm{d}x-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{J}(\beta h_{\varepsilon})\nabla\big(\frac{1}{\beta}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\big)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(2.28)

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{R\beta}|Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}|^{2}(0)\mathrm{d}x-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{R\beta}\varepsilon\partial_{t}[{\rm div\,},Z^{J}]u\cdot Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{R\beta}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}(Z^{J}\varepsilon\partial_{t}u)\cdot\textbf{n}\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s.

Let us write further the second term in the L.H.S. of the above identity as:

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{J}(\beta h_{\varepsilon})\nabla

\displaystyle\big(\frac{1}{\beta}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\big)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s=-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{J}\nabla\sigma\cdot\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(2.29)

\displaystyle+\kappa\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma|Z^{J}(\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s-\sum_{j=3}^{7}{\mathcal{I}}_{j},

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{3}=\frac{\kappa}{R}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{J}(u\cdot\nabla u_{\varepsilon})\nabla\big(\frac{1}{\beta}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{4}=-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\nabla\beta}{\beta}\cdot(Z^{J}\nabla\sigma-\mu\Gamma Z^{J}{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u_{\varepsilon})Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

(2.30)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{5}=\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\big([Z^{J},\beta]\nabla\sigma-\mu[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u_{\varepsilon}\big)\nabla\big(\frac{1}{\beta}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{6}=-\kappa\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma Z^{J}(\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon})[\nabla,Z^{J}]{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{7}=\kappa\mu\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma Z^{J}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

Next, applying
Z^{J}\nabla
and
\nabla Z^{J}
to the first and the third equation of (
2.3
) respectively, we find that:

(2.31)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)(Z^{J}\nabla\varrho)+\frac{1}{\gamma\varepsilon}\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}=\mathscr{C}_{\varrho}^{J},\\[5.0pt]
\frac{C_{v}}{R}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)Z^{J}\nabla\theta+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}-\kappa\nabla Z^{J}[\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)]=\mathscr{C}_{\theta}^{J},\end{array}\right.

where

(2.32)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\varrho}^{J}=\colon-Z^{J}(\nabla u\cdot\nabla\varrho)-[Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]\nabla\varrho-\frac{1}{\gamma}[Z^{J},\nabla]{\rm div\,}u,

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{\theta}^{J}=\colon-\frac{C_{v}}{R}\big([\nabla,Z^{J}]\partial_{t}\theta+[\nabla Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]\theta\big).

Multiplying the equations (
2.31
) by
\kappa(\gamma^{2}Z^{J}\nabla\varrho,\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}Z^{J}\nabla\theta)
, integrating in space and time, we find after suitable integration by parts that:

\displaystyle\quad\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\gamma^{2}|Z^{J}\nabla\varrho|^{2}+\frac{C_{v}^{2}(\gamma-1)}{R^{2}}|Z^{J}\nabla\theta|^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma\beta|Z^{J}\Delta\theta|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(2.33)

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\gamma^{2}|Z^{J}\nabla\varrho|^{2}+\frac{C_{v}^{2}(\gamma-1)}{R^{2}}|Z^{J}\nabla\theta|^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}x-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{J}\nabla\sigma\cdot\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\sum_{j=8}^{12}{\mathcal{I}}_{j},

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{8}=\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}Z^{J}[\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)]Z^{J}\nabla\theta\cdot\textbf{n}\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{9}=-\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma\beta Z^{J}\Delta\theta[{\rm div\,},Z^{J}]\nabla\theta\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

(2.34)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{10}=-\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\big([Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\Delta\theta+Z^{J}(\Gamma\nabla\beta\cdot\nabla\theta)\big){\rm div\,}(Z^{J}\nabla\theta)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{11}=\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}{\rm div\,}u\big(\gamma^{2}|Z^{J}\nabla\varrho|^{2}+\frac{C_{v}^{2}(\gamma-1)}{R^{2}}|Z^{J}\nabla\theta|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{12}=\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\gamma^{2}\mathscr{C}_{\varrho}^{J}\cdot Z^{J}\nabla\varrho+\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}\mathscr{C}_{\theta}^{J}\cdot Z^{J}\nabla\theta\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

Note that the relation
\gamma\nabla\varrho+\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}\nabla\theta=\varepsilon\nabla\sigma
has been used in the derivation of (
2.2
).
Collecting (
2.2
), (
2.2
) and (
2.2
), we find the following identity:

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}|Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}|^{2}+\gamma^{2}|Z^{J}\nabla\varrho|^{2}+\frac{C_{v}^{2}(\gamma-1)}{R^{2}}|Z^{J}\nabla\theta|^{2}\big)(t)\,\mathrm{d}x

(2.35)

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})|Z^{J}(\nabla{\rm div\,}u)|^{2}+\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}\Gamma\beta|Z^{J}\Delta\theta|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s=\sum_{j=0}^{12}{\mathcal{I}}_{j},

where
{\mathcal{T}}_{3}-{\mathcal{I}}_{7}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{8}-{\mathcal{T}}_{11}
are defined in (
2.2
)-(
2.2
)
and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{0}=\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}|Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}|^{2}+\gamma^{2}|Z^{J}\nabla\varrho|^{2}+\frac{C_{v}^{2}(\gamma-1)}{R^{2}}|Z^{J}\nabla\theta|^{2}\big)(0)\,\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{1}=-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{R\beta}\varepsilon\partial_{t}[{\rm div\,},Z^{J}]u\cdot Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{2}=\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{R\beta}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}(Z^{J}\varepsilon\partial_{t}u)\cdot\textbf{n}\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s.

To get the desired energy estimates, it suffices to control the terms
{\mathcal{T}}_{0}-{\mathcal{T}}_{12}
which is the task of the following proof.
By the definition of
Y_{m}(0)
in (
1.25
)
and the property (
2.11
),
one can find that:

(2.36)

{\mathcal{I}}_{0}\lesssim\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(\varepsilon\sigma,\varepsilon u,\theta)(0)\|_{H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0).

For the term
{\mathcal{I}}_{1},
we use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the assumption (
1.34
) and the identity (
2.18
) to get that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\big\|Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\big\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}[{\rm div\,},Z^{J}]u\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

(2.37)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

For the next term
{\mathcal{I}}_{2},
one can assume that
Z^{J}
contains at least one spatial tangential derivative
\partial_{y^{i}}\,(i=1,2),
otherwise it vanishes identically. By
the fact

Z^{J}(\varepsilon\partial_{t}u)\cdot\textbf{n}=[Z^{J}\varepsilon\partial_{t},\textbf{n}]u

and by the duality between
H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)
and
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega),
one finds that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{2}\lesssim\kappa\big|{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\big|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}\big|[Z^{J}\varepsilon\partial_{t},\textbf{n}]u\big|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

The trace inequality (
15.21
) then yields:

(2.38)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{2}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla{\rm div\,}u,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla u,u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\varepsilon{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

We now focus on the estimates of
{\mathcal{I}}_{3}-{\mathcal{I}}_{12}
which are defined in (
2.2
), (
2.2
). Let us begin
with the term
{\mathcal{I}}_{3}.

\underline{\text{Estimate of }{\mathcal{I}}_{3}}.
To avoid losing derivatives, we integrate by parts in space to write:

{\mathcal{I}}_{3}={\mathcal{I}}_{3,1}+{\mathcal{I}}_{3,2}+{\mathcal{I}}_{3,3},

where

(2.39)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{I}}_{3,1}=\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}{\rm div\,}(\frac{u}{R\beta})|Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,\quad{\mathcal{I}}_{3,2}=\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{R\beta}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}Z^{J}(u\cdot\nabla u_{\varepsilon})\cdot\textbf{n}\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{I}}_{3,3}=-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\big([{\rm div\,},Z^{J}](u\cdot\nabla u_{\varepsilon})+Z^{J}(\nabla u\cdot\nabla u_{\varepsilon})+[Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\big)\frac{1}{R\beta}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

Thanks to (
2.11
), the first term
{\mathcal{I}}_{3,1}
can be controlled as:

(2.40)

{\mathcal{I}}_{3,1}\lesssim T\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|({\rm div\,}u,\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim T\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Let us now bound
{\mathcal{I}}_{3,2}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{3,2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{3}{4}}|{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}\kappa^{\frac{1}{4}}|Z^{J}(u\cdot\nabla u_{\varepsilon})\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}.

Since
(u\cdot\nabla)|_{\partial\Omega}=(u_{1}\partial_{y^{1}}+u_{2}\partial_{y^{2}})|_{\partial\Omega}
is a tangential vector and
(u\cdot\textbf{n})|_{\partial\Omega}=0,

one can write

Z^{J}(u\cdot\nabla u_{\varepsilon})\cdot\textbf{n}=[Z^{J},\textbf{n}\cdot](u_{1}\partial_{y_{1}}u_{\varepsilon}+u_{2}\partial_{y_{2}}u_{\varepsilon})-Z^{J}\big((u\cdot\nabla\textbf{n})\cdot u_{\varepsilon}\big)\text{ on }\partial\Omega,

which together with the product estimate (
15.5
) and the trace inequality (
15.20
), gives rise to:

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{4}}|Z^{J}(u\cdot\nabla u_{\varepsilon})\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{4}}|u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u,u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

This, combined with the following estimate which can be derived again from the trace inequality (
15.20
)

\kappa^{\frac{3}{4}}|{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}\lesssim\|(\kappa\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon},\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

yields that,

(2.41)

{\mathcal{I}}_{3,2}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

We now proceed to deal with the term
{\mathcal{I}}_{3,3}
defined in (
2.39
).
It follows from the identity (
2.18
) that :

(2.42)

{\mathcal{I}}_{3,3}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|(\nabla u\cdot\nabla u_{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|u\cdot\nabla\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|[Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\big).

By counting the derivatives hitting on each term and using (
2.22
), one finds
that:

(2.43)

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla u\cdot\nabla u_{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}|\!|\!|\nabla u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}|\!|\!|\varepsilon\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The term
\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}u\cdot\nabla\nabla u_{\varepsilon}
is easier to control in the interior domain
\Omega_{0}
, where the conormal spaces are equivalent to the usual Sobolev norm, we thus
focus on the case near the boundary. Let
\chi_{i}
be the cut-off function associated to the chart
\Omega_{i}.

We have by using the local coordinates that

(2.44)

\chi_{i}u\cdot\nabla=\chi_{i}\big(u_{1}\partial_{y_{1}}+u_{2}\partial_{y_{2}}+\frac{u\cdot\textbf{N}}{\phi}\phi\partial_{z}\big).

Moreover, since
u\cdot\textbf{N}|_{\partial\Omega}=0,
it follows from the Hardy inequality and the fundamental theorem of calculus that

(2.45)

\displaystyle\|\chi_{i}\frac{u\cdot\textbf{N}}{\phi}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{l}}\lesssim\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{l}},\quad|\!|\!|\frac{u\cdot\textbf{N}}{\phi}|\!|\!|_{l,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|(u,\nabla u)|\!|\!|_{l,\infty,t}.

As a result, one obtains by noticing (
2.22
) that

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\chi_{i}u\cdot\nabla\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big(|\!|\!|(u,\nabla u)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\varepsilon\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\varepsilon\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}|\!|\!|(u,\nabla u)|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

To summarize, we have found that:

(2.46)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u\cdot\nabla\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Finally, as
[Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}=[Z^{J},u]\cdot\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}+u\cdot[Z^{J},\nabla]{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon},

it follows from the identity (
2.18
), the commutator estimate (
15.4
) and the fact (
2.22
) that:

(2.47)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|[Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}|\!|\!|\varepsilon\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla{\rm div\,}u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}]-1,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Plugging the estimates (
2.43
), (
2.46
), (
2.47
) into (
2.42
), we obtain that:

{\mathcal{I}}_{3,3}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2},

which, combined with (
2.41
) and (
2.40
), yields:

(2.48)

{\mathcal{I}}_{3}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

\underline{\text{Estimate of }{\mathcal{I}}_{4}}.

In view of the very definition in (
2.2
), we have by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the inequality (
2.11
) that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla\sigma,\mu{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u_{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)

(2.49)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

\underline{\text{Estimate of }{\mathcal{I}}_{5}}.
Thanks to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the commutator estimate (
15.3
), one has that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{5}

\displaystyle=\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\big([Z^{J},\beta]\nabla\sigma-\mu[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u_{\varepsilon}\big)\nabla\big(\frac{1}{\beta}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\big\|\big([Z^{J},\beta]\nabla\sigma,\mu[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u_{\varepsilon}\big)\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\|\nabla\big(\frac{1}{\beta}Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(Z\beta,Z(\Gamma\beta),\nabla\sigma,\mu{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u_{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\|\kappa(\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon},{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon},\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, it follows from the estimates (
15.8
), (
15.11
) that

\displaystyle\|(Z\beta,Z(\Gamma\beta))\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)|\!|\!|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The above two estimates, together with the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}
(see (
1.21
))
then lead to:

(2.50)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{5}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

\underline{\text{Estimate of }{\mathcal{I}}_{6}}.
In view of the identity (
2.18
), this one can be bounded as:

(2.51)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{6}\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

\underline{\text{Estimate of }{\mathcal{I}}_{7}}.
We split it into two terms:

{\mathcal{I}}_{7}={\mathcal{I}}_{7,1}+{\mathcal{I}}_{7,2}
where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{7,1}=\kappa\mu\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma[Z^{J},\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits]\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u_{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{7,2}=\kappa\mu\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(Z^{J}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u_{\varepsilon})\cdot\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

Using the identity (
2.18
), the fact (
2.11
),
one can estimate
{\mathcal{I}}_{7,1}
as:

(2.52)

{\mathcal{I}}_{7,1}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|(\varepsilon\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Moreover, integrating by parts in space, one controls
{\mathcal{I}}_{7,2}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{7,2}

\displaystyle=-\kappa\mu\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\big(Z^{J}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u_{\varepsilon}\times\textbf{n}\big)\cdot\Pi\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad+\kappa\mu\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{J}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u_{\varepsilon}\cdot(\nabla\Gamma\times\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}|Z^{J}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\Pi\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\big|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\varepsilon^{2}\|(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla({\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon},u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\nabla\sigma|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big).

As
\Pi\nabla
contains only the tangential derivatives, we have by the trace inequality (
15.21
) that:

(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\Pi\nabla Z^{J}{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon}\big|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}\lesssim(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\nabla{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon},{\rm div\,}u_{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}.

Furthermore, it follows from the identity (
2.15
), the identity

Z^{J}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u)\times\textbf{n}=Z^{J}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\textbf{n})+[Z^{J},\textbf{n}\times]\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u,

as well as the boundary conditions (
1.3
), (
1.16
) that

\displaystyle(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}|Z^{J}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}\lesssim(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(|u|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}+|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(|u|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}+|{\rm div\,}u|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}\big)\lesssim(\kappa\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big).

The previous two inequalities then yield
{\mathcal{I}}_{7,2}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2},
which, combined with (
2.52
), leads to that:

(2.53)

{\mathcal{I}}_{7}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

We now begin to control the terms
{\mathcal{I}}_{8}-{\mathcal{I}}_{12}
defined in
\eqref{sec2:eq11}.

\underline{\text{Estimate of }{\mathcal{I}}_{8}}.
Due to the Neumann boundary condition of
\theta,

this term vanishes if
Z^{J}
contains only the time derivatives, we thus can assume that
Z^{J}=\partial_{y^{i}}Z^{\tilde{J}},(i=1,2)
where
|\tilde{J}|\lesssim m-2.
Therefore, by integration by parts along the physical boundary
\partial\Omega,
we control
{\mathcal{I}}_{8}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{8}

\displaystyle=-\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}Z^{J}\big(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)[Z^{J},\textbf{n}\cdot]\nabla\theta\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}Z^{\tilde{J}}\big(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\partial_{y^{i}}\big([Z^{J},\textbf{n}\cdot]\nabla\theta\big)\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2}\big|\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\big|\nabla\theta\big|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}.

By the product estimate on the boundary (
15.5
), the assumption
m\geq 7,

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{5}{4}}|\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(|Z\Gamma|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-3}}+\kappa^{\frac{5}{4}}|{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\sigma,\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big|\big(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta,\kappa^{\frac{5}{4}}\nabla\theta,\kappa^{\frac{5}{4}}\Delta\theta\big)\big|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Thanks to the trace inequality (
15.20
):

\displaystyle|\varepsilon\sigma,\theta|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|(\nabla,\text{Id})(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{5}{4}}|\Delta\theta|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|(\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla\Delta\theta,\kappa\Delta\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{3}{4}}|\nabla\theta|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}\lesssim\|(\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Note that in light of the definition (
1.21
)
for
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}={\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u,\theta)

\|(\nabla,\text{Id})(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We thus obtain by collecting the previous three estimates that:

(2.54)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{8}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

\underline{\text{Estimate of }{\mathcal{I}}_{9}}.
Simply using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and identity (
2.18
), one can get that:

(2.55)

{\mathcal{I}}_{9}\lesssim\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

\underline{\text{Estimate of }{\mathcal{I}}_{10}}.

We estimate this term by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the commutator estimate (
15.3
) and the product estimate (
15.1
):

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{10}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\kappa{\rm div\,}(Z^{J}\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\big(\|\kappa[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|\kappa\Gamma\nabla\beta\cdot\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)

(2.56)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big(\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta,\theta,\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

\underline{\text{Estimate of }{\mathcal{I}}_{11}}.
It is direct to see that:

(2.57)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{11}\lesssim T\kappa\|(\nabla\varrho,\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|{\rm div\,}u|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim T\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

\underline{\text{Estimate of }{\mathcal{I}}_{12}}.
Let us bound this term as:

{\mathcal{I}}_{12}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(\varrho,\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathscr{C}_{\varrho}^{J},\mathscr{C}_{\theta}^{J})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}},

which, together with the following estimate that will be shown in the next lemma,

\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathscr{C}_{\varrho}^{J},\mathscr{C}_{\theta}^{J})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

leads to the estimate

(2.58)

{\mathcal{I}}_{12}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Finally, inserting the estimates (
2.36
)-(
2.38
), (
2.48
)-(
2.58
) into (
2.2
), we achieve the desired estimate (
2.27
).
∎

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
\mathscr{C}_{\varrho}^{J},\mathscr{C}_{\theta}^{J}
be defined in (
2.32
), we have,
under the same assumption as in Proposition
2.4
that, for any
|J|\leq m-1,
any
0<t\leq T,

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathscr{C}_{\varrho}^{J},\mathscr{C}_{\theta}^{J})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

The above estimate can be shown by applying the product and commutator estimates in Proposition
15.1
, let us only sketch the estimate of

\mathscr{C}_{\theta}^{J}=-\frac{C_{v}}{R}\big([\nabla,Z^{J}]\partial_{t}\theta+[\nabla Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]\theta\big).

It follows from the fact (
2.18
) and the equation
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{3}

that,

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|[\nabla,Z^{J}]\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla\partial_{t}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\big((u\cdot\nabla)\theta,{\rm div\,}u,\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, we can bound
[\nabla Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]\theta
as:

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|[\nabla Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(u\cdot\nabla)\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|[Z^{J},\nabla u]\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|[Z^{J},u\cdot]\nabla\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

∎

3.
Uniform estimates for the time derivatives

In this section, we prove the uniform bound for the (weighted) time derivatives to the order
m-1,
namely
\|(\sigma,u,\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}.
As the time derivatives commute with the singular terms in the system (
1.17
), this can be done by energy estimates. Nevertheless, due to the appearance of the term
\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\kappa\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)
in the equation
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{1},
the (linearized) penalized operator associated to the singular terms in the system (
1.17
)

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&{\rm div\,}&\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\kappa\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}(\beta(0)\nabla\cdot)\\
\nabla&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right)

is not skew-symmetric.
Moreover, the term
{\rm div\,}u
in the equation
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{3}
is troublesome due to the absence of the a-priori control of
\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}.

Therefore, the system
(
1.17
)
is not an appropriate target to perform energy estimates. Inspired by
[
2
]
, we introduce the unknown

(3.1)

w=u-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\overline{\Gamma}\kappa\beta(\theta)\nabla\theta

and consider the system satisfied by
(\sigma,w,\theta)
as follows:

(3.2)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{1}{\gamma}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\sigma+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}{\rm div\,}w=F_{\sigma},\\[5.0pt]
\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta)}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)w+\frac{\nabla\sigma}{\varepsilon}-\mu\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u+\frac{(\gamma-1)}{C_{v}\gamma}\overline{\Gamma}\kappa\nabla\big(-{\rm div\,}w+\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\\[5.0pt]
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=F_{w}-\varepsilon\frac{\kappa}{C_{v}}\overline{\Gamma}\nabla F_{\sigma},\\
\frac{C_{v}}{R}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\theta+{\rm div\,}w-\kappa\frac{\overline{\Gamma}}{\gamma}{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)=F_{\theta},\end{array}\right.

with

\displaystyle F_{\sigma}=\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\big(\frac{\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}}{\varepsilon}\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta(\theta)\nabla\theta)+\mu\varepsilon\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma){\mathcal{L}}u\cdot\mathbb{S}u\big),

(3.3)

\displaystyle F_{\theta}=\kappa(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}){\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)+\mu\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma){\mathcal{L}}u\cdot\mathbb{S}u,

\displaystyle F_{w}=\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\frac{\gamma-1}{R\gamma}\nabla u\cdot\nabla\theta-\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\frac{\gamma-1}{R\gamma}(\nabla\ln\beta)(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\theta+\varepsilon\mu\frac{\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}}{\varepsilon}{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u.

Proposition 3.1
.

Suppose that the assumption (
1.34
) holds and
m\geq 7,
then for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

any
0<t\leq T,
we have the following estimate:

\displaystyle\|(\sigma,w,\kappa\nabla\theta)\|^{2}_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}+\kappa\|{\rm div\,}w\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}

(3.4)

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Proof.

Applying
(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}
to the equation (
3.2
) and denoting

(\sigma^{j},w^{j},\theta^{j},u^{j})=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}(\sigma,w,\theta,u),
we find that

(3.5)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{1}{\gamma}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\sigma^{j}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}{\rm div\,}w^{j}=F_{\sigma}^{j},\\[5.0pt]
\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta)}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)w^{j}+\frac{\nabla\sigma^{j}}{\varepsilon}-\mu\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u^{j}+\frac{(\gamma-1)}{C_{v}\gamma}\overline{\Gamma}\kappa\nabla\big(-{\rm div\,}w^{j}+\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=F_{w}^{j}-\varepsilon\frac{\kappa}{C_{v}}\overline{\Gamma}\nabla(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}F_{\sigma},\\[5.0pt]
\frac{C_{v}}{R}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\theta^{j}+{\rm div\,}w^{j}-\kappa\frac{\overline{\Gamma}}{\gamma}{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta^{j})=F_{\theta}^{j},\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle F_{\sigma}^{j}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}F_{\sigma}+\frac{1}{\gamma}[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},u\cdot\nabla]\sigma,

(3.6)

\displaystyle F_{w}^{j}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}F_{w}-[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\frac{1}{R\beta}]\partial_{t}w-[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\frac{1}{R\beta}u\cdot\nabla]w,

\displaystyle F_{\theta}^{j}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}F_{\theta}-\frac{C_{v}}{R}[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},u\cdot\nabla]\theta+(\kappa\overline{\Gamma}/{\gamma}){\rm div\,}\big([(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\beta]\nabla\theta\big).

Multiplying the equation (
3.5
) by
\big(\sigma^{j},w^{j},-\kappa^{2}(\overline{\Gamma}/{\gamma})^{2}\frac{(\gamma-1)}{C_{v}}{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta^{j})\big)
and integrating in space and time, one gets by using the boundary condition
u^{j}\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta^{j}|_{\partial\Omega}=0
the following identity:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\big(\frac{1}{\gamma}|\sigma^{j}|^{2}+\frac{1}{R\beta}|w^{j}|^{2}+\kappa^{2}(\overline{\Gamma}/{\gamma})^{2}\frac{(\gamma-1)}{R}\beta|\nabla\theta^{j}|^{2}\big)(t)\mathrm{d}x

(3.7)

\displaystyle+\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}|\nabla u^{j}|^{2}+(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\overline{\Gamma}|{\rm div\,}u^{j}|^{2}+\frac{(\gamma-1)\overline{\Gamma}}{C_{v}\gamma}\kappa\big|{\rm div\,}w^{j}-(\overline{\Gamma}/{\gamma})\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta^{j})\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{6}{\mathcal{G}}_{k},

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\big(\frac{1}{\gamma}|\sigma^{j}|^{2}+\frac{1}{R\beta}|w^{j}|^{2}+\kappa^{2}(\overline{\Gamma}/{\gamma})^{2}\frac{(\gamma-1)}{R}\beta|\nabla\theta^{j}|^{2}\big)(0)\,\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{1}=\lambda_{1}\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{\textbf{n}}u^{j}\cdot w^{j}\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{\gamma}{\rm div\,}u|\sigma^{j}|^{2}+\frac{1}{R}\big(\partial_{t}(\frac{1}{\beta})+{\rm div\,}(\frac{1}{\beta}u)\big)|w^{j}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

(3.8)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{3}=\kappa^{2}(\overline{\Gamma}/{\gamma})^{2}\frac{\gamma-1}{R}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{t}\beta+{\rm div\,}(\beta u))|\nabla\theta^{j}|^{2}-\beta\nabla\theta^{j}\cdot(\nabla u\cdot\nabla\theta^{j})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{4}=\mu\kappa\frac{(\gamma-1)\overline{\Gamma}^{2}}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\lambda_{1}\nabla u^{j}\cdot\nabla(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}(\beta\nabla\theta)+(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}){\rm div\,}u^{j}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{5}=\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\frac{(\gamma-1)}{C_{v}\gamma^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\bigg(\overline{\Gamma}\kappa{\rm div\,}\big([(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\beta]\nabla\theta\big)+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\gamma-1}\varepsilon(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}F_{\sigma}\bigg){\rm div\,}w^{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{6}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\sigma^{j}F_{\sigma}^{j}+w^{j}\cdot F_{w}^{j}-\kappa^{2}(\overline{\Gamma}/{\gamma})^{2}\frac{(\gamma-1)}{C_{v}}{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta^{j})F_{\theta}^{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

Therefore, it is just a matter to control
{\mathcal{G}}_{0}-{\mathcal{G}}_{6}
defined above.
By the definition of
Y_{m}(0)
in (
1.25
):

(3.9)

{\mathcal{G}}_{1}\lesssim\|(\sigma,\kappa\nabla\theta,u)(0)\|_{H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0).

For the term
{\mathcal{G}}_{1},
we get with the help of the the boundary conditions
(
1.16
)
and

(w^{j}\cdot\textbf{n})|_{\partial\Omega}=0,

the trace inequality (
15.20
) that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{1}=\lambda_{1}\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Pi\partial_{\textbf{n}}u^{j}\cdot\Pi w^{j}\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\mu|(u^{j},w^{j})|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(T\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(u^{j},w^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(u^{j},w^{j},\nabla u^{j},\nabla w^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Next,
{\mathcal{G}}_{2}+{\mathcal{G}}_{3}
can be estimated directly as:

(3.10)

{\mathcal{G}}_{2}+{\mathcal{G}}_{3}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\|(\sigma^{j},w^{j},\kappa\nabla\theta^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim T\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Moreover, the term
{\mathcal{G}}_{4}
is bounded by using the identity (
15.7
), the estimates (
15.8
), (
15.11
) for
\beta,\Gamma,

and the Young’s inequality:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\|\kappa\nabla(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\bigg(\|\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\big(\|(\theta,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\bigg)

(3.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\|\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{1}\mu\|\nabla u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+C(\lambda_{1},c_{0})\mu\|\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

As for the term
{\mathcal{G}}_{5}
, one first derives from the commutator and product estimates
(
15.3
), (
15.1
) and Corollary
15.3
that:

\displaystyle\kappa\|{\rm div\,}\big([(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\beta]\nabla\theta\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\|\big([(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\beta]\Delta\theta,[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\nabla\beta\cdot]\nabla\theta\big)\|_{{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\kappa\|\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|(\theta,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|F_{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|\varepsilon^{-1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}),\mu\nabla u,\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The above two estimates, together with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, leads to

(3.12)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{5}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Finally, let us see the estimate of
{\mathcal{G}}_{6},
which is a bit lengthy since it contains many terms. We first use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to get:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}_{6}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|(\sigma^{j},w^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}})\|(F_{\sigma}^{j},F_{w}^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\|\kappa F_{\theta}^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}.

It can be verified that:

\|\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which, combined with the estimates of
\|(F_{\sigma}^{j},F_{w}^{j},F_{\theta}^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

in Lemma
3.2
,
enables us to find:

(3.13)

{\mathcal{G}}_{6}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Gluing (
3.9
)-(
3.13
) into (
3
), one finds that:

\displaystyle\|(\sigma,w,\kappa\nabla\theta)\|^{2}_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}+(\kappa+\mu)\|{\rm div\,}w\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}+\|\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2},

which, together with the estimate (
2.27
), gives rise to (
3.1
).
∎

Lemma 3.2

(Estimates on
(F_{\sigma}^{j},F_{w}^{j},F_{\theta}^{j})
)
.

Recall that
(F_{\sigma}^{j},F_{w}^{j},F_{\theta}^{j})
are defined in (
3
), the following estimates hold:

(3.14)

\displaystyle\|(F_{\sigma}^{j},F_{w}^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(3.15)

\displaystyle\|F_{\theta}^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

We will focus on the estimate of
F_{w}^{j},
since the other two can be controlled in a similar way and some of the terms appearing in
(F_{\sigma}^{j},F_{\theta}^{j})
have essentially been dealt with during the analysis of

{\mathcal{G}}_{4}-{\mathcal{G}}_{5}
in the above proposition. Let us begin with the term
(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}F_{w},

where

F_{w}=\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\frac{\gamma-1}{R\gamma}\nabla u\cdot\nabla\theta-\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\frac{\gamma-1}{R\gamma}(\nabla\ln\beta)(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\theta+\varepsilon\mu\frac{\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}}{\varepsilon}{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u.

By the product estimate (
15.1
), the estimates (
15.8
), (
15.11
) and the assumption
m\geq 7,

\displaystyle\kappa\|\nabla u\cdot\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla u,\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\nabla u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\|\varepsilon^{-1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}){\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\sigma,\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\sigma|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\kappa\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\kappa\|(\nabla\ln\beta)(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla u,\partial_{t}\theta,u\cdot\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\partial_{t}\theta,\nabla\theta,u)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\theta,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We get from the previous three estimates that, for any
j\leq m-1,

\|(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}F_{w}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

To finish the estimate of
\|F_{w}^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}},
it remains to control the
L_{t}^{2}L^{2}
norm of

[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\frac{1}{R\beta}u\cdot\nabla]w,\quad[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\frac{1}{R\beta}]\partial_{t}w.

Let us give the detail for the second one, the former one being easier. It follows from the formula

(3.16)

[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\frac{1}{R\beta}]\partial_{t}f=\sum_{l=0}^{j-1}C_{j}^{l+1}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{l}\partial_{t}(\frac{1}{R\beta})(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j-l}f

that:

\displaystyle\|[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\frac{1}{R\beta}]\partial_{t}w\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\theta,\partial_{t}\theta,\varepsilon\partial_{t}w)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2,0}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\partial_{t}\theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|w|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]+1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Note that since
m\geq 7,

\displaystyle|\!|\!|w|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]+1,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(u,\theta,\kappa\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

The estimate of
\|F_{w}^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}
is now complete.
∎

4.
Uniform estimates for
\theta

In this section, we aim to get various
uniform control of energy norms of
\theta
summarized in
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}(\theta):

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\kappa}\|\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}+\|\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co,\sqrt{\kappa}}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{\kappa}\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co,\sqrt{\kappa}}^{m-2}\cap L_{t}^{2}H_{co,\sqrt{\kappa}}^{m-1}}+\kappa\|\nabla^{3}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co,\sqrt{\kappa}}^{m-2}}.

Proposition 4.1
.

Suppose that the assumption (
1.34
) holds and
m\geq 7,
we have the following estimate for
\theta:
there exists a constant

\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}>0,
such that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

any
0<t\leq T:

(4.1)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}(\theta)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}.

Proof.

In view of estimate (
2.1
) it remains for us to control the following quantities:

\|\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}},\quad\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}},\quad\sqrt{\kappa}\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co,\sqrt{\kappa}}^{m-2}\cap L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}},\quad\kappa\|\nabla^{3}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co,\sqrt{\kappa}}^{m-2}},

which are done in the following three propositions.
∎

Proposition 4.2
.

Under the same assumption as in Proposition
4.1
, we have that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\|\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Proof.

We could have expected to get this inequality by performing direct energy estimates on
\theta.
However, the presence of the term

{\rm div\,}u
in the equation of
\theta
prevents us from doing so, since otherwise the estimate cannot be uniform in
\kappa.
The strategy is to introduce and work on the new unknown

\tilde{\theta}=\colon\frac{C_{v}}{R}\theta-\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}\sigma.

By the equation
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{1},\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{3},
we find that
\tilde{\theta}
solves the equation:

(4.3)

(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\tilde{\theta}-\kappa\gamma^{-1}\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)=\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}\mathfrak{N},

where
\mathfrak{N}
is defined in (
1.18
).
Applying
Z^{I},(|I|\leq m-1)
on the above equation and multiplying it by
Z^{I}\tilde{\theta},
one obtains the following estimate:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|Z^{I}\tilde{\theta}|^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{C_{v}\kappa}{R\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))Z^{I}{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(4.4)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|Z^{I}\tilde{\theta}|^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}{\rm div\,}u|Z^{I}\tilde{\theta}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\big([Z^{I},u\cdot\nabla]\tilde{\theta}+\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}Z^{I}\mathfrak{N}\big)Z^{I}\tilde{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{\varepsilon\kappa}{\gamma^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))Z^{I}\sigma\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Note that in the derivation of the last inequality, we have used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the facts:

\displaystyle\|Z^{I}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|Z^{I}\tilde{\theta}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\|\big([Z^{I},u\cdot\nabla]\tilde{\theta},Z^{I}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)),Z^{I}\sigma,Z^{I}\mathfrak{N}\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which result from the product and commutator estimates in Proposition
15.1
.
It suffices to see the second term in the left hand side of (
4
).
Let us write:

\displaystyle-\frac{C_{v}\kappa}{R\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))Z^{I}{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{v}\kappa}{R\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma\beta|Z^{I}\nabla\theta|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s-\frac{C_{v}\kappa}{R\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}Z^{I}(\Gamma\beta\nabla\theta)\cdot\textbf{n}Z^{I}\theta\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C_{v}\kappa}{R\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\big([Z^{I},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\theta\cdot\nabla Z^{I}\theta+\Gamma\beta Z^{I}(\nabla\theta)\cdot[\nabla,Z^{I}]\theta\big)-\big([Z^{I},{\rm div\,}](\Gamma\beta\nabla\theta)-Z^{I}(\beta\nabla\Gamma\cdot\nabla\theta)\big)Z^{I}\theta\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

One can verify that the last line in the above identity can be bounded as:

T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Such an estimate is achieved by the direct (although a bit lengthy) application of Proposition
15.1
and Corollary
15.3
, we thus omit the detail.
Let us now control the boundary term appearing in the above identity by taking the benefits of the Neumann boundary condition for
\theta
and the trace inequality (
15.20
):

\displaystyle\frac{C_{v}\kappa}{R\gamma}\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}Z^{I}(\Gamma\beta\nabla\theta)\cdot\textbf{n}Z^{I}\theta\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s\bigg|

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{v}\kappa}{R\gamma}\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}[Z^{I},\textbf{n}](\Gamma\beta\nabla\theta)Z^{I}\theta\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s\bigg|\lesssim|\kappa^{\frac{3}{4}}\Gamma\beta\nabla\theta|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}|\kappa^{\frac{1}{4}}\theta|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\big(\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta,({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\|(\theta,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim T\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

To summarize, we have found that:

\displaystyle-\frac{C_{v}\kappa}{R\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))Z^{I}{\theta}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\geq\frac{C_{v}\kappa}{R\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma\beta|Z^{I}\nabla\theta|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t-T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Substituting this estimate into (
4
), we obtain (
4.2
)
by using (
2.11
). ∎

Proposition 4.3
.

Under the same assumption as in Proposition
4.1
, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,
it holds that:

(4.5)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\kappa\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

Taking a vector field
Z^{J},|J|\leq m-2,
applying
Z^{J}\nabla
on the equation
(
4.3
), we get that:

(4.6)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\theta}-\kappa\gamma^{-1}\nabla Z^{J}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))=\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{1}+\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{2}+\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{3},

where

\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{1}=\kappa\gamma^{-1}[Z^{J},\nabla](\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)),\quad\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{2}=Z^{J}\big(\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}\mathfrak{N}-\nabla u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\theta}\big),\quad\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{3}=-[Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]\nabla\tilde{\theta}.

For the first term, we get in light of the identity (
2.18
) that:

\displaystyle\|\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{3}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\kappa\|\nabla(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}.

By using the equation (
4.3
), the product estimate (
15.1
) and the estimates (
15.11
), (
15.8
) for
\Gamma,\beta
. we control the right hand further as follows:

\displaystyle\kappa\|\nabla(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\|\nabla{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\kappa\big\|\nabla\big(\frac{\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}}{\varepsilon}\Gamma^{-1}(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)\tilde{\theta}\big)\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\|\beta\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Next, thanks to the product estimate (
15.2
),

\displaystyle\|\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\sigma,\nabla(\sigma,u,\tilde{\theta},\varepsilon\mu\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}|\!|\!|\sigma,\nabla(u,\tilde{\theta},\varepsilon\mu\nabla u)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, it results from the commutator estimate (
15.3
) and the identity (
2.44
) that:

\displaystyle\|\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{3}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u,\nabla u,\nabla\tilde{\theta})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big(|\!|\!|\nabla\tilde{\theta}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m+1}{2}],\infty,t}+\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}W_{co}^{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Note that by the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
) and the assumption
m\geq 7,

\displaystyle\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}W_{co}^{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty}}\lesssim\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

To summarize, we have obtained so far

(4.7)

\|(\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{1},\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{2},\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{3})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Now, we take the inner product of the equation (
4.6
) by
Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\theta}

and integrate in space and time to obtain that:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\theta}|^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x+\kappa\gamma^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma\beta|Z^{J}\Delta\theta|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\theta}|^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}x+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}{\rm div\,}u|Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\theta}|^{2}+Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\theta}\cdot\big(\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{1}+\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{2}+\eqref{sec4:eq2}_{3}\big)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s}_{\eqref{sec4:eq3}_{1}}

(4.8)

\displaystyle\quad\underbrace{-\kappa\gamma^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{J}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))[{\rm div\,},Z^{J}]\nabla\theta+\big(Z^{J}(\Gamma\nabla\beta\cdot\nabla\theta)+[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\Delta\theta\big)Z^{J}\Delta\theta\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s}_{\eqref{sec4:eq3}_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\underbrace{-\varepsilon\kappa\gamma^{-2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nabla Z^{J}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))\cdot Z^{J}\nabla\sigma\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s}_{\eqref{sec4:eq3}_{3}}.

Thanks to (
4.7
), the term
\eqref{sec4:eq3}_{1}
can be controlled by using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:

(4.9)

\eqref{sec4:eq3}_{1}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\theta}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Next, one can show by using repeatedly the identity (
2.18
), the Proposition
15.1
and Corollary
15.3
that for any
|J|\leq m-2,

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}[{\rm div\,},Z^{J}]\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}\lesssim\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}},

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}\lesssim(\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}})\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\Gamma\nabla\beta\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The term
\eqref{sec4:eq3}_{2}
can thus be controlled as:

\displaystyle|\eqref{sec4:eq3}_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\big([{\rm div\,},Z^{J}]\nabla\theta,[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\theta\big)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}

(4.10)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

It now remains to handle the term
\eqref{sec4:eq3}_{3},
which cannot be done directly by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, since we are not able to control

\varepsilon\kappa\|\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}
uniformly in
\kappa.
Therefore, integration by parts is needed. Before doing so,
we introduce
\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma{\rm div\,}u
and write:
