\displaystyle\eqref{sec4:eq3}_{3}=\varepsilon\kappa\gamma^{-2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nabla Z^{J}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))\cdot Z^{J}\nabla(\tilde{\sigma}+\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma{\rm div\,}u)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

By Remark
1.5
, for any
(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
it holds that
\mu\sim\kappa,
we thus can have that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\kappa\mu\gamma^{-2}(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nabla Z^{J}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))\cdot Z^{J}\nabla(\Gamma{\rm div\,}u)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(4.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\|\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Moreover, integration by parts in space gives rise to:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\kappa\gamma^{-2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nabla Z^{J}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))\cdot Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(4.12)

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\kappa\gamma^{-2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{J}\big(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\big(Z^{J}\Delta\tilde{\sigma}+[{\rm div\,},Z^{J}]\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\kappa\gamma^{-2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}Z^{J}\big(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\cdot\textbf{n}\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\colon\eqref{sec4:eq4}_{1}+\eqref{sec4:eq4}_{2}.

One can bound the term
\eqref{sec4:eq4}_{1}
by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:

(4.13)

\displaystyle\eqref{sec4:eq4}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big(\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\big),

which, combined with the estimate (
4.18
) for
\tilde{\sigma}
proved in the next lemma, yields:

(4.14)

\displaystyle\eqref{sec4:eq4}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

For the boundary term
\eqref{sec4:eq4}_{2},
we control it as:

(4.15)

\eqref{sec4:eq4}_{2}\lesssim\varepsilon|\kappa\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}(\partial\Omega)}|Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}.

Thanks to the trace inequality (
15.20
) and the estimates (
4.19
), (
4.20
), we can have that:

\displaystyle|Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}+|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-3}}

(4.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}+\|(\nabla\tilde{\sigma},\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, by the product estimate (
15.5
) on the boundary,

\displaystyle\kappa|\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big|\big(\sigma,\theta,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta,\kappa\Delta\theta\big)\big|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

It results from the trace inequality (
15.20
) that:

\big|\big(\sigma,\theta,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta,\kappa\Delta\theta\big)\big|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

For instance,

\displaystyle\big|\kappa\Delta\theta\big|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|(\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla\Delta\theta,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We thus obtain that:

\kappa|\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which, together with (
4.15
), (
4
), leads to that

\eqref{sec4:eq4}_{2}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Combined with (
4.14
), one derives:

\varepsilon\kappa\gamma^{-2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\nabla Z^{J}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))\cdot Z^{J}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

This inequality and (
4
) then enable us to conclude:

(4.17)

\eqref{sec4:eq3}_{3}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Inserting (
4.9
), (
4
), (
4.17
) into (
4
), we eventually arrive at (
4.5
).
∎

Remark 4.4
.

Following the similar arguments as in the proof of (
4.5
), we can show that:

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{1}}^{2}+\|(\varepsilon\kappa)^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{1}}^{2}\lesssim\|\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}\nabla\theta(0)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{1}}^{2}+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Such an estimate plays no role in the uniform estimates but will be useful in the proof of convergence result stated in Theorem
1.11
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma{\rm div\,}u.
Under the assumption (
1.34
), the following estimates hold:

(4.18)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}})\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(4.19)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(4.20)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(4.21)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|(\nabla\tilde{\sigma},(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

Taking the divergence of the equations
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{2}
for the velocity, we find that
\tilde{\sigma}
solves the elliptic problem

(4.22)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta\tilde{\sigma}={\rm div\,}f\text{ in }\Omega,\\[2.0pt]
\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}=f\cdot\textbf{n},\text{ on }\partial\Omega,\end{array}\right.

where

(4.23)

f=-\frac{1}{R\beta}(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)u-\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})({\rm div\,}u)\nabla\Gamma.

Applying the elliptic estimates (
15.24
), (
15.25
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}})\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|f\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|{\rm div\,}f\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}),

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|{\rm div\,}f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}.

The proof of (
4.18
), (
4.19
) is finished once we have controlled the right hand sides of the above two inequalities by
\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},
which are shown in the next lemma.

We now prove the estimate (
4.20
). The first quantity can be bounded by the trace inequality (
15.20
):

\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma},\nabla\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The second quantity can be controlled by using the explicit expression of
\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}
in
\eqref{eq-tiltasigma}_{2}.
Thanks to the boundary condition
u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=0
as well as the fact

u\cdot\nabla|_{\partial\Omega}=(u_{1}\partial_{y^{1}}+u_{2}\partial_{y^{2}})|_{\partial\Omega},
one derives that:

\displaystyle|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle=|f\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}

(4.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\big|\big((u\cdot\nabla)\textbf{n}\cdot u,\mu\partial_{\textbf{n}}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u,\mu\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}\big)\big|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}.

It follows from the product estimate (
15.5
), the trace inequalities (
15.20
),
(
15.19
) that:

\displaystyle|(u\cdot\nabla)\textbf{n}\cdot u|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\lesssim|u|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle|\mu\partial_{\textbf{n}}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|(\varepsilon^{-1}\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\Gamma,(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm div\,}u|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\sigma,\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\varepsilon\mu\nabla)(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

from which we see that:

|\big((u\cdot\nabla)\textbf{n}\cdot u,\mu\partial_{\textbf{n}}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u\big)|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

This, combined with the estimate (
4.29
) proved later in Lemma
4.7
, yields that:

|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We thus finish the proof of (
4.20
).

Finally, let us show the
L_{t,x}^{\infty}
estimate (
4.21
). First, by using the definition of

\tilde{\sigma},
we get that:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\!|\!|\nabla\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|({\rm div\,}u,\varepsilon\mu\nabla{\rm div\,}u,\varepsilon\sigma,\varepsilon\nabla\sigma)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

(4.25)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, in the local coordinate, there are some coefficients
a_{ij}
that depends smoothly on
\textbf{n},
such that (we use the convention
\partial_{y^{3}}=\partial_{\textbf{n}}
):

(4.26)

\Delta=\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}+\sum_{0\leq i,j\leq 3,(i,j)\neq(3,3).}\partial_{y^{i}}(a_{ij}\partial_{y^{j}}\cdot).

Consequently,

(4.27)

|\!|\!|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{k,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|\Delta\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{k,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{k+1,\infty,t},\,\forall k\geq 0.

However, in view of the equation
(
4.22
)
and
the definition (
4.23
), we find that

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\Delta\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}+(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\Delta\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}=|\!|\!|{\rm div\,}f|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}+(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|{\rm div\,}f|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\nabla(\sigma,u,\theta),\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}(\sigma,u)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with (
4
), (
4.27
), yields (
4.21
).
∎

In the following two lemmas, we give some complementary estimates on
f
and
(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n})|_{\partial\Omega},
which are used in the proof of Lemma
4.5
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Suppose that (
1.34
) is satisfied and let
f
be defined in (
4.23
). For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,
it holds that:

\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|{\rm div\,}f\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|f\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}})+\|{\rm div\,}f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

These estimates can be derived by using Proposition
15.1
, Corollary
15.3
as well as the definitions of
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}.

To prove this property,
it requires the estimates for four norms of four terms in the definition of
f.

We will only detail two of these sixteen estimates, the others can be treated similarly. Let us focus on the control of
\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}\varepsilon\partial_{t}u\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}},

\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}(\varepsilon\mu\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}.
It follows from the product estimate (
15.1
) and the estimate (
15.8
) for
\beta
that:

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\frac{1}{R\beta}\varepsilon\partial_{t}{\rm div\,}u,\varepsilon\partial_{t}u\cdot\nabla(\frac{1}{R\beta}\varepsilon\partial_{t}u))\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(u,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|(\theta,\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\nabla(u,\theta)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(u,\theta)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m+1}{2}],\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, using (
15.11
), one can find that:

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}(\varepsilon\mu\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}=\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\Gamma\cdot\varepsilon\mu\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon\|(\nabla\sigma,\varepsilon\mu\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\nabla\sigma,\varepsilon\mu\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

∎

Lemma 4.7
.

The following estimates hold:

(4.28)

\displaystyle|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

(4.29)

\displaystyle|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}+|(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(4.30)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}|(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-j-\frac{5}{2}}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\varepsilon{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

Denote
\omega=\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u.

We begin with the identity

(4.31)

-\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}={\rm div\,}(\omega\times\textbf{n})+\omega\cdot\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\textbf{n}.

Near the boundary, it follows from (
2.15
) that:

(4.32)

\displaystyle{\rm div\,}(\omega\times\textbf{n})

\displaystyle=\partial_{\textbf{n}}(\omega\times\textbf{n})\cdot\textbf{n}+(\Pi\partial_{y^{1}}(\omega\times\textbf{n}))^{1}+(\Pi\partial_{y^{2}}(\omega\times\textbf{n}))^{2}

\displaystyle=-(\omega\times\textbf{n})\cdot\partial_{\textbf{n}}\textbf{n}+(\Pi\partial_{y^{1}}(\omega\times\textbf{n}))^{1}+(\Pi\partial_{y^{2}}(\omega\times\textbf{n}))^{2}.

Therefore, by noticing the boundary conditions (
1.3
), (
1.16
) and the identity
(
2.15
), one finds after the use of the trace inequalities (
15.21
), (
15.19
) that:

\displaystyle|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim|({\rm div\,}u,\partial_{y}u,u)|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|({\rm div\,}u,\partial_{y}u,u)|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\lesssim(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}|({\rm div\,}u,\partial_{y}u,u)|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}

(4.33)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\|(\varepsilon\mu\nabla{\rm div\,}u,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|(\varepsilon\mu\nabla u,u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We thus finish the proof of (
4.29
). Moreover, using the fact

\varepsilon|u|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

one can show (
4.30
) by adapting the proof made in (
4.33
).
∎

Proposition 4.8
.

Under the same assumption as in Proposition
4.1
, we can find some
\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}>0
such that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

any
0<t\leq T:

(4.34)

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}+\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\|\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}

(4.35)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}.

Proof.

By the equation (
4.3
) satisfied by
\tilde{\theta}=\colon\frac{C_{v}}{R}\theta-\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}\sigma,
we can rewrite the equation for
\theta
as:

\frac{C_{v}}{R}\partial_{t}\theta-\kappa\gamma^{-1}\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)=-u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\theta}+\gamma^{-1}\varepsilon(\partial_{t}\sigma+\mathfrak{N}).

Taking a vector field
Z^{J},

|J|\leq m-2
and
applying
\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}Z^{J}\nabla
on the above equation,
we get that:

(4.36)

\frac{C_{v}}{R}\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}Z^{J}\nabla\theta-\gamma^{-1}\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}Z^{J}(\Gamma\beta\nabla\Delta\theta)=\eqref{rew-theta}_{1}+\eqref{rew-theta}_{2},

where

\displaystyle\eqref{rew-theta}_{1}=\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}Z^{J}\nabla\big(-u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\theta}+\gamma^{-1}\varepsilon(\partial_{t}\sigma+\mathfrak{N})\big),\quad\eqref{rew-theta}_{2}=\gamma^{-1}\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}Z^{J}\big(\nabla(\Gamma\nabla\beta\cdot\nabla\theta)+\nabla(\Gamma\beta)\Delta\theta\big).

Taking the inner product of the equation (
4.36
) with

-\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla{\rm div\,}(Z^{J}\nabla\theta),
we get after integration in space and time the following identity:

\displaystyle\frac{C_{v}}{2R}\kappa\int_{\Omega}|{\rm div\,}Z^{J}\nabla\theta|^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x+\gamma^{-1}\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Gamma\beta|Z^{J}\nabla\Delta\theta|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(4.37)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{v}}{2R}\kappa\int_{\Omega}|{\rm div\,}Z^{J}\nabla\theta|^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}x\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\kappa^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\eqref{rew-theta}_{1}+\eqref{rew-theta}_{2})\kappa\nabla{\rm div\,}(Z^{J}\nabla\theta)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s}_{\eqref{sec4:eq13}_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad-\underbrace{\gamma^{-1}\kappa^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\Delta\theta\cdot\nabla{\rm div\,}Z^{J}\nabla\theta+\Gamma\beta Z^{J}\nabla\Delta\theta[\nabla{\rm div\,},Z^{J}]\nabla\theta\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s}_{\eqref{sec4:eq13}_{2}}.

We first prove (
4.34
) and thus assume
|J|=k\leq m-3.
Let us begin with the the estimate of
\eqref{sec4:eq13}_{2}.

By using the identity (
2.18
) twice, one can get that:

\kappa\|[\nabla{\rm div\,},Z^{J}]\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\kappa\|\nabla^{3}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k-1}}\lesssim\kappa\|\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k-1}}

and thus

(4.38)

\kappa\|\nabla{\rm div\,}Z^{J}\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\kappa\|\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}.

Moreover, since
k\leq m-3,
we bound the commutator term
[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\Delta\theta
simply by

\displaystyle\kappa\|[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\!|\!|Z(\Gamma\beta)|\!|\!|_{k-1,\infty,t}\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

In view of the previous three estimates, we can now control
\eqref{sec4:eq13}_{2}
as:

\displaystyle\eqref{sec4:eq13}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\|\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}

(4.39)

\displaystyle\leq\delta\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}^{2}+C_{\delta}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k-1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Let us now see the term
\eqref{sec4:eq13}_{1}.
It can be verified without much trouble that for
|J|\leq m-3,

\displaystyle\kappa^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\eqref{rew-theta}_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\sigma,u,\nabla(\sigma,u,\theta,\varepsilon\mu\nabla u))\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\kappa^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\eqref{rew-theta}_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta,\nabla(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta),\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We thus derive from the above two estimates and (
4.38
) that:

\eqref{sec4:eq13}_{1}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which, together with (
4
), allow us to find, by choosing
\delta
small enough that for any
0\leq k\leq m-3,

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k}}^{2}+\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}

(4.40)

\displaystyle+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}^{2}\big)\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k-1}}^{2}+\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k-1}}^{2}.

Note that in the above, we have used the convention
\|\cdot\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{l}}=\|\cdot\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{l}}=0,
if
l=-1.

It thus then follows from induction that:

(4.41)

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}+\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}^{2}\big)Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Note that we denote
\Lambda
a polynomial that may differ from line to line.
By the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
) and the estimates (
4.2
), (
4.5
),

(4.42)

|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\|\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

plugging which into (
4.41
), we find a constant
\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}>0
such that
(
4.34
) holds.

We now comment on the proof of (
4.35
). Assume
|J|=m-2
in (
4
) and multiply it by
\kappa.
Following the same path as what we just did for
|J|\leq m-3,
we find the following estimate:

(4.43)

\displaystyle\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\|\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}+\|\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|(\eqref{rew-theta}_{1},\eqref{rew-theta}_{2})\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+\|\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}^{2}.

One can then verify that for
|J|=m-2,

\displaystyle\|\eqref{rew-theta}_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\big(\|\nabla(u,\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)+\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}+\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}},

\displaystyle\|\eqref{rew-theta}_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\|(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta,\nabla(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta),\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)|\!|\!|_{m-2,\infty,t}\big)\|\kappa\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, by the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
) and estimates (
2.1
), (
2.27
),

(4.44)

\displaystyle\kappa|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)|\!|\!|_{m-2,\infty,t}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\|(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}^{2}+\kappa\|\nabla(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Plugging the previous four estimates into (
4.43
), we obtain (
4.35
) by using (
4.34
) and by choosing
\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}
smaller if necessary.
∎

5.
Estimates for the second normal derivatives

In this section, we prove some (non-uniform) estimates for the second normal derivatives of the velocity
u

which are included in the energy norm
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,T}(\sigma,u)
defined in
(
1.22
).

Proposition 5.1
.

Under the assumption (
1.34
), there is a constant
\vartheta_{1}>0,
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

(5.1)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{2}\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, the following estimate also holds:

(5.2)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu\big(\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\big)+\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}\mu\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

By the virtue of the equivalence of the
conormal spaces and the usual Sobolev spaces in
\Omega_{0}
(interior of the domain), the identities
(
2.15
) and

\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}u)=\Pi(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\textbf{n})+\Pi\nabla(u\cdot\textbf{n})+\Pi(-(D\textbf{n})u)

as well as the established estimate (
2.1
), it suffices to prove the corresponding estimates on each chart
\Omega_{i}(i=1,\cdots N)
near the boundary for
\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times(\chi_{i}\textbf{n})
where
\chi_{i}
is the cut-off function associated to
\Omega_{i}.

Let us define

\omega_{\textbf{n}i}=\colon\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n}-2\Pi(\chi_{i}(-au+D\textbf{n}\cdot u)),

then again by (
2.1
), the matter is reduced to proving the following estimate: for any
i=1,\cdots N,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{2}\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}i}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}i}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu\big(\|\omega_{\textbf{n}i}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}i}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\big)+\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}\mu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}i}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}

(5.4)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

The reason to work on
\omega_{\textbf{n}i}
instead of
\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n}
is that by the boundary condition (
1.3
),
\omega_{\textbf{n}i}
vanishes on the boundary:

(5.5)

\omega_{\textbf{n}i}|_{\partial\Omega}=0,

which is more favorable for energy estimates.
By lengthy but direct computations, we find that
\omega_{\textbf{n}i}
is governed by the following transport-diffusion equation:

(5.6)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\omega_{\textbf{n}i}-\lambda_{1}R\mu(\beta\Gamma)\Delta\omega_{\textbf{n}i}=F_{i}^{\omega}+L_{i}

where
F_{i}^{\omega}
and
L
are the source terms respectively of the equations for
\omega\times(\chi_{i}\textbf{n}),(\omega=\colon\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u),
and

-2\Pi(\chi_{i}(-au+D\textbf{n}\cdot u)):

(5.7)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle F_{i}^{\omega}=F_{1,i}^{\omega}\times(\chi_{i}\textbf{n})+\omega\times(u\cdot\nabla(\chi_{i}\textbf{n}))-\lambda_{1}\mu R(\beta\Gamma)\big(\omega\times\Delta(\chi_{i}\textbf{n})+2\nabla\omega\times\nabla(\chi_{i}\textbf{n})\big),

\displaystyle L_{i}=2R\beta\Pi L_{1,i}+[\Pi,u\cdot\nabla]\big(2\chi_{i}(-au+D\textbf{n}\cdot u)\big)-\lambda_{1}\mu R(\beta\Gamma)[\Pi,\Delta]\big(2\chi_{i}(-au+D\textbf{n}\cdot u)\big),

with

\displaystyle F_{1,i}^{\omega}=\omega\cdot\nabla u-\omega{\rm div\,}u-\nabla(R\beta)\times\frac{\nabla\sigma}{\varepsilon}+\nabla(R\beta\Gamma)\times\mu{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u,

\displaystyle L_{1,i}=\frac{1}{R\beta}\big(-u\cdot\nabla(\chi_{i}D\textbf{n}\cdot)u+u\cdot\nabla(a\chi_{i})u\big)+\chi_{i}(D\textbf{n}\cdot{\nabla\sigma}+a\nabla\sigma)/\varepsilon

\displaystyle+\mu\lambda_{1}\Gamma\big(2\nabla(\chi_{i}D\textbf{n})\cdot\nabla u+\Delta(\chi_{i}D\textbf{n})\cdot u-2\nabla(a\chi_{i})\nabla u+\Delta(a\chi_{i})u\big)+\mu\Gamma\chi_{i}(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})(a-D\textbf{n}\cdot)\nabla{\rm div\,}u.

In view of the above
expression of
F_{i}^{\omega},L_{i},
the following fact can be verified by using the Proposition
15.1
and the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}:

Lemma 5.2
.

For any
1\leq i\leq N,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|F_{i}^{\omega}+L_{i}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\varepsilon\|F_{i}^{\omega}+L_{i}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We will skip the proof of the above lemma and proceed to
prove (
5.4
), (
5.3
). Performing standard energy estimates for the system (
5.6
) and (
5.5
), one gets that (we omit the subscript
i
for simplicity)

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\omega_{\textbf{n}}|^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon\mu R\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\beta\Gamma|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}{\rm div\,}u|\omega_{\textbf{n}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\omega_{\textbf{n}}|^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}(F^{\omega}+L)\cdot\omega_{\textbf{n}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s-\varepsilon\mu R\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}(\nabla(\Gamma\beta)\cdot\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}})\cdot\omega_{\textbf{n}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

Therefore, it follows from the estimate (
5.8
), Young’s inequality and the
fact
\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}
that:

(5.9)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Let us now focus on the higher regularity estimates.
For any vector field
Z^{J},|J|=k\leq m-1,
we have the following energy identity:

(5.10)

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{\Omega}|Z^{J}\omega_{\textbf{n}}|^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon\mu R\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\beta\Gamma|Z^{J}\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{\Omega}|Z^{J}\omega_{\textbf{n}}|^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}x+\varepsilon\sum_{l=1}^{4}\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{l},

where

(5.11)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|Z^{J}\omega_{\textbf{n}}|^{2}{\rm div\,}u\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,\,\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{2}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\big(Z^{J}(F^{\omega}+L)-[Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]\omega_{\textbf{n}}\big)\cdot Z^{J}\omega_{\textbf{n}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{3}

\displaystyle=-R\lambda_{1}\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{J}(\nabla(\Gamma\beta)\cdot\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}})\cdot Z^{J}\omega_{\textbf{n}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{4}

\displaystyle=-R\lambda_{1}\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\cdot\nabla Z^{J}\omega_{\textbf{n}}+\Gamma\beta Z^{J}\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\cdot[\nabla,Z^{J}]\omega_{\textbf{n}}

\displaystyle-[Z^{J},{\rm div\,}](\Gamma\beta\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}})\cdot Z^{J}\omega_{\textbf{n}}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

Let us first consider the case when
|J|=k\leq m-3.
The term
\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{1}
is controlled easily
as:

(5.12)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{1}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}|\!|\!|{\rm div\,}u|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Next, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{2}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}\|\varepsilon\big(Z^{J}(F^{\omega}+L),[Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]\omega_{\textbf{n}}\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}.

Thanks to the identity (
2.44
), the commutator estimate (
15.3
) and the estimate (
2.45
), we get
for any
k\leq m-2
that

\displaystyle\|[Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}+\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(u,\nabla u)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k}{2}],\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which, combined with the property (
5.8
),
yields that:

(5.13)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{2}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Moreover, applying the product estimate (
15.1
),
we can proceed to control
\varepsilon\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{3}
as:

(5.14)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}\big(\|\nabla(\Gamma\beta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|\varepsilon\mu\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Finally, it follows from the identity (
2.18
), the integration by parts and Young’s inequality that:

(5.15)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{4}\leq\delta\varepsilon\mu\|Z^{J}\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\big({\delta},\frac{1}{c_{0}}\big)\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k-1}}^{2}\big(1+|\!|\!|\Gamma\beta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}^{2}\big).

Inserting (
5.14
)-(
5.15
) into (
5.10
), we find by choosing
\delta
sufficiently small
that for any
0\leq k\leq m-3,

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k}}^{2}+\varepsilon\mu\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+\varepsilon\mu\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k-1}}^{2}\big(1+|\!|\!|\Gamma\beta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}^{2}\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, combined with (
5.9
) and the induction arguments, leads to that

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}+\varepsilon\mu\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, by using the estimate (
4.42
) and the fact
|\!|\!|\varepsilon\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),
we can find a constant
\vartheta_{1}>0,
such that:

(5.16)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}+\varepsilon\mu\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Let us now focus on the case
|J|=m-2.

Due to the difficulty arising from the control of
\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{4},
we need to multiply (
5.10
) by
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}
or
\mu
when
|J|=m-2.
The following paragraph is thus devoted to the control of
\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}\sum_{l=1}^{4}\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{l}
and
\varepsilon\mu\sum_{l=1}^{4}\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{l}
when
|J|=m-2.

Analogues to (
5.12
)-(
5.14
),
one can show that if
|J|=m-2,

(5.17)

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{1}+\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{2}+\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{3})\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

It remains to estimate
\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{4}
and
\varepsilon\mu\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{4}.
We have by the property (
2.18
), the integration by parts and the Young’s inequality that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\nu\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{4}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu\nu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big(\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|\Gamma\beta\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\big)

where
\nu
is a placeholder of
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}
or
\mu.
If
\nu=\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}},

the term
[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}},\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma\beta\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}

can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\big\|[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|\Gamma\beta\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{6}}\|(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}|\!|\!|\Gamma\beta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+T^{\frac{1}{6}}\|Z(\Gamma\beta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W_{co}^{m-3,6}}\|\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{3}L^{3}}.

By the usual Sobolev embedding and interpolation as well as the estimates (
15.11
) and (
15.8
) for
\Gamma
and
\beta,

\displaystyle\|Z(\Gamma\beta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W_{co}^{m-3,6}}\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\Gamma,\beta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\sigma,\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{3}L^{3}}\lesssim|\!|\!|\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}^{\frac{1}{3}}\|(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}^{\frac{2}{3}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, combined with the previous estimate, leads to that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\big\|[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|\Gamma\beta\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\big)\lesssim(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Consequently, we obtain that, for
|J|=m-2,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{4}\leq\delta\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}\mu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with (
5.17
), yields that

(5.18)

\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{\frac{4}{3}}\mu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, if
\nu=\mu,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\mu\|[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\mu\|\Gamma\beta\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\lesssim(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}|\!|\!|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma\beta|\!|\!|_{m-2,\infty,t},

and thus:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{4}\leq\delta\varepsilon\mu^{2}\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\|(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}|\!|\!|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma\beta|\!|\!|_{m-2,\infty,t},

which, combined with (
5.17
), yields that, by choosing
\delta
small enough,

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\mu^{2}\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)|\!|\!|_{m-2,\infty,t}\big).

Therefore, by noticing (
4.44
),
and (
5.16
), one obtains by choosing
\vartheta_{1}
smaller if necessary that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\mu^{2}\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

The desired estimate (
5.3
) is now complete in light of the above estimate and (
5.16
).

In view of (
5.18
), to finish the proof of (
5.4
), it remains to control

\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}),
we thus multiply the both sides of (
5.10
)
by
\varepsilon\mu
and control the terms
\varepsilon^{2}\mu\sum_{l=1}^{4}\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{l}.
Similar to (
5.12
)-(
5.13
), one can verify that,
when
|J|=m-1,

(5.19)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu(\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{1}+\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{2}+\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{3})\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

For instance, thanks to the estimate (
5.8
) and the identity (
2.44
),
we can bound
\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{2},\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{3}
in the following way:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(Z^{J}(F^{\omega}+L),[Z^{J},u\cdot\nabla]\omega_{\textbf{n}}\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(\Gamma\beta)\cdot\nabla(\varepsilon\mu\omega_{\textbf{n}})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, it results again from the property (
2.18
), the integration by parts and the Young’s inequality that,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{4}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\varepsilon\mu\big(\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|\Gamma\beta\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big).

We thus find by using the following estimate

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\big(\|[Z^{J},\Gamma\beta]\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|\Gamma\beta\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}|\!|\!|\Gamma\beta|\!|\!|_{2,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|Z(\Gamma\beta)|\!|\!|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}|\!|\!|\varepsilon\mu\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)

that

\varepsilon^{2}\mu\mathfrak{F}^{J}_{4}\lesssim(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

This, together with (
5.19
), leads to the estimate:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu\|\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\mu^{2}\|\nabla\omega_{\textbf{n}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

The proof of (
5.4
) is thus finished.
∎

Proposition 5.3
.

Under the assumption (
1.34
), it holds that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

(5.20)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|\mu\nabla(\varepsilon u,\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\big\|\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}\varepsilon\partial_{t}u,\nabla\sigma\big)\big\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

It stems from the equation
\eqref{newsys}_{1}
that:

(5.21)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, we use
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{2}
to write:

(5.22)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}\Gamma\Delta u=-\varepsilon\mu(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma\nabla{\rm div\,}u+\frac{\varepsilon}{R\beta}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)u+\nabla\sigma,

which, combined with the local expression of the Laplacian (
4.26
), leads to that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|\varepsilon\mu\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|(\varepsilon\mu\nabla{\rm div\,}u,\frac{1}{R\beta}\varepsilon\partial_{t}u,\nabla\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

The desired estimate (
5.20
) then follows from the above inequality and (
5.21
).
∎

Remark 5.4
.

It follows from the similar arguments as above that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla^{3}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

6.
Uniform (in Mach number) estimates for the compressible part-I

In this section, we aim to recover the higher order spatial derivatives for the compressible part
(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u),
more precisely, we will control
\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}},\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}.

Before stating and prove the main results in this section, we recall the definition of the Leray projection.

Definition of the Leary projection.

We define the projection operator

\mathbb{Q}
:

(6.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}:

\displaystyle L^{2}(\Omega)^{3}\rightarrow L^{2}(\Omega)^{3}

\displaystyle f\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}f=\nabla\Psi

where
\Psi
is defined as the unique
solution of

(6.2)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\Delta\Psi={\rm div\,}f\quad\text{in}\quad\Omega,\\
\displaystyle\partial_{\textbf{n}}\Psi=f\cdot{\textbf{n}}\quad\text{on}\quad\partial\Omega,\\
\int_{\Omega}\Psi\,\mathrm{d}x=0\text{ if }\Omega\text{ is bounded},\Psi(x)\xlongrightarrow{|x|\rightarrow\infty}0\text{ if }\Omega\text{ is an exterior domain.}\end{array}\right.

Note that the solvability of the Neumann problem (
6.2
) in
H^{1}(\Omega)
is well-known as an application of the Lax-Milgram theorem.
The Leary projection is then defined as:

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}={\rm Id}\,-\mathbb{Q}.

We are now ready to prove the following result:

Proposition 6.1
.

Under the assumptions (
1.34
), for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,
we have the following estimate:

(6.4)

\displaystyle\kappa\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

Let us first prove that

(6.5)

\kappa\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

However, as it has been shown in (
3.1
) that:

(6.6)

\kappa\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

we only need to control
\kappa\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}.
Applying
(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}\,(0\leq j\leq m-1)\,
on
(\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{1},\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{2})
and multiplying them by
\varepsilon\kappa\big(\frac{\gamma}{R\beta}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}{\rm div\,}u,-(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}\nabla\sigma\big),
one finds by denoting
f^{j}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}f
that:

\displaystyle\kappa\|(\nabla\sigma)^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{l=1}^{4}{\mathcal{B}}_{l}^{j},

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{1}^{j}=-\varepsilon\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{R\beta}(\nabla\sigma)^{j}\cdot u^{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\kappa\gamma\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{R\beta}|({\rm div\,}u)^{j}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{2}^{j}=-\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\varepsilon\partial_{t}\sigma^{j}\nabla(\frac{1}{R\beta})\cdot u^{j}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\varepsilon\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(\frac{1}{R\beta})(\nabla\sigma)^{j}\cdot u^{j}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{3}^{j}=\varepsilon\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{R\beta}\big(u\cdot\nabla\sigma-\varepsilon\mu(\gamma-1)\Gamma{\mathcal{L}}u\cdot\mathbb{S}u\big)^{j}({\rm div\,}u)^{j}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{4}^{j}=\varepsilon\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\big(\big(-\frac{u}{R\beta}\cdot\nabla u+\mu\Gamma{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u\big)^{j}-[\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\frac{1}{R\beta}]\partial_{t}u\big)\cdot(\nabla\sigma)^{j}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

In view of the estimate (
2.11
), the first term
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}^{j}
can be controlled directly as:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{1}^{j}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+\|(\varepsilon\kappa\nabla\sigma,u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}+\kappa\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}.

Next, thanks to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{2}^{j}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\varepsilon\partial_{t},\nabla)\sigma^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{3}^{j}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm div\,}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(u,\nabla\sigma,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Note that by using the equation
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{1},

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}\sigma^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\|(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm div\,}u,\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta))\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Finally, by the Young’s inequality, the term
{\mathcal{B}}_{4}^{j}
can be controlled as:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{4}^{j}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\sigma^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\|\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa}{2}\|\nabla\sigma^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}^{2}+C\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}}\big)\|\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Collecting the previous estimates for
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}^{j}-{\mathcal{B}}_{4}^{j},

we find that:

\displaystyle\kappa\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+\|(\varepsilon\kappa\nabla\sigma,u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}+\kappa\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

By using the estimates (
2.1
) for
\|\varepsilon\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}},
(
3.1
) for
\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}{}^{m-1,0}}
, (
6.6
) for
\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}},
(
5.2
) for
\|\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}},

we see that
\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}
can be controlled by the right hand side of (
6.5
).

We will show in below that for any
j,l
with
0\leq j+l\leq m-1,l\geq 1,
the following estimate holds:

(6.7)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\nabla{\sigma},{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\|(\nabla{\sigma},{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}

\displaystyle+\|(\kappa\Delta\theta,\varepsilon\mu\nabla{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

This estimate in hand, one can show
by induction that:

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|(\kappa\Delta\theta,\varepsilon\mu\nabla{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with (
6.5
), (
2.1
), (
5.2
), yields (
6.4
).

Let us now prove (
6.7
).
First, the equation
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{1}
can be rewritten as:

(6.8)

{\rm div\,}u=-\frac{1}{\gamma}(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)\sigma+\kappa\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)+\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\mathfrak{N},

from which we can deduce that:

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,0}}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(u\cdot\nabla\sigma,\mu\Gamma{\mathcal{L}}u\cdot\mathbb{S}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}.

Moreover, it follows from the product estimate (
15.1
) and the commutator estimate (
15.3
) that the
last line in the above inequality can be bounded by:

\displaystyle\kappa\|\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Consequently, we obtain that:

(6.9)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\kappa\|\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We now switch to control
\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}.
Denote
\mathbb{Q}(\frac{1}{R\beta}u)
and
\mathbb{P}(\frac{1}{R\beta}u)
as the compressible part and incompressible part of
\frac{1}{R\beta}u.
By the definition of
\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P}
in (
6.1
), (
6.3
) as well as the boundary condition
\frac{1}{R\beta}u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=0,

we have that

{\rm div\,}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)={\rm div\,}\mathbb{Q}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big),\quad\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=\mathbb{Q}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=0.

Therefore, the equation (
4.22
) for
\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}){\Gamma}{\rm div\,}u
can be rewritten as:

(6.10)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta\tilde{\sigma}={\rm div\,}\big(f+\varepsilon\partial_{t}\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)+\mu\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\big)\text{ in }\Omega,\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}=\big(f+\varepsilon\partial_{t}\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)\big)\cdot\textbf{n}\text{ on }\partial\Omega,\end{array}\right.

where
f
is defined in (
4.23
).
Thanks to the elliptic estimate (
15.24
):

(6.11)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f+\varepsilon\partial_{t}\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)+\mu\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}.

In view of (
4.23
), it holds that:

\displaystyle f+\varepsilon\partial_{t}\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)+\mu\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}\mathbb{Q}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)+\varepsilon\partial_{t}(\frac{1}{R\beta})u-\frac{\varepsilon}{R\beta}u\cdot\nabla u-\mu\varepsilon\lambda_{1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})({\rm div\,}u)\nabla\Gamma,

from which we can conclude that:

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f+\varepsilon\partial_{t}\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)+\mu\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbb{Q}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

However, by the elliptic estimates (
15.24
), (
15.26
):

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbb{Q}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbb{Q}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1}}+\|\nabla\mathbb{Q}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{1}{R\beta}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1}}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}(\frac{1}{R\beta}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{1}{R\beta}{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u,\nabla(\frac{1}{R\beta})\cdot u\big)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}.

With the help of product estimates (
15.7
) and (
15.1
), one can conclude that:

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{1}{R\beta}{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}})\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u,\nabla(\frac{1}{R\beta})\cdot u\big)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\theta,u,\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Consequently, it is found that:

\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f+\varepsilon\partial_{t}\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{1}{R\beta}u\big)-\mu\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which, together with (
4.29
), (
6.11
), yields:

(6.12)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We can then obtain (
6.7
) from the estimates (
6.9
) and (
6.12
),
and thus finish the proof.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

By (
6.4
) and (
4.1
), it holds that by choosing
\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}
(found in Proposition
4.1
) smaller if necessary,

(6.13)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{m,T}^{2}(\theta)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Remark 6.3
.

One can find some
\vartheta_{2}>0
such that:

(6.14)

\displaystyle\|\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Such a fact can be shown in the following way.
First, by using the product estimate (
15.1
) and the estimate (
15.8
) for
\nabla\beta,

\displaystyle\kappa\|\nabla\beta\cdot\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|(\theta,\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big).

By the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
),

(6.15)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}\lesssim\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\kappa\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}.

As
m\geq 7,
it holds that
[\frac{m}{2}]\leq m-4,
we thus conclude from the above estimate and (
4.42
) that:

(6.16)

\kappa\|\nabla\beta\cdot\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\|\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|(\nabla\theta,\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big).

Next, we control
\kappa\beta\Delta\theta
as follows:

\displaystyle\kappa\|\beta\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}|\!|\!|\beta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}Z\beta\|_{m-3,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}})\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\theta\|_{m-2,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big).

It follows again from the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
) that:

\displaystyle\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\theta\|_{m-2,\infty,t}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}),

which, combined with (
4.42
), yields:

(6.17)

\kappa\|\beta\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}\big)+\|\kappa\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big).

In view of estimates (
6.16
) and (
6.17
),
we have found that:

\displaystyle\|\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}(\theta)\big),

which, combined with (
6.13
), allows us to
find some
\vartheta_{2}>0,
such that (
6.14
) holds.

The next proposition is devoted to the control of the second normal derivatives for
\sigma,\mathbb{Q}u:

Proposition 6.4
.

Under the assumption (
1.34
), the following estimate holds: for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

(6.18)

\displaystyle\kappa\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

(6.19)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu\kappa\|\nabla^{2}\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\kappa\|\nabla^{2}\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

Let us first control
\kappa\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}.
It follows from (
6.8
) that:

\displaystyle\kappa\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\|\nabla\big(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)\sigma,\kappa\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta),\mathfrak{N}\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\|\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+(T+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, combined with (
4.35
), (
6.4
), leads to (
6.18
).

We now switch to the proof of (
6.19
).
Thanks to the estimates (
2.27
), (
6.4
) and the fact (
4.26
), it suffices for us to
control
\varepsilon^{2}\mu\kappa\|\Delta\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\kappa\|\Delta\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}
by the right hand side of (
6.19
). Let us begin with the derivation of the equation satisfied by
\Delta\sigma.
Applying
\varepsilon\mu\gamma(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma\nabla
on the equation
\eqref{newsys}_{1}
solved by
\varrho=\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}\sigma-\frac{C_{v}}{R}\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\theta
, we find that
\nabla\sigma
is governed by:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma\bigg((\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)\nabla\sigma+\nabla{\rm div\,}u\bigg)=\mathfrak{H}_{1}

with

(6.20)

\mathfrak{H}_{1}=\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma\bigg(\frac{C_{v}(\gamma-1)}{R}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\nabla\theta-\gamma\nabla u\cdot\nabla\varrho\bigg).

By using
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{2},
we can
substitute the quantity
\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma\nabla{\rm div\,}u
in the above equation by

\frac{1}{R\beta}(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)u-\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\mu\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u+\nabla\sigma

to find that:

(6.21)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\nabla\sigma+\nabla\sigma=\mathfrak{H}_{2},

where

(6.22)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}_{2}=-\frac{1}{R\beta}(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)u-\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\mu\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u+\mathfrak{H}_{1}.

Taking the divergence of the equation (
6.21
), one derives the equation satisfied by
\Delta\sigma:

(6.23)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\Delta\sigma+\Delta\sigma={\rm div\,}\mathfrak{H}_{2}-\varepsilon^{2}\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\big(\nabla\Gamma\cdot\partial_{t}\nabla\sigma+\nabla(\Gamma u)\cdot\nabla\sigma\big).

It then follows from the direct energy estimates on (
6.23
) and the Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu\kappa\|\Delta\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\kappa\|\Delta\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+\varepsilon^{2}\mu\kappa|\!|\!|{\rm div\,}u|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\|\Delta\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\|\text{ R.H.S of }\eqref{eqDeltasigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2},

which, combined with the estimates (
6.24
), (
6.25
) in Lemma
6.5
, yields that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\mu\kappa\|\Delta\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+\kappa\|\Delta\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}+\kappa\|({\rm div\,}u,\mu\Delta\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}.

We thus finish the proof of (
6.19
)
by noticing (
6.4
) and (
2.27
).
∎

We list in the next lemma some estimates used in the previous proposition for the right hand side of (
6.23
) .

Lemma 6.5
.

The following estimates hold true:

(6.24)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\|\big(\nabla\Gamma\cdot\partial_{t}\nabla\sigma+\nabla(\Gamma u)\cdot\nabla\sigma\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}\cap L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(6.25)

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm div\,}\mathfrak{H}_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|({\rm div\,}u,\mu\Delta\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(6.26)

\displaystyle\|{\rm div\,}\mathfrak{H}_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}\lesssim\|({\rm div\,}u,\mu\Delta\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

These estimates stem from the very expression of each target term, the product estimates in the Proposition

15.1
as well as the definitions of
{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t},{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},
we thus skip the details.

Remark 6.6
.

By the equation (
6.23
), the estimates (
6.19
), (
6.24
), (
6.26
) and the identity (
4.26
), we have also that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

(6.27)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u,\mu\Delta\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

7.
Uniform estimates for the incompressible part

In this section, we introduce the incompressible part for the modified velocity–
r_{0}u,
where

(7.1)

r_{0}=\frac{1}{R\beta}\exp(\varepsilon R\tilde{\sigma}/C_{v}\gamma)=\frac{1}{R\beta(0)}\exp(-R\tilde{\theta}/{C_{v}})\exp(\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})R\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u/C_{v}\gamma),

with

\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma{\rm div\,}u,\quad\tilde{\theta}=\frac{C_{v}}{R}\theta-\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}\sigma.

Denote
r_{1}=\varepsilon^{-1}(\exp(\varepsilon R\tilde{\sigma}/C_{v}\gamma)-1).

The system
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{2}
can be rewritten as:

(7.2)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)(r_{0}u)+\frac{\nabla\tilde{\sigma}}{\varepsilon}+\mu\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u

\displaystyle=u(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}-r_{1}(\nabla\tilde{\sigma}+\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u)-\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})(1+\varepsilon r_{1}){\rm div\,}u\nabla\Gamma.

By writing further:

\mu\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u=\mu\lambda_{1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u),

the system (
7.2
) can thus be reformulated as:

(7.3)

(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)(r_{0}u)+\frac{\nabla\tilde{\sigma}}{\varepsilon}+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)=-r_{1}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}+G,

where

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle=u(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u

(7.4)

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}r_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u-\mu\lambda_{1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u-\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})(1+\varepsilon r_{1}){\rm div\,}u\nabla\Gamma.

Let us now derive the equations for the incompressible part
v=\mathbb{P}(r_{0}u)
of the quantity
r_{0}u.

Taking the Leray projection
\mathbb{P}
(defined in (
6.3
)) on the equations (
7.3
) and
noticing that
r_{1}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}
can be written as a gradient

r_{1}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}=\varepsilon^{-1}\nabla\bigg({C_{v}\gamma}\big(\exp(\varepsilon R\tilde{\sigma}/C_{v}\gamma)-1\big)/{\varepsilon R}-\tilde{\sigma}\bigg)

and also the identity
\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)=-\Delta v,

we find that
v
solves the equation:

(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)v-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathbb{P}(r_{0}^{-1}\Delta v)=-[\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)+\mathbb{P}G.

Let us define

\nabla q=\mathbb{Q}(G+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\Delta v),
the above equation can thus be rewritten as:

(7.5)

(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)v-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\Delta v+\nabla q=-[\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)+G.

Moreover, by the boundary condition (
1.16
), the definition
(
7.1
),
we find that,

(7.6)

v\cdot\textbf{n}=0\text{ on }\partial\Omega,

\displaystyle\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}v)=

\displaystyle\Pi\partial_{\textbf{n}}(r_{0}u)-\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}\mathbb{Q}(r_{0}u))=\partial_{\textbf{n}}r_{0}\Pi u+r_{0}\Pi\partial_{\textbf{n}}u+\Pi((D\textbf{n})\mathbb{Q}(r_{0}u))

(7.7)

\displaystyle=\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}r_{0}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}\Pi u+r_{0}\Pi\big(-2au+(D\textbf{n})u\big)+\Pi\big((D\textbf{n})\mathbb{Q}(r_{0}u)\big)\text{ on }\partial\Omega.

In the next several lemmas, we give some preliminary estimates for
r_{0},r_{1},

G,\nabla q
and
[\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u),
when
m\geq 7,

\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
which will be quite useful for the estimate of
v.

Let us begin with the estimates for
r_{0}:

Lemma 7.1
.

Suppose that the assumption (
1.34
) holds for some
T>0,
then
for any
0<t\leq T,
we have the following estimates:

(7.8)

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t},\nabla)r_{0})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(7.9)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(r_{0},r_{0}^{-1})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

Since
(\partial_{t},\nabla)r_{0}=-r_{0}(\partial_{t},\nabla)(\theta-\varepsilon RC_{v}\tilde{\sigma}/\gamma),
we can apply the commutator estimate (
15.4
) and the estimate (
15.12
) to obtain that, by denoting

\Theta=(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma,\varepsilon^{2}\mu\Gamma{\rm div\,}u),

\displaystyle\|(\partial_{t},\nabla)r_{0})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\partial_{t},\nabla)\Theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\partial_{t},\nabla)\Theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We thus finish the proof of (
7.8
).
To prove (
7.9
), we use commutator estimate (
15.3
) and the estimate (
15.12
) to get:

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(r_{0},r_{0}^{-1})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)\Theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\nabla\Theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\Theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\theta,\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\varepsilon\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\sigma,\varepsilon\mu\Gamma{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

∎

In the following lemma, we present some estimates for the source term
G:

Lemma 7.2
.

Let

(7.10)

G_{1}=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(R\beta r_{0})^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,R\beta r_{0}]u,

Under the assumption (
1.34
),
the following estimates hold: for any
0<t\leq T,

(7.11)

\displaystyle\|G_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(7.12)

\displaystyle\quad\|G-G_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Remark 7.3
.

We can only control the derivatives of
(R\beta r_{0})^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,R\beta r_{0}]u
to the order
m-2,
as it may involve two derivatives of
\tilde{\sigma}.

But due to the smoothing effect,
\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta
can be bounded in the regularity norm
L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}.

Proof.

The above estimates rely on the use the product and commutator estimates in Proposition
15.1
, the estimates for
\Gamma
in Corollary
15.3

as well as the facts:
