\|\nabla(u,\sigma,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\|(u,\theta,\tilde{\sigma},\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla u,\nabla\theta,\nabla\tilde{\sigma}),\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u,\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Let us first prove (
7.11
).
By the definition (
7.1
) for
r_{0},

R\beta r_{0}=\exp(\varepsilon R\tilde{\sigma}/{C_{v}\gamma})=1+\varepsilon r_{1}.

Moreover, direct calculation yields:

(7.13)

\displaystyle[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,f]u=\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(\nabla f\times u)+\nabla f\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u.

We can use successively the product estimate (
15.1
),
the estimate (
15.14
) for
r_{1},
the estimate (
4.18
) for
\tilde{\sigma}

to obtain that:

\displaystyle\|\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(R\beta r_{0})^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,R\beta r_{0}]u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(u,\tilde{\sigma},\nabla(u,\tilde{\sigma},\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We now show the proof of (
7.12
). First, as

\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u-(R\beta r_{0})^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,R\beta r_{0}]u\big)=-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(R\beta r_{0})^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,R\beta](r_{0}u),

we can control this term by using the identity (
7.13
), the product estimate (
15.1
), the estimate (
15.8
), (
15.9
) for
\beta

the estimate (
15.12
) for
r_{0}
that:

\displaystyle\mu\big\|(R\beta r_{0})^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,R\beta](r_{0}u)\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

(7.14)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u,\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma},\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla(u,\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma}),\mu\nabla^{2}\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

To finish the proof of (
7.12
), it still remains for us to control several terms including

u(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0},\quad-(\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}r_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u+\mu\lambda_{1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u),\quad-\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})(1+\varepsilon r_{1}){\rm div\,}u\nabla\Gamma.

Let us begin with the estimate of the first term. It follows from the definition (
7.1
) for
r_{0}
and the equation (
4.3
) for
\tilde{\theta}
that:

(7.15)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{R}{C_{v}}r_{0}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)(\tilde{\theta}-\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u/\gamma)

\displaystyle=-\frac{Rr_{0}}{C_{v}\gamma}\big(\kappa\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)+\varepsilon\mathfrak{N}\big)-\frac{R}{C_{v}\gamma}(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})r_{0}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)(\mu\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u).

We can thus derive from the product estimate (
15.1
) and the estimate (
15.12
) for
r_{0}

that:

\displaystyle\|u(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u,\theta,\varepsilon\sigma,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta,\mu\varepsilon(\varepsilon\partial_{t},\nabla){\rm div\,}u,\mu\varepsilon\nabla u,\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

(7.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Next, using the product estimate (
15.1
) and the estimate (
15.14
) for
r_{1},

the last two terms can be estimated in a similar way:

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}r_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u+\mu\lambda_{1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

(7.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\sigma,\tilde{\sigma},\varepsilon\mu\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\|\mu(1+\varepsilon r_{1}){\rm div\,}u\nabla\Gamma\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\sigma,{\rm div\,}u,\nabla\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

(7.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We thus finish the proof of (
7.12
) by collecting (
7
)-(
7
).
∎

In the next lemma, we give the estimate for
\nabla q:

Lemma 7.4
.

There exist
\nabla q_{1},\nabla q_{2}\in L_{t}^{2}L^{2},
such that

\nabla q=\nabla q_{1}+\nabla q_{2}
and

(7.19)

\displaystyle\|\nabla q_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},\quad\|\nabla q_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

By the definition:
\nabla q=\mathbb{Q}(G+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\Delta v)
and the identity

r_{0}^{-1}\Delta v=-r_{0}^{-1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)=-\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}^{-1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u))+\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u),

we find that
\nabla q
solves the following elliptic equation (assume might as well that
\Omega
is bounded)

(7.20)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\Delta q={\rm div\,}\big(G+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)\big)\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{ in }\Omega,\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{\textbf{n}}q=\big(G+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)\big)\cdot\textbf{n}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}^{-1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u))\cdot\textbf{n}\text{ on }\partial\Omega,\\[4.0pt]
\int_{\Omega}q\,\mathrm{d}x=0.\end{array}\right.

Note that when
\Omega
is an exterior domain, the zero mean condition should be replaced as
q(\cdot,x)\rightarrow 0
as
|x|\rightarrow+\infty.

The same changes should be made in the following.
We define
\nabla q_{1},\nabla q_{2}
as the solutions to the following elliptic equations:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\Delta q={\rm div\,}G_{1}\qquad\quad\text{ in }\Omega,\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{\textbf{n}}q_{2}=G_{1}\cdot\textbf{n}-g\quad\text{ on }\partial\Omega,\\[4.0pt]
\int_{\Omega}q_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x=0,\end{array}\right.\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\Delta q_{2}={\rm div\,}G_{2}\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{ in }\Omega,\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{\textbf{n}}q_{2}=G_{2}\cdot\textbf{n}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}^{-1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u))\cdot\textbf{n}+g\text{ on }\partial\Omega,\\[4.0pt]
\int_{\Omega}q_{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=0.\end{array}\right.

where
G_{1}
is defined in (
7.10
) and

G_{2}=G-G_{1}+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u),

(7.21)

g=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big((\Pi\partial_{y^{1}}(r_{0}^{-1}(\nabla r_{0}\times u)\times\textbf{n}))^{1}+(\Pi\partial_{y^{2}}(r_{0}^{-1}(\nabla r_{0}\times u)\times\textbf{n}))^{2}\big).

It follows from the elliptic estimate (
15.24
), the estimates (
7.11
) for
G_{1},
(
7.12
) for
G-G_{1},
as well as estimates (
7.22
), (
7.23
) for the boundary term that:

\displaystyle\|\nabla q_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|G_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}|(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}g|_{L_{t}^{2}{H}^{m-j-\frac{5}{2}}}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\|\nabla q_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|(G-G_{1},\mu\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}+\mu|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}^{-1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u))\cdot\textbf{n}-g|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

∎

Lemma 7.5
.

Similar to (
4.29
), it can be verified that:

(7.22)

\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}|(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}g|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-j-\frac{5}{2}}}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(7.23)

\mu|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}^{-1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u))\cdot\textbf{n}-g|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

Taking the benefits of the boundary conditions
u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta|_{\partial\Omega}=0
, we find that:

(7.24)

\displaystyle r_{0}^{-1}(\nabla r_{0}\times u)\times\textbf{n}=r_{0}^{-1}u\partial_{\textbf{n}}r_{0}=\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}u\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}\,\text{ on }\partial\Omega.

Therefore, by the product estimate (
15.5
), the trace inequality
(
15.20
) and the estimate (
4.18
):

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}|(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}g|_{L_{t}^{2}{H}^{m-j-\frac{5}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\mu|(u,\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma})|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(u,\nabla(u,\tilde{\sigma},\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}))\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We now proceed to prove (
7.23
). Let us write

\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}^{-1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u))\cdot\textbf{n}-g=\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}+\big(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla r_{0}\times u)\cdot\textbf{n}-g\big).

The first term
\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}
has been controlled in (
4.29
),
we thus focus on the second one. By using the identity

(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits a)\cdot b={\rm div\,}(a\times b)+a\cdot\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits b,

one finds that:

\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla r_{0}\times u)\cdot\textbf{n}={\rm div\,}(r_{0}^{-1}(\nabla r_{0}\times u)\times\textbf{n})+r_{0}^{-1}(\nabla r_{0}\times u)\cdot\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\textbf{n}.

It follows from (
2.15
) that:

\displaystyle{\rm div\,}(r_{0}^{-1}(\nabla r_{0}\times u)\times\textbf{n})

\displaystyle=(r_{0}^{-1}(\nabla r_{0}\times u)\times\partial_{\textbf{n}}\textbf{n})\cdot\textbf{n}+g.

The above two equalities, together with the trace inequality (
15.20
) and estimates (
15.12
), (
15.13
) enable us to find that:

\displaystyle\mu|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla r_{0}\times u)\cdot\textbf{n}-g|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim\mu|r_{0}^{-1}\nabla r_{0}\times u|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\text{Id},\nabla)\big(u,({\rm Id}\,,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

∎

In the next lemma, we present the estimate of
[\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u),
which is needed later for the estimate of
v.

Lemma 7.6
.

It holds that,
for any
0<t\leq T,

(7.25)

\displaystyle\big\|[\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

First, it follows from the product estimate (
15.1
), the elliptic estimate (
15.24
) that:

\displaystyle\big\|[\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\cdot\nabla(r_{0}u,v)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}

(7.26)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\text{Id},\nabla)(u,\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Next, one gets from the fact
{\rm div\,}\mathbb{P}=0
that:

(7.27)

\displaystyle\big\|{\rm div\,}\big([\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)\big)\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}=\|\nabla u\cdot\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

By writing
[\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)=u\cdot\nabla\mathbb{Q}(r_{0}u)-\mathbb{Q}(u\cdot\nabla(r_{0}u))
and
using the fact
\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\nabla=\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathbb{Q}=0,
we have that:

(7.28)

\displaystyle\big\|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\big([\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)\big)\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}=\|\nabla u\times\nabla\mathbb{Q}(r_{0}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, it can be derived from

(u\cdot\nabla)|_{\partial\Omega}=(u_{y^{1}}\partial_{y^{1}}+u_{y^{2}}\partial_{y^{2}})|_{\partial\Omega},\quad(v\cdot\textbf{n})|_{\partial\Omega}=0,

that

[\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=-(u\cdot\nabla)v\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=(u\cdot\nabla)\textbf{n}\cdot v|_{\partial\Omega},

which gives rise to

|[\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

This, together with (
7.27
) and (
7.28
), yields that:

(7.29)

\big\|\nabla\big([\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)\big)\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

In light of (
7
) and (
7.29
),
we finish the proof of (
7.25
).
∎

The above lemmas in hand, we are now able to prove the uniform estimates for
v=\mathbb{P}(r_{0}u):

Proposition 7.7
.

Assume that assumption (
1.34
) holds and

m\geq 7,
then for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
for any
0<t\leq T,
we have that

(7.30)

\displaystyle\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

We first show that:

(7.31)

\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Indeed, for any
j\leq m-1,
it follows from (
7.8
), (
7.9
) that

\displaystyle\big\|[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},r_{0}]u\big\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\partial_{t}r_{0},u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2,0}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\big\|[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},r_{0}]\nabla u\big\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\partial_{t}r_{0},\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2,0}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla r_{0}\otimes u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\nabla r_{0},u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Consequently, we obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla(r_{0}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}^{2}+(T+\varepsilon^{2})\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2},

which, combined with estimate (
3.1
),
elliptic estimates (
15.25
), (
15.24
) and also the definition
v=r_{0}u-\mathbb{Q}(r_{0}u),
yields (
7.31
).

We now focus on the control of
\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}
and
\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}.
Let
Z^{I}
be a vector field with
|I|\leq m-1,I_{0}\leq m-2.
Applying
Z^{I}
on the equation
(
7.5
), we find that:

(7.32)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)Z^{I}v+Z^{I}\nabla q-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}Z^{I}(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v)=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}[Z^{I},{\rm div\,}](r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v)+H^{I}

where

H^{I}=-[Z^{I},u\cdot\nabla]v-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}Z^{I}(\nabla r_{0}^{-1}\cdot\nabla v)+Z^{I}(G-[\mathbb{P},u\cdot\nabla](r_{0}u)).

Taking the vector product of the resulting equation with
Z^{I}v,
we find that:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|Z^{I}v|^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}r_{0}^{-1}|Z^{I}\nabla v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|Z^{I}v|^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}{\rm div\,}u|Z^{I}v|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(7.33)

\displaystyle\quad-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v)\cdot[\nabla,Z^{I}]v+[Z^{I},r_{0}^{-1}]\nabla v\cdot Z^{I}\nabla v\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}\nabla q\cdot Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\textbf{n}\cdot Z^{I}(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v)\cdot Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}[Z^{I},{\rm div\,}](r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v)\cdot Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}v\cdot H^{I}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\colon{\mathcal{H}}_{0}+\cdots{\mathcal{H}}_{6}.

We now control
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}-{\mathcal{H}}_{6}.
Firstly, let us bound
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}-{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
as:

(7.34)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{0}+{\mathcal{H}}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+T\|Z^{I}v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}|\!|\!|{\rm div\,}u|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Next, by using the identity (
2.18
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we control
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v)\cdot[\nabla,Z^{I}]v+[Z^{I},r_{0}^{-1}]\nabla v\cdot Z^{I}\nabla v\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\|r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\mu\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|[Z^{I},r_{0}^{-1}]\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla v,Zr_{0})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, for the term
{\mathcal{H}}_{3}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}\nabla q\cdot Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,
we apply Proposition
7.4
to write:

{\mathcal{H}}_{3}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}\cdot\nabla q_{1}Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}\nabla q_{2}\cdot Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

By assumption,
Z^{I}
contains at least one spatial tangential derivative,
we thus estimate the first term by integration by parts in space and using the estimate for
\nabla q_{1}
in (
7.19
):

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}\nabla q_{1}\cdot Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla q_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Thanks to the estimate for
\nabla q_{2}
in (
7.19
), we can control the second one directly by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}\nabla q_{1}\cdot Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\nabla q_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

The above two estimates then yield that:

(7.35)

{\mathcal{H}}_{3}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

We now deal with the boundary term
{\mathcal{H}}_{4}=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\textbf{n}\cdot Z^{I}(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v)\cdot Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s,
which can be split further into three terms
{\mathcal{H}}_{4}={\mathcal{H}}_{41}+{\mathcal{H}}_{42}+{\mathcal{H}}_{43}
with

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{41}

\displaystyle=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}Z^{I}(r_{0}^{-1}\partial_{\textbf{n}}v\cdot\textbf{n})(Z^{I}v\cdot\textbf{n})\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{42}

\displaystyle=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}Z^{I}(\Pi(r_{0}^{-1}\partial_{\textbf{n}}v))\cdot\Pi Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{43}

\displaystyle=-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\big([Z^{I},\textbf{n}](r_{0}^{-1}\partial_{\textbf{n}}v)\cdot(Z^{I}v\cdot\textbf{n})+[Z^{I},\Pi](r_{0}^{-1}\partial_{\textbf{n}}v)\cdot\Pi Z^{I}v,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+[Z^{I},\textbf{n}](r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v)\cdot Z^{I}v\big)\,\mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s.

Let us begin with the treatment of
{\mathcal{H}}_{41}.
One may assume that
Z^{I}
contains at least one spatial tangential derivative, that is
Z^{I}=\partial_{y^{j}}Z^{\tilde{I}},j=1,2.

By the identity (
2.15
),

(7.36)

\partial_{\textbf{n}}v\cdot\textbf{n}=-(\Pi\partial_{y^{1}}v)^{1}-(\Pi\partial_{y^{2}}v)^{2}.

Moreover, by the boundary condition (
7.6
),

(Z^{I}v\cdot\textbf{n})|_{\partial\Omega}=-([Z^{I},\textbf{n}]v)|_{\partial\Omega},

we thus can use the duality between
H^{-1}(\partial\Omega)
and
H^{1}(\partial\Omega)
, the trace inequality (
15.20
) to get that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{41}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu|r_{0}^{-1}\partial_{\textbf{n}}v\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\big|[Z^{I},\textbf{n}]v\big|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}(|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}+|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}})\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

(7.37)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Note that in the derivation of the last inequality, we have used the facts:

(7.38)

\displaystyle\|v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\|(\theta,\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\sigma,\mu{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Next, for
{\mathcal{H}}_{42},
we use the boundary condition (
7
) to obtain that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{42}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\varepsilon|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}\Pi u|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}+\mu\big|r_{0}^{-1}\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}v)-\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}\Pi u\big|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\mu\varepsilon|(\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma},\Pi u)|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}}+\mu|(u,v,r_{0}^{-1})|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Applying the trace inequality (
15.20
) and the property (
7.38
) as well as the estimate (
15.12
) for
r_{0},r_{0}^{-1},

one finds that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{42}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(u,\nabla u,\nabla\tilde{\sigma},\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

(7.39)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Finally, the term
{\mathcal{H}}_{43}
can be controlled roughly by

(7.40)

{\mathcal{H}}_{43}\lesssim\mu|r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}\lesssim\mu|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma},\nabla v)|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

By the identity (
7.36
), the boundary condition (
7
), we find that:

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\partial_{\textbf{n}}v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}+\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}v)|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}+\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|(u,v,\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma},\varepsilon\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma})|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

The trace inequality (
15.20
) and the estimates (
4.20
)
thus yield that

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}+\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\theta,\nabla\theta,\nabla(\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma},\varepsilon\nabla\tilde{\sigma}))\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

(7.41)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, by the fact (
7.38
) and the trace inequality (
15.20
),
we can get more precisely that:

(7.42)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{\frac{1}{2}})\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Plugging the above two inequalities into (
7.40
), we find:

(7.43)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{43}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2},

which, together with (
7
) and (
7
), yields:

(7.44)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{4}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We now proceed to control the term
{\mathcal{H}}_{5}=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}[Z^{I},{\rm div\,}](r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v)\cdot Z^{I}v\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.
By using the identity (
2.18
), we integrate by parts to get that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{5}\lesssim\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\mu|r_{0}^{-1}\nabla v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}|v|_{L_{t}^{2}\tilde{H}^{m-1}},

which, combined with (
7
), (
7.42
) and the fact

\displaystyle\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(r_{0}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla(r_{0}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

leads to the estimate:

(7.45)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{5}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

It remains the control of
{\mathcal{H}}_{6}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}v\cdot H^{I}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

which can be split into two terms:

{\mathcal{H}}_{6}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}v\cdot Z^{I}G_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}v\cdot Z^{I}(H^{I}-Z^{I}G_{1})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

where
G_{1}
is defined in (
7.10
). Similar to the estimate of
{\mathcal{H}}_{2},
we integrate by parts in space for the first term use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for both of these two terms:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{6}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}v\cdot Z^{I}G_{1}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}Z^{I}v\cdot Z^{I}(H^{I}-Z^{I}G_{1})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\|H^{I}-Z^{I}G\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}.

Using the product estimate (
15.1
) and the commutator estimate (
15.3
), one finds that:

\displaystyle\|\mu\nabla r_{0}^{-1}\cdot\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma},\nabla u,\nabla\theta,\varepsilon\nabla\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle\|[Z^{I},u\cdot\nabla]v\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim(\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}})\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with (
7.12
) and (
7.25
), leads to the estimate

\|{\mathcal{H}}^{I}-Z^{I}G_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Therefore, we can estimate
{\mathcal{H}}_{6}
by using (
7.11
) for
G_{1}
and the above inequality:

(7.46)

{\mathcal{H}}_{6}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Inserting (
7.34
)-(
7
), (
7.44
)-(
7.46
) into (
7
),
we find by summing up for
|I|\leq m-1,I_{0}\leq m-2
that

(7.47)

\displaystyle\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

In view of the (
7.31
) and (
7.47
),
we finish the proof of (
7.30
).
∎

Remark 7.8
.

We can now conclude that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

(7.48)

\displaystyle\mu\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Indeed, in view of (
6.4
), (
7.30
), it stems from the estimate:

(7.49)

\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\mathbb{P}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which can be shown in the following way. On the one hand, for any conormal vector fields

Z^{I},|I|\leq m-1,
we write

r_{0}Z^{I}\nabla\mathbb{P}u=Z^{I}(r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u)-Z^{I}r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u-[Z^{I},r_{0},\nabla\mathbb{P}u].

This gives rise to the estimate:

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\mathbb{P}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

(7.50)

\displaystyle\qquad+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}Zr_{0},\nabla\mathbb{P}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|Zr_{0}|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla\mathbb{P}u|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

On the other hand,
by the identity

\nabla v-r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u=\nabla\mathbb{P}(r_{0}u)-r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u=\nabla[\mathbb{P},r_{0}]u+\nabla r_{0}\cdot\mathbb{P}u,

the estimate

\displaystyle\|\nabla[\mathbb{P},r_{0}]u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\rm div\,}([\mathbb{P},r_{0}]u),\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits([\mathbb{Q},r_{0}]u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\nabla r_{0}\cdot\mathbb{P}u,\nabla r_{0}\times\mathbb{Q}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}},

as well as (
7.9
), one can find that:

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla v-r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla r_{0},u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla r_{0}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(u,\mathbb{Q}u)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

This, together with (
7.8
), yields (
7.49
).

Lemma 7.9
.

Denote
u^{j}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}u,
and
v^{j}=\mathbb{P}(r_{0}u^{j}),
we have the following estimate for

v^{j},

(7.51)

\displaystyle\|v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1-j}}^{2}\lesssim Y_{m}^{2}(0)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),\,\,\forall j=0,1\cdots m-1,

where we use
{\mathcal{H}}^{k}={\mathcal{H}}^{0,k}
to denote the norm that involves only spatial conormal derivatives.

Proof.

In fact, (
7.51
) can be derived from the estimate (
7.30
) and the following property:

\displaystyle\|v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1-j}}\lesssim\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which, in light of the definition

v^{j}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}v+\mathbb{P}([r_{0},(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}]u),

is the consequence of the following estimate:

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{P}([r_{0},(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}]u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1-j}}\lesssim\|[r_{0},(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}]u\|_{{}_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1-j}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\partial_{t}r_{0},u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\partial_{t}r_{0},u)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

∎

8.
Uniform estimates for the gradient of the velocity

In the previous section, we have controlled uniformly the incompressible part
v^{j}=\mathbb{P}(r_{0}u^{j})
of the unknowns
r_{0}u^{j}=r_{0}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}u\,(j=0,\cdots m-1)

in the space
L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1-j}.

In this section, we focus on the uniform estimate of
\nabla v^{j}
in
L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}
which is crucial in order to control the
L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}
norm of the gradient of the velocity.

Proposition 8.1
.

Suppose that (
1.34
)
holds and
m\geq 7
, then there exists a constant
\vartheta_{3}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,
the following estimate holds:

(8.1)

\displaystyle\|\nabla v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),\quad\forall\,j=0,\cdots m-2.

Proof.

Since in the interior domain, the conormal spatial derivatives are equivalent to the standard spatial derivatives, we only have to estimate
\nabla v
near the boundary, say

\|\chi_{i}\nabla v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}
where

\chi_{i},(i=1\cdots N)
are smooth functions associated to the covering (
1.13
) and are compactly supported in
\Omega_{i}.
Close to the boundary, it follows from the identities (
7.36
) and

(8.2)

\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}v^{j})=\Pi((\nabla v^{j}-Dv^{j})\textbf{n})+\Pi((Dv^{j})\textbf{n})=\Pi(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits v^{j}\times\textbf{n})+\Pi\nabla(v^{j}\cdot\textbf{n})+\Pi(-(D\textbf{n})v^{j})

that (since
\Pi\nabla
only involves tangential derivative):

\displaystyle\|\chi_{i}\nabla v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\chi_{i}\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}v^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}+\|v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\chi_{i}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits v^{j}\times\textbf{n})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}+\|v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1-j}}.

Consequently, this proposition can be proved by combining the estimate (
7.51
) for

v^{j}
and the next lemma for
\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits v^{j}\times\textbf{n}.

∎

Proposition 8.2
.

Under the same assumption as in Proposition
8.1
, there exists
\vartheta_{3}>0,
such that
for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,
any
j=0,\cdots m-2
, it holds that:

(8.3)

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\{1,\cdots N\}}\|\chi_{i}(\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\textbf{n})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

where
\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}=\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits v^{j}=\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}u),
and
\chi_{i}
is a smooth function compactly supported in
\Omega_{i}.

Proof.

Let us first derive the equation satisfied by
\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}=\colon\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}u).

Applying time derivatives
(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}
on the equations
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{2},
we find that
u^{j}=\colon(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}u
solves the equation:

(8.4)

\frac{1}{R\beta}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)u^{j}+\frac{\nabla\tilde{\sigma}^{j}}{\varepsilon}+\mu\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{j}={\mathcal{C}}_{u}^{j},

where

\tilde{\sigma}^{j}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}\tilde{\sigma}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}(\sigma-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma{\rm div\,}u),

(8.5)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{u}^{j}=\colon

\displaystyle-[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\frac{1}{R\beta}]\partial_{t}u-[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\frac{1}{R\beta}u\cdot\nabla]u

\displaystyle-\mu\lambda_{1}[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\Gamma]\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u-\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}({\rm div\,}u\nabla\Gamma).

Similar to the derivation of (
7.3
), we rewrite this equation as:

(8.6)

(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)(r_{0}u^{j})+\varepsilon^{-1}(1+\varepsilon r_{1})\nabla{\tilde{\sigma}^{j}}+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u^{j})=G^{j}

where

\displaystyle G^{j}

\displaystyle=u^{j}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u^{j}

(8.7)

\displaystyle\qquad-\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}r_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{j}-\mu\lambda_{1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{j}+(1+\varepsilon r_{1}){\mathcal{C}}_{u}^{j}.

Applying the curl of the equations (
8.6
), we find that
\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}
solves the equation:

(8.8)

(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\Delta\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}=\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits G^{j}-\sum_{l=1}^{3}\nabla u_{l}\times\partial_{l}(r_{0}u^{j})+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}.

Taking the cross product of the above equations with
\chi_{i}\textbf{n},
we can derive further the equation for
\chi_{i}\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\textbf{n}:

(8.9)

(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)(\chi_{i}\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\textbf{n})-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\Delta(\chi_{i}\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\textbf{n})=H_{i}^{j},

where

H_{i}^{j}=H_{i1}^{j}+H_{i2}^{j}
with

(8.10)

\displaystyle H_{i1}^{j}

\displaystyle=\colon\big(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits G^{j}+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}-\sum_{l=1}^{3}\nabla u_{l}\times\partial_{l}(r_{0}u^{j})\big)\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n},

\displaystyle H_{i2}^{j}

\displaystyle=\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times(u\cdot\nabla(\chi_{i}\textbf{n}))-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\big(2{\rm div\,}(\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\nabla(\chi_{i}\textbf{n}))-\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\Delta(\chi_{i}\textbf{n})\big).

Moreover, it follows from the identity (
8.2
) and the boundary condition (
1.16
) that:

(8.11)

\displaystyle\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\textbf{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}v^{j})+\Pi((D\textbf{n})v^{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\textbf{n}}r_{0}\Pi u^{j}+r_{0}\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}u^{j})-\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}\mathbb{Q}(r_{0}u^{j}))+\Pi((D\textbf{n})v^{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}r_{0}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}\Pi u^{j}+2r_{0}\Pi\big(-au^{j}+(D\textbf{n})u^{j}\big)\qquad\quad\text{ on }\partial\Omega.

The important feature here is that
\chi_{i}\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\textbf{n}
is governed by a transport-diffusion equation (
8.8
) without any singular terms. Nevertheless, due to the non-homogeneous Dirichlet boundary condition (
8.11
),
the uniform estimates can not be obtained by simply performing energy estimates. Our general strategy is to split the system for
\chi_{i}(\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\textbf{n})
into two parts, one of which solves a heat equation (with variable coefficients) with the nontrivial Dirichlet boundary condition and non-homogeneous source term.
The other satisfies a convection-diffusion equation with homogeneous Dirichlet boundary condition which is amenable to energy estimates.
To deal with the first system,
the explicit formula for heat equation with variable coefficient will play an important role. It is thus helpful to transform the problem to the half-space. Instead of coordinates (
1.14
), it is convenient here for us to use another parameterization, which is the so-called
normal geodesic coordinates: (by shrinking
\Omega_{i}
and taking smaller
\delta_{i},\epsilon_{i}
if necessary)

(8.12)

\displaystyle{\Psi}_{i}:

\displaystyle(-\delta_{i},\delta_{i})^{2}\times(0,\epsilon_{i})\rightarrow\Omega_{i}\cap{\Omega}

\displaystyle(y,z)^{t}\rightarrow{\Psi}_{i}(y,z)=(y,\varphi_{i}(y))^{t}-z\textbf{n}^{i}(y)

where
\textbf{n}^{i}(y)=\textbf{N}^{i}/|\textbf{N}^{i}|
,

\textbf{N}^{i}(y)=(\partial_{y^{1}}\varphi_{i}(y),\partial_{y^{2}}\varphi_{i}(y),-1)^{t}.
Note that since we are considering at most
m-2
order of regularities, the following estimates hold for a domain with
C^{m+2}
smooth boundaries. As we work in the local coordinates (
8.12
), it is useful to know the expressions of gradient and Laplacian in the new coordinates.
By straightforward calculations, one can find that:

(8.13)

\displaystyle(\nabla f)\circ\Psi_{i}=

\displaystyle P_{i}{\nabla}(f\circ{\Psi_{i}}),\quad({\rm div\,}F)\circ\Psi_{i}=\frac{1}{d_{i}}{{\rm div\,}}\big(d_{i}P_{i}^{*}(F\circ\Psi_{i})\big),\quad

\displaystyle(\Delta f)\circ\Psi_{i}=\frac{1}{d_{i}}{{\rm div\,}}\big(d_{i}P_{i}^{*}P_{i}\nabla\big(f\circ\Psi_{i})\big),

where
P_{i}=(D\Psi_{i})^{-1}
,
d_{i}=\det(D\Psi_{i})
and the matrix

(8.14)

D\Psi_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1&0&\partial_{y^{1}}\varphi_{i}\\
0&1&\partial_{y^{2}}\varphi_{i}\\
n_{1}^{i}&n_{2}^{i}&n_{3}^{i}\end{array}\right)-z\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\partial_{y^{1}}\textbf{n}_{1}^{i}&\partial_{y^{1}}\textbf{n}_{2}^{i}&\partial_{y^{1}}\textbf{n}_{3}^{i}\\
\partial_{y^{2}}\textbf{n}_{1}^{i}&\partial_{y^{2}}\textbf{n}_{2}^{i}&\partial_{y^{2}}\textbf{n}_{3}^{i}\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right).

Note that since
|\det((D\Psi_{i})|_{z=0})|=|\textbf{N}^{i}|\geq 1,
the matrix is invertible
as long as
z
is sufficiently small.
By further computation, one could
deduce that the Laplacian in this new coordinates has the form

(8.15)

\frac{1}{d_{i}}{{\rm div\,}}\big(d_{i}P_{i}^{*}P_{i}\nabla\cdot\big)=\partial_{z}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}(\ln|g_{i}|)\partial_{z}+\Delta_{{g_{i}}}

where
\Delta_{{g_{i}}}
involves only the tangential derivatives:

(8.16)

\Delta_{{g_{i}}}=|{g_{i}}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{l,k=1}^{2}\partial_{y^{l}}(g_{i}^{lk}|{g_{i}}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y^{k}}),

and
|g_{i}|
denotes the determinate of the symmetric invertible matrix

(8.17)

g_{i}(y,z)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}g_{i}^{11}&g_{i}^{12}\\
g_{i}^{21}&g_{i}^{22}\end{array}\right)(y,z)

whose entries depends smoothly on
D\Psi_{i}.
Note that
|g_{i}|
has uniform lower bound and the matrix
g_{i}
is positive definite
as long as
z
is small enough.

For a function
f:\Omega_{i}\cap{\Omega}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},
we
use the notation
f^{\Psi_{i}}=f\circ\Psi_{i}.
We will also denote

\tilde{\zeta}_{i}^{j}=(\chi_{i}\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\textbf{n})^{\Psi_{i}}.
Since
{\rm Supp}\,\chi_{i}|_{\bar{\Omega}}\Subset\Omega_{i}\cap\bar{\Omega},

we can extend the definition of
\tilde{\zeta}_{i}^{j}
and
(H_{i}^{j})^{\Psi_{i}}
from
{\Psi}_{i}^{-1}(\Omega_{i}\cap\bar{\Omega})
to
\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}
by zero extension,
which are still denoted as
\tilde{\zeta}_{i}^{j}
and
(H_{i}^{j})^{\Psi_{i}}.

By (
8.9
),
\tilde{\zeta}_{i}^{j}
solves the equation:

(8.18)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{u}_{i}\cdot\nabla)\tilde{\zeta}_{i}^{j}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(\frac{1}{r_{0}})^{\Psi_{i}}(\partial_{z}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}(\ln|g_{i}|)\partial_{z}+\Delta_{{g_{i}}})\tilde{\zeta}_{i}^{j}=(H_{i}^{j})^{\Psi_{i}},\,\,(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\[2.5pt]
\tilde{\zeta}_{i}^{j}|_{z=0}=\big[\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}r_{0}\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}\Pi u^{j}+2r_{0}\chi_{i}\Pi\big((D\textbf{n})u^{j}-au^{j}\big)\big]^{\Psi_{i}}|_{z=0},\\[2.5pt]
\tilde{\zeta}_{i}^{j}|_{t=0}=(\chi_{i}\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}|_{t=0}\times\textbf{n})^{\Psi_{i}},\end{array}\right.

where
\nabla=(\partial_{y^{1}},\partial_{y^{2}},\partial_{y^{3}})
and
{\rm div\,}=(\nabla)^{*}

stand for the gradient and the divergence in the new coordinates and
\tilde{u}_{i}
is defined as:

(8.19)

\tilde{u}_{i}=(\tilde{u}_{i,1},\tilde{u}_{i,2},\tilde{u}_{i,3})=P_{i}^{*}(\chi_{i}u)^{\Psi_{i}}.

Note that on the boundary
z=0,

(8.20)

\tilde{u}_{i,3}|_{z=0}=\big[(D\Psi_{i}|_{z=0})^{-*}(\chi_{i}u)^{\Psi_{i}}\big]_{3}\big|_{z=0}=-((\chi_{i}u\cdot\textbf{N}^{i})|_{\partial\Omega})^{\Psi_{i}}=0.

Let us set

(8.21)

\mathcal{Z}_{j}=\partial_{y^{j}},\,\,j=1,2,\quad\mathcal{Z}_{3}=\phi(z)\partial_{z}

and define energy norm

(8.22)

\|f^{\Psi_{i}}\|_{m,t}=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}f^{\Psi_{i}}\|_{L^{2}([0,t]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})},\,\|f^{\Psi_{i}}(t)\|_{m}=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|(\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}f^{\Psi_{i}})(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}

and
L_{t,x}^{\infty}
norm:

(8.23)

|\!|\!|f^{\Psi_{i}}|\!|\!|_{*,m,\infty,t}=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq m}\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\alpha}f^{\Psi_{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})},

where
\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}=\mathcal{Z}_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\mathcal{Z}_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{Z}_{3}^{\alpha_{3}},\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}).
We emphasize that the above norms involves only spatial conormal derivatives and will be frequently used throughout this section. For later use, let us also define here:

(8.24)

|\!|\!|f^{\Psi_{i}}|\!|\!|_{m,\infty,t}=\sum_{k\leq m}\|(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{k}f^{\Psi_{i}}\|_{*,m-k,\infty,t}.

Since

{\Psi_{i}}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{i}
is a diffeomorphism between
\Phi_{i}^{-1}(\Omega_{i}\cap\Omega)\rightarrow\Psi_{i}^{-1}(\Omega_{i}\cap\Omega)
and satisfies
({\Psi_{i}}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{i})|_{z=0}={\rm Id}\,,
we can deduce from the Leibniz’s rule and the definition of the conormal spaces (
1.10
) that:

(8.25)

\|f^{\Psi_{i}}\|_{k,t}\approx\|f\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{k}(\Omega)},\quad\|f^{\Psi_{i}}(t)\|_{k}\approx\|f(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{k}(\Omega)}.

By (
8.25
), the proof of (
8.3
) can be reduced to the following estimate for
\tilde{\zeta}_{i}^{j}
.
∎

Proposition 8.3
.

Under the same assumption as in Proposition
8.1
,
there exists a constant
\vartheta_{3}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,
any
j=1\cdots m-2,
any
i=1,\cdots N,
the following estimate holds:

(8.26)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\tilde{\zeta}_{i}^{j}(t)\|_{m-2-j}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}\big).

Remark 8.4
.

Note that the notations (
8.22
)-(
8.23
) will be used frequently in the proof of this proposition.

Proof.

In the following, we will drop the subscript
i
for the sake of the notational clarity
and in particular, we will write
\tilde{\zeta}_{i},(H_{i}^{j})^{\Psi_{i}},\tilde{u}_{i}
by

\tilde{\zeta}^{j},(H^{j})^{\Psi},\tilde{u}.
We introduce further

\zeta^{j}=|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\tilde{\zeta}^{j}

and derive from (
8.18
)
the equation satisfied by
\zeta^{j}:

(8.27)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla){\zeta}^{j}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(\frac{1}{r_{0}})^{\Psi}(\partial_{z}^{2}+\Delta_{{g}})\zeta^{j}=|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}(H^{j})^{\Psi}+H^{j,{\dagger}},\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\[2.5pt]
\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}=\big[\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}r_{0}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi u^{j}+2r_{0}|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi\big((D\textbf{n})u^{j}-au^{j}\big)\big]^{\Psi}|_{z=0},\\[2.5pt]
\zeta^{j}|_{t=0}=|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\tilde{\zeta}^{j}|_{t=0},\end{array}\right.

where

(8.28)

H^{j,{\dagger}}=\big(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla(|g|^{\frac{1}{4}})\big)(|g|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\zeta^{j})-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\big(|g|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\partial_{z}^{2}(|g|^{\frac{1}{4}})\zeta^{j}-[\Delta_{g},|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}](|g|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\zeta^{j})\big).

Since
g
involves two derivations of the boundary parameterization function
\varphi,
one can deduce from the assumption on the smoothness of the boundary that
g,|g|,|g|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\in C^{m}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}).
Therefore, in order to show
(
8.26
), it suffices to establish the following estimate for
\zeta^{j}\,(j=0,1\cdots m-2),

(8.29)

\displaystyle\|\zeta^{j}(t)\|_{m-2-j}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}\big)\quad\forall\,0<t\leq T.

Due to some technical reason,
we have to distinguish the case
j=0
or
1\leq j\leq m-2.

The estimate (
8.29
) is thus the consequence of the following two estimates:

(8.30)

\displaystyle\|\zeta^{j}(t)\|_{m-2-j}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}\big),\quad j=1,2\cdots m-2,

(8.31)

\displaystyle\|\zeta^{0}(t)\|_{m-2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{5}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}\big),

where the constants
\vartheta_{4},\vartheta_{5}\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
are independent of
\varepsilon,\mu,\kappa.

The above two estimates can be achieved by splitting the system in suitable (different) ways, we will detail them in the following two subsections respectively.
∎

8.1.
Uniform high regularity with at least one time derivatives-proof of (
8.30
)

This section is dedicated to the high regularity estimate for
\zeta^{j}(1\leq j\leq m-2)
which is summarized in
(
8.30
). As explained before, such an estimate is unlikely to be obtained by sorely energy estimates due to the non-homogeneous boundary conditions. The strategy to prove (
8.30
) is to
split
\zeta^{j}
into two unknowns:

\zeta^{j}=\zeta_{1}^{j}+\zeta_{2}^{j},
where
\zeta_{1}^{j},\zeta_{2}^{j}
solve the following systems respectively:

(8.32)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}{\zeta}_{1}^{j}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(\frac{1}{r_{0}})^{\Psi}|_{z=0}\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{1}^{j}=0\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\[2.5pt]
\zeta_{1}^{j}|_{z=0}=\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}=\big[\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}r_{0}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi u^{j}+2r_{0}|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi\big((D\textbf{n})u^{j}-au^{j}\big)\big]^{\Psi}|_{z=0},\\[2.5pt]
{\zeta}_{1}^{j}|_{t=0}=0,\end{array}\right.

(8.33)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla){\zeta_{2}^{j}}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(\frac{{1}}{r_{0}})^{\Psi}(\partial_{z}^{2}+\Delta_{{g}})\zeta_{2}^{j}={\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j},\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\[2.5pt]
\zeta_{2}^{j}|_{z=0}=0,\quad\zeta_{2}^{j}|_{t=0}=\zeta^{j}|_{t=0},\end{array}\right.

with the source term

(8.34)

{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j}=-(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla){\zeta_{1}^{j}}+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\bigg(\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}\Delta_{{g}}\zeta_{1}^{j}+\big(\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}-\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}\big|_{z=0}\big)\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{1}^{j}\bigg)+H^{j,{\dagger}}+|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}(H^{j})^{\Psi}.

The estimate (
8.30
) thus follows from
the studies on
\zeta_{1}^{j}
and
\zeta_{2}^{j}
carried out in Proposition
8.5
and Proposition
8.6
respectively.

We begin with the estimate for
\zeta_{1}^{j}(1\leq j\leq m-2).

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
\zeta_{1}^{j}
be the solution of the equations (
8.32
). Suppose that
m\geq 7
and the assumption (
1.34
) holds.
By using the notation in (
8.22
),
the following estimates hold for
\zeta_{1}^{j}:

for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

any
0<t\leq T,

(8.35)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\zeta_{1}^{j}|\!|\!|_{*,[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),\quad\forall j=1,2\cdots[\frac{m}{2}]-1,

(8.36)

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{1}^{j}(t)\|_{m-2-j}+\|\zeta_{1}^{j}\|_{m-1-j,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},\quad\forall j=1,2\cdots m-2.

Proof.

For notational convenience, we denote that

(8.37)

b_{0}=b_{0}(t,y)=\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}|_{z=0}.

Note that by the assumption (
1.34
) and the property (
1.33
),

(8.38)

c_{0}\leq b_{0}(t,y)\leq\frac{1}{c_{0}},\quad\forall\,(t,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}.

Applying (
15.46
) with
a_{1}=a_{2}=a_{3}=0,
we get the following formula:

(8.39)

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}^{j}(t,y,z)

\displaystyle=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\frac{1}{(\pi Y)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{z}{2Y}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}\big)(t,t^{\prime},y)(b_{0}\zeta^{j}|_{z=0})(t^{\prime},y)\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime},

where

(8.40)

Y=Y(t,t^{\prime},y)=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}b_{0}(\tau,y)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Note that by (
8.38
), we have that:

(8.41)

\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}c_{0}(t-t^{\prime})\leq Y(t,t^{\prime},y)\leq\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(t-t^{\prime})/c_{0},\quad\forall\,0\leq t^{\prime}\leq t\leq T,y\in\times\mathbb{R}^{2}.

We first show the
L^{\infty}_{t,x}
estimate (
8.35
) by assuming
1\leq j\leq[\frac{m}{2}]-1.

Let
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3})
be a multi-index with length
|\alpha|\leq[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j.
It follows from the property
(
15.29
) found and proved in the appendix that: there is a polynomial
P_{2|\alpha|+1}
with degree
2|\alpha|+1,
such that:

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\alpha}\zeta_{1}^{j}(t,\cdot,z)\|_{L_{y}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{Y}P_{2|\alpha|+1}\big(\frac{z}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

(8.42)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t}\big)\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha}\big|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}\big|_{L^{\infty}([0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Thanks to (
8.41
), (
8.38
)
we find that for any
z\in\mathbb{R}_{+},0\leq t\leq T,

\displaystyle\mu\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{Y}P_{2|\alpha|+1}\big(\frac{z}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\lesssim\mu z^{-2}\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{P}_{2|\alpha|+3}\bigg(\big(\frac{z^{2}}{\mu(t-t^{\prime})}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(t-t^{\prime})/c_{0}}}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\leq C(\frac{1}{c_{0}}),

where we denote
\tilde{P}_{2|\alpha|+3}(\cdot)=|\cdot|^{2}P_{2|\alpha|+1}(\cdot).

Moreover, in view of the expression of
\zeta|_{z=0}
in
\eqref{eqzeta1}_{2}
and the definition (
8.37
) for
b,
we get that for any
j=1,2\cdots[\frac{m}{2}]-1,

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\zeta_{1}^{j}|\!|\!|_{*,[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma},r_{0})|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}u|\!|\!|_{*,[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

(8.43)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|b_{0}|\!|\!|_{*,[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|b_{0}|\!|\!|_{*,m-3,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{*,m-3,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Inserting the previous three estimates into (
8.42
), we
find (
8.35
).

Let us now take a vector field
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}

and start to estimate

\|\zeta_{1}^{j}(t)\|_{m-2-j}
by assuming
0\leq|\gamma|\leq m-2-j\leq m-3.

Thanks to the properties (
8.41
) and (
15.29
), we have by using Minkowski inequality that:

(8.44)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\zeta_{1}^{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}\lesssim\mu\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(\mu(t-t^{\prime}))^{\frac{3}{4}}}|\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}(t^{\prime},\cdot)|_{H^{m-2-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b_{0}|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big),

which, combined with (
8.43
), yields,

(8.45)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\zeta_{1}^{j}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\mu T)^{\frac{1}{4}}|\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2-j}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

We now detail the estimate of
\|\zeta_{1}^{j}(t)\|_{m-1-j,t}.
Taking a multi-index
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}),
with
0\leq|\alpha|\leq m-1-j,

we use the property (
15.30
) if
j=1
and (
15.29
) if
2\leq j\leq m-2
to find that

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\alpha}\zeta_{1}^{j}(s)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}}{(s-s^{\prime})^{\frac{3}{4}}}\big(\big|\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}(s^{\prime})\big|_{{H}^{m-1-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+|b_{0}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\big)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b_{0}|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\zeta^{1}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t,y}^{\infty}}\big).

By using Young’s inequality in time (after extending
\zeta|_{z=0}
by zero to
t\leq 0
and
t\geq T
), and the trace inequality (
15.20
),
we obtain that for any
0<t\leq T,

(8.46)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\alpha}\zeta_{1}^{j}\|_{L^{2}([0,t]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\mu T)^{\frac{1}{4}}\big(\big|\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}\big|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{m-1-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+|b_{0}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-2}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

In view of the estimates (
8.45
) and (
8.46
), we can finish the proof of (
8.36
) by showing the following estimates for

\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}
and
b:

(8.47)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2-j}\cap L_{t}^{2}{H}^{m-1-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+|b_{0}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),\,1\leq j\leq m-2.

The estimate for
b
follows from the definition (
8.37
), the trace inequality (
15.19
) and the estimate (
15.12
) for
r_{0}^{-1},
we omit the detail.
Let us focus on the estimate of
\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}

and detail the estimate of
\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.
In view of its definition in
\eqref{eqzeta1}_{2}

we can control this term in the following way.
If
m-2-j\leq[\frac{m}{2}]-1,
we use the trace inequality (
15.19
), the estimate (
4.21
) for
\tilde{\sigma}
to find that:

\displaystyle|\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim|u^{j}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{m-2-j}(\partial\Omega)}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma},\varepsilon\nabla\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

If
m-2-j\geq[\frac{m}{2}],
or
j\leq[\frac{m-3}{2}],
we use successively the classical Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
), the
trace inequality (
15.19
), the estimate (
15.12
) for
r_{0},
(
4.20
) for
\tilde{\sigma}

to obtain that

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}\big|(u^{j},\varepsilon\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma},r_{0})\big|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{m-2-j}(\partial\Omega)}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(u^{j},\partial_{\textbf{n}}\sigma,r_{0})|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\nabla r_{0}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+|\varepsilon\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{m-3}(\partial\Omega)}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The
L_{t}^{2}{H}^{m-1-j}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2})
norm of
\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}
can be controlled in a similar way. More precisely, one can use the trace inequality (
15.20
) and the estimates (
4.19
) (
4.20
) for
\tilde{\sigma}
to get:

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\zeta^{j}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{2}{H}^{m-1-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}\big|(u^{j},\varepsilon\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma},r_{0})\big|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{m-1-j}(\partial\Omega)}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\big(\|(u,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\varepsilon|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{m-2}(\partial\Omega)}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The proof of (
8.47
) is now complete. ∎

To show (
8.30
), it remains to control
\zeta_{2}^{j}
(defined in (
8.33
)),
which is the aim of the following proposition.

Proposition 8.6
.

Let
\zeta_{2}^{j}\,(j=1,2\cdots m-2)
be the solution to the system (
8.33
). Suppose that the assumption (
1.34
) holds and
m\geq 7.
There exists
\vartheta_{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{2}],

such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

(8.48)

\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

Taking a multi-index
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})
with
|\gamma|=k\leq m-2-j
and applying
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\alpha}
on the equations (
8.33
), we find that
\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}=\colon{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\zeta_{2}^{j}
solves the following equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla)\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\partial_{z}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0})+\partial_{y^{k}}(({g^{kl}}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0})\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma})\big)={\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j}+\sum_{i=1}^{5}{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{j,\gamma},\\
\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}|_{z=0}=0,\quad\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}|_{t=0}={\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}(\zeta^{j}|_{t=0}),\end{array}\right.

where
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j}
is defined in (
8.34
) and

(8.51)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{j,\gamma}

\displaystyle=-[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla]\zeta_{2}^{j},\quad{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{j,\gamma}=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\big(\partial_{z}^{2}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{2}^{j}\big),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{3}^{j,\gamma}

\displaystyle=-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}([{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\partial_{z}]+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma})\big(\partial_{z}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{2}^{j}\big),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{4}^{j,\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big([{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\partial_{z}](\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0})+\partial_{z}([{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},1/r^{\Psi}_{0}]\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j})\big),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{j,\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\bigg(\partial_{y^{k}}\big([{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},{g^{kl}}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0}]\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta_{2}^{j}\big)-{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\big(\partial_{y^{k}}({|g|^{-\frac{1}{2}}}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0})g^{kl}|g|^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta_{2}^{j}\big)\bigg).

Note that Einstein’s summation convention has been employed.

Performing the standard energy estimates on (
8.1
) and using the boundary condition (
8.20
), we find that:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}|\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}|^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\big(|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}|^{2}+g^{kl}\partial_{y^{k}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\big)/r^{\Psi}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(8.52)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}|\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}|^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}x-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\partial_{z}]\zeta_{2}^{j}\cdot\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}/r^{\Psi}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}|\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}|^{2}{\rm div\,}\tilde{u}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot\big({\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{j,\gamma}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

Since the matrix
g
defined in (
8.17
) is positive definite, and
r_{0}^{\Psi}\leq\frac{1}{c_{0}}
by the assumption (
1.34
) and the property (
1.33
),
we can find a constant
\iota>0
which are independent of
\varepsilon,\mu,\kappa,
such that

(8.53)

\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\big(|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}|^{2}+g^{kl}\partial_{y^{k}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\big)/r^{\Psi}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\geq\iota c_{0}\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}.

Moreover, by induction, one gets that for
k=|\gamma|\geq 1,

(8.54)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\beta<\gamma}C_{\phi,\beta,\gamma}\partial_{z}\mathcal{Z}^{\beta}

where
C_{\phi,\beta,\gamma}
are smooth functions that depend on
\phi
and its derivatives. We can thus control the second term in the right hand side of (
8.1
) by using the
Young’s inequality:

\displaystyle\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\partial_{z}]\zeta_{2}^{j}\cdot\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}/r^{\Psi}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(8.55)

\displaystyle\leq\delta c_{0}\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}^{2}+C(\delta,\frac{1}{c_{0}})\mu\sum_{0\leq|\beta|\leq|\gamma|-1}\|\nabla{\mathcal{Z}}^{\beta}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{0,t}.

Next, by using the fact (
8.25
) and the estimate (
8.36
), we have that:

(8.56)

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\zeta^{j},\zeta_{2}^{j})\|_{m-2-j,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|\zeta_{2}^{j}(s)\|_{m-2-j}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which, allows us to control the third term as:

(8.57)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}|\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}|^{2}{\rm div\,}\tilde{u}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}^{2}|\!|\!|{\rm div\,}\tilde{u}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim T\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Our following task is to control the commutator terms

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{j,\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

(
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{j,\gamma}-{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{j,\gamma}
are defined in (
8.51
)),
which rely on the estimates for
{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{j,\gamma}
presented in
Lemma
8.7
.
We first use (
8.56
) and the estimate (
8.69
) to get that,

(8.58)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot({\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{j,\gamma}+{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{j,\gamma})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\leq\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\|({\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{j,\gamma},{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{j,\gamma})\|_{0,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Next, in view of the identity (
8.54
),
the vanishing boundary condition (
8.1
), we can integrate by parts in space and use the Young’s inequality as well as the estimates (
8.70
) (
8.71
) to obtain that:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{R}}_{3}^{j,\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}\|\partial_{z}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}

(8.59)

\displaystyle\leq\delta c_{0}\mu\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}^{2}+T\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{R}}_{4}^{j,\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-3-j,t}|\!|\!|1/r^{\Psi}_{0}|\!|\!|_{*,m-3,\infty,t}

(8.60)

\displaystyle\leq\delta c_{0}\mu\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}^{2}+C_{\delta}\mu\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{k-1,t}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}^{2}\big).

Finally, it follows from the estimates (
8.56
), (
8.72
) and Young’s inequality that:

(8.61)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{j,\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\|{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{\gamma}\|_{0,t}

\displaystyle\leq\delta c_{0}\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}^{2}+C(\delta)T\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Collecting (
8.58
)-(
8.61
),
we find:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{5}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{j,\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq 3\delta c_{0}\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}^{2}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}\mu\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-3-j,t}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}^{2}\big).

We are now left to control the term

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s
appearing in
(
8.1
). According to the definition (
8.34
) for
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j},
it can be written as the sum of the following four terms:

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}((\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla){\zeta_{1}^{j}})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{2}

\displaystyle=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\bigg(\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}\Delta_{{g}}\zeta_{1}^{j}+\big(\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}-\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}\big|_{z=0}\big)\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{1}^{j}\bigg)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}H^{j,{\dagger}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\mathscr{S}_{4}=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}(|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}(H^{j})^{\Psi})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s.

The control of these terms are based on the properties for the terms contained in
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j},

which are listed in Lemma
8.8
.
Thanks to the estimate (
8.56
), the estimate (
8.79
) for the convection term
(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla){\zeta_{1}^{j}},
we control the first term directly as:

(8.62)

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{1}\lesssim\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\|(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla){\zeta_{1}^{j}}\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Moreover, by the virtue of the identities (
8.80
), (
8.54
), we integrate by parts in space to control the term
\mathscr{S}_{2}
as

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{2}\lesssim\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}\|({\mathcal{S}}_{21}^{j},{\mathcal{S}}_{22}^{j},{\mathcal{S}}_{23}^{j})\|_{m-2-j,t}+\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\|{\mathcal{S}}_{24}^{j}\|_{0,t}.

It then follows from Young’s inequality, the estimates (
8.56
), (
8.81
) that

(8.63)

\mathscr{S}_{2}\leq\delta c_{0}\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}^{2}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Next, in view of the definition (
8.28
) for
H^{j,{\dagger}}
and the fact (
8.25
),
we can have that:

\displaystyle\|H^{j,{\dagger}}\|_{m-2-j,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\tilde{u},\zeta^{j},\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}\zeta^{j})\|_{m-2-j,t}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u,({\rm Id}\,,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u^{j}))\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Consequently, one finds by the estimate (
8.56
) that:

(8.64)

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{3}\lesssim\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\|H^{j,{\dagger}}\|_{m-2-j,t}\leq T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

It remains to control
\mathscr{S}_{4},
which requires a closer look on
(H^{j})^{\Psi}
defined in (
8.10
). We will show in Lemma
8.8
that it takes the form

(H^{j})^{\Psi}=\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{y^{1}}{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{1}+\partial_{y^{2}}{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{2}+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{3})+{\mathcal{H}}_{4}^{j},

where
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{j}-{\mathcal{H}}_{4}^{j}
are some vector valued functions with amenable property (
8.82
). As a result, it follows successively the integration by parts, Young’s inequality and the estimate (
8.56
) that:

(8.65)

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\|({\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{j},{\mathcal{H}}_{2}^{j},{\mathcal{H}}_{3}^{j})\|_{m-2-j,t}+\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\|{\mathcal{H}}_{4}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}

\displaystyle\leq\delta c_{0}\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}+C_{\delta}(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

In view of (
8.62
)-(
8.65
), we have obtained that:

(8.66)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{2}^{j,\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\leq 3\delta c_{0}\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}+C_{\delta}T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Plugging the estimates (
8.53
) (
8.1
), (
8.57
), (
8.1
) (
8.66
) into (
8.1
), one finds by choosing
\delta
sufficiently small compared to
\iota
that for any
0\leq k\leq m-2-j,

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{k,t}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{k,t}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\iota}Y_{m}^{2}(0)+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}+\mu\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{k-1,t}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}^{2}\big).

Note that the convention
\|\cdot\|_{l,t}=0
if
l=-1
has been employed. This estimate in hand, we can derive by induction on
k
that:

(8.67)

\displaystyle\qquad\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}^{2}+Y_{m}^{2}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

However, by the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
), the estimate (
4.19
) for
\tilde{\sigma},

one has:

(8.68)

\displaystyle\varepsilon|\!|\!|\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\varepsilon\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Inserting the above estimate and (
4.42
) into (
8.67
) and recalling that

\Lambda
are polynomials, we thus find a constant
\vartheta_{4}\in(0,\frac{1}{2}],
which is independent of
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
such that,

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

∎

In the following two lemmas, we state and prove some estimates for
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{j,\gamma}-{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{j,\gamma}
and

{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j}
respectively, which have been used in the proof of the previous proposition.

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{j,\gamma}-{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{j,\gamma}
be defined in (
8.51
). Under the assumption (
1.34
)
we have
for any
1\leq j\leq m-2,|\gamma|\leq m-2-j,
any

\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

(8.69)

\displaystyle\|({\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{j,\gamma},{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{j,\gamma})\|_{0,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(8.70)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(8.71)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0}\|_{m-3-j,t}+\|[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},1/r^{\Psi}_{0}]\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-3-j,t}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big),

(8.72)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

We first control
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{j,\gamma}
in
L_{t}^{2}L^{2}
norm. Let us write:

(8.73)
