[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla]=[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}_{1}]\partial_{y^{1}}+[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}_{2}]\partial_{y^{2}}+[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi]{\mathcal{Z}}_{3}.

Therefore, by arguing separately for
1\leq j\leq[\frac{m}{2}]-2
and
j\geq[\frac{m}{2}]-1,
we can have that:

\displaystyle\|[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla]\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{0,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\tilde{u}_{1},\tilde{u}_{2},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)\|_{m-3,t}|\!|\!|\zeta_{2}^{j}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t}\mathbb{I}_{\{1\leq j\leq[\frac{m}{2}]-2\}}

(8.74)

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|\zeta_{2}^{j}(s)\|_{m-2-j}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|(\tilde{u}_{1},\tilde{u}_{2},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)(s)\|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For instance, if
1\leq j\leq[\frac{m}{2}]-2,
we control

[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi]{\mathcal{Z}}_{3}\zeta_{2}^{j}
as:

\displaystyle\|[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi]{\mathcal{Z}}_{3}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\|\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi\|_{m-3,t}|\!|\!|\zeta_{2}^{j}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t}+\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|\zeta_{2}^{j}(s)\|_{m-2-j}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|(\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)(s)\|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

while if
j\geq[\frac{m}{2}]-1,|\gamma|\leq m-2-j\leq[\frac{m-1}{2}],
we have:

\displaystyle\|[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi]{\mathcal{Z}}_{3}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|\zeta_{2}^{j}(s)\|_{m-2-j}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|(\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)(s)\|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since
\tilde{u}_{3}|_{z=0}=0,
we can use the Hardy inequality and the fundamental theorem of calculus respectively to obtain that:

(8.75)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi\|_{k,t}\lesssim\|\nabla\tilde{u}_{3}\|_{k,t},\quad\|\tilde{u}_{3}(s)/\phi\|_{k,\infty}\lesssim|\!|\!|\partial_{z}\tilde{u}_{3}(s)\|_{k,\infty}.

Note that by the definition of
\tilde{u}
in (
8.19
), one could find a smooth vector valued function
\psi
which depends only on the boundary parametrization
\varphi,
such that

\partial_{z}\tilde{u}_{3}=(\partial_{\textbf{n}}(\chi u)\cdot\textbf{n})^{\Psi}+\psi\cdot z\partial_{z}(\chi u)^{\Psi}+\partial_{z}(D\Psi)^{-*}(\chi u)^{\Psi}.

This, together with (
8.75
), leads to that:

(8.76)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi(s)\|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|{\rm div\,}u(s)\|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty}^{2}+\|u(s)\|_{[\frac{m+1}{2}],\infty}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, in view of (
8.35
), for any
1\leq j\leq[\frac{m}{2}]-2
,

(8.77)

|\!|\!|\zeta_{2}^{j}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|(\zeta^{j},\zeta_{1}^{j})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u^{j}))^{\Psi},\zeta_{1}^{j}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

The estimate for
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{j,\gamma}
in (
8.69
) then follows from
the facts (
8.25
), (
8.56
) and the estimates (
8.1
)-(
8.77
).

Let us switch to the control of
\|{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{\gamma}\|_{0,t}.
We distinguish the case
1\leq j\leq[\frac{m}{2}]-1,
and

j\geq[\frac{m}{2}]
to find that:

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\|\partial_{z}^{2}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-3,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\|\partial_{z}^{2}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\|_{m-2-j,t}|\!|\!|\zeta_{2}^{j}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}|\!|\!|\mu\partial_{z}^{2}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}.

It results from (
15.13
) and the definition (
7.1
) for
r_{0}
that:

\displaystyle\mu\|\partial_{z}^{2}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\|_{m-3,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\mu\|(\nabla,\nabla^{2})r_{0}^{-1}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\mu\partial_{z}^{2}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla,\mu\nabla^{2})(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

The proof of (
8.69
) is thus finished.

Next, the property (
8.70
) follows from similar arguments as above,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\partial_{z}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\|_{m-3,t}+\|\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t})(\|\partial_{z}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\zeta_{2}^{j}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t})

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

As for the proof of (
8.71
), by noticing the fact
|\gamma|\leq m-2-j\leq m-3,
we attribute the
L_{t,x}^{\infty}
norm on
1/r^{\Psi}_{0}
to obtain:

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0}\|_{m-3-j,t}+\|[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},1/r^{\Psi}_{0}]\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-3-j,t}|\!|\!|1/r^{\Psi}_{0}|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}.

Observe that by the definition (
7.1
) for
r_{0},

(8.78)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|1/r^{\Psi}_{0}|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big).

Finally, to show (
8.72
), we count the number of derivatives hitting on

1/r^{\Psi}_{0}.
The highest one
m-2
happens only when
j=1
and the vector field
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}
hits on
\partial_{y^{k}}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0}).
Therefore, we can conclude that

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{j,\gamma}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\zeta_{2}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}|\!|\!|1/r^{\Psi}_{0}|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+\|\partial_{y}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\|_{m-3,t}|\!|\!|\zeta_{2}^{1}|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}.

Note that by (
15.12
), it holds that:

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\|_{m-3,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

∎

In the next lemma, we give some estimates concerning
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{j}.

Lemma 8.8
.

Under the same assumption as in Lemma
8.7
,
the following three aspects hold for any
1\leq j\leq m-2
any

\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T:

(1). The convection term enjoys the estimate:

(8.79)

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla){\zeta_{1}^{j}}\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

(2). There exist four functions
{\mathcal{S}}_{21}^{j}-{\mathcal{S}}_{24}^{j}
such that:

(8.80)

\displaystyle(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\Delta_{{g}}\zeta_{1}^{j}+\big((1/r^{\Psi}_{0})-(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})|_{z=0}\big)\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{1}^{j}=\partial_{y^{1}}{\mathcal{S}}_{21}^{j}+\partial_{y^{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{22}^{j}+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{S}}_{23}^{j}+{\mathcal{S}}_{24}^{j},

and

(8.81)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|({\mathcal{S}}_{21}^{j},{\mathcal{S}}_{22}^{j},{\mathcal{S}}_{23}^{j})\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},\quad\mu\|{\mathcal{S}}_{24}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

(3). Let
H^{j}
be defined in (
8.10
),

(H^{j})^{\Psi}=H^{j}\circ\Psi
has the following form:

(H^{j})^{\Psi}=\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{y^{1}}{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{1}+\partial_{y^{2}}{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{2}+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{3})+{\mathcal{H}}_{4}^{j},

where the vector valued functions
{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{1}-{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{4}
enjoy the property:

(8.82)

\sum_{l=1}^{3}\|{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{l}\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},\quad\|{\mathcal{H}}_{4}^{j}\|_{m-2-j}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

\underline{Proof\,of\,(1)}.

Let us write

(8.83)

\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla=\tilde{u}_{1}\partial_{y^{1}}+\tilde{u}_{2}\partial_{y^{2}}+(\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi){\mathcal{Z}}_{3}.

Following the similar arguments as the estimate of

{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{\gamma}
in the last lemma,
we can control the convection term as:

\displaystyle\|{(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla)\zeta_{1}^{j}}\|_{m-2-j,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\tilde{u}_{1},\tilde{u}_{2},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)\|_{m-2,t}|\!|\!|\zeta_{1}^{j}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1-j,\infty,t}\mathbb{I}_{\{1\leq j\leq[\frac{m}{2}]-2\}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|\zeta_{1}^{j}(s)\|_{m-2-j}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|(\tilde{u}_{1},\tilde{u}_{2},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)(s)\|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\zeta_{1}^{j}(s)\|_{m-1-j,t}|\!|\!|(\tilde{u}_{1},\tilde{u}_{2},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t},

which, combined with (
8.35
), (
8.36
), (
8.75
), (
8.76
), yields (
8.79
).

\underline{Proofof\,(2)}.
By direct computation, we can set:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{2k}^{j}=\sum_{l=1}^{2}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})g^{kl}\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta_{1}^{j},\quad k=1,2,\quad{\mathcal{S}}_{23}^{j}=\big((1/r^{\Psi}_{0})-(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})|_{z=0}\big)\partial_{z}\zeta_{1}^{j}-\partial_{z}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{1}^{j},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{24}^{j}=-\partial_{y^{k}}(|g|^{-\frac{1}{2}}/r^{\Psi}_{0})g^{kl}|g|^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta_{1}^{j}+\partial_{z}^{2}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{1}^{j}.

Since
j\geq 1,
we can use (
8.36
) and (
8.78
) to control
{\mathcal{S}}_{2k}^{j}
:

\|{\mathcal{S}}_{2k}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim\|\zeta_{1}^{j}\|_{m-1-j,t}|\!|\!|1/r^{\Psi}_{0}|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We now sketch the proof of (
8.81
).
For the first term of
{\mathcal{S}}_{23}^{j},
we take benefit of the vanishing boundary condition for

\mathscr{H}=(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})-(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})|_{z=0}
to obtain that:

(8.84)

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{H}\cdot\partial_{z}\zeta_{1}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim\|\mathscr{H}/\phi\|_{m-3,t}|\!|\!|{\mathcal{Z}}_{3}\zeta_{1}^{j}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-j-2}\mathbb{I}_{\{1\leq j\leq[\frac{m}{2}]-2\}}+\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{3}\zeta_{1}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}|\!|\!|\mathscr{H}/\phi|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty,t}.

It follows from the fundamental theorem of calculus, the Hardy inequality as well as the estimate for
r_{0}^{-1}
in (
15.12
) that:

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\mathscr{H}/\phi|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty,t}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{H}/\phi\|_{m-3,t}\lesssim\|\partial_{z}\mathscr{H}\|_{m-3,t}\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which, together with (
8.84
), (
8.36
) yield,

\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathscr{H}\cdot\partial_{z}\zeta_{1}^{j}\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The second term in
{\mathcal{S}}_{23}^{j}
can be controlled in the same way as in (
8.70
), the estimate for
{\mathcal{S}}_{24}^{j}
bears some resemblances with the control of
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{\gamma}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{\gamma}
in the previous lemma, we omit the detail and thus finish the proof of (
8.81
).

Proof of  (3)
.
For convenience, we write here the definitions of
H^{j}=H_{1}^{j}+H_{2}^{j}:

\displaystyle H_{1}^{j}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits G^{j}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}-\sum_{l=1}^{3}\nabla u_{l}\times\partial_{l}(r_{0}u^{j})\big)\times\chi\textbf{n},

\displaystyle H_{2}^{j}

\displaystyle=\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times(u\cdot\nabla(\chi_{i}\textbf{n}))-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}(2{\rm div\,}(\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\nabla(\chi\textbf{n})-\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\Delta(\chi\textbf{n})),

and

\displaystyle G^{j}-(1+\varepsilon r_{1}){\mathcal{C}}_{u}^{j}

\displaystyle=u^{j}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u^{j}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\mu r_{1}\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{j}-\mu\lambda_{1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{j},

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{u}^{j}
is defined in (
8.5
).
By looking at every term appearing in the definition of
H_{1}^{j}
and
H_{2}^{j},
we find that there are three troublesome terms that may involve one more normal derivative than what we can control, namely,

\displaystyle H_{11}^{j}=\colon\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\big(G^{j}-(1+\varepsilon r_{1}){\mathcal{C}}_{u}^{j}\big)\times\chi\textbf{n},

\displaystyle H_{12}^{j}=\colon\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\big)\times\chi\textbf{n},\quad H_{21}^{j}=\colon-2\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}{\rm div\,}(\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\nabla(\chi\textbf{n})).

To continue, it is convenient to write these four terms as a perfect derivative plus reminders.
In view of the identity

(8.85)

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits f\times\chi\textbf{n}=\partial_{\textbf{n}}(\chi f)-\nabla(\chi\textbf{n}\cdot f)+D(\chi\textbf{n})f-f\partial_{\textbf{n}}\chi,

we split further

\displaystyle H_{11}^{j}=H_{111}^{j}+(H_{11}^{j}-H_{111}^{j}),

where

(8.86)

\displaystyle H_{111}^{j}=\big(\partial_{\textbf{n}}(\chi\cdot)-\nabla(\chi\textbf{n}\cdot)\big)\big(G^{j}-(1+\varepsilon r_{1}){\mathcal{C}}_{u}^{j}\big).

Moreover, let us write

(8.87)

\displaystyle H_{12}^{j}

\displaystyle=-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\chi\partial_{\textbf{n}}(r_{0}^{-1})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}-\chi\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\cdot\textbf{n}\big)

\displaystyle=-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\big(\chi\partial_{\textbf{n}}(r_{0}^{-1})\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\big)+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\nabla(\chi\partial_{\textbf{n}}(r_{0}^{-1})\times\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}+\chi\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\cdot\textbf{n}\big)

\displaystyle=\colon H_{121}^{j}+H_{122}^{j}

and

(8.88)

\displaystyle H_{21}^{j}

\displaystyle=-2\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}\big(r_{0}^{-1}\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\nabla(\chi\textbf{n})\big)+2\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\nabla r_{0}^{-1}\cdot\big(\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\nabla(\chi\textbf{n})\big)=\colon H_{211}^{j}+H_{212}^{j}.

By the change of variable, one can find three functions
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{j},{\mathcal{H}}_{2}^{j},{\mathcal{H}}_{3}^{j}
such that:

(8.89)

\displaystyle(H_{111}^{j}+H_{121}^{j}+H_{211}^{j}\big)^{\Psi}=\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\partial_{y^{1}}{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{1}+\partial_{y^{2}}{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{2}+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{H}}^{j}_{3}\big)

and
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{j}-{\mathcal{H}}_{3}^{j}
are the linear combinations of the following quantities (with the coefficients depending on
\chi,(D\Psi)^{-1}
and their first two derivatives),

\hbox to9.93pt{\vbox to9.93pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-4.9644pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=2\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(r_{0}^{-1}\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\times\nabla(\chi\textbf{n})\big)^{\Psi}.

We can then define

{\mathcal{H}}_{4}^{j}
as:

(8.90)

{\mathcal{H}}_{4}^{j}=\big(H_{1}^{j}-\sum_{l=1}^{2}H_{1l1}^{j}+H_{2}^{j}-H_{211}^{j}\big)^{\Psi},

where
H_{111}^{j},H_{121}^{j},H_{211}^{j}
are defined in (
8.86
)-(
8.88
).

Now it remains to justify the property (
8.82
). First, by the definition, we have that:

(8.91)

\displaystyle\|({\mathcal{H}}_{1}^{j},{\mathcal{H}}_{2}^{j},{\mathcal{H}}_{3}^{j})\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim\big\|(\hbox to9.93pt{\vbox to9.93pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-4.9644pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},\hbox to9.93pt{\vbox to9.93pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-4.9644pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

Similar (and indeed easier) to the proof of (
8.70
), it can be verified that:

(8.92)

\displaystyle\big\|\hbox to9.93pt{\vbox to9.93pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-4.9644pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.59 0 C 6.59 3.64 3.64 6.59 0 6.59 C -3.64 6.59 -6.59 3.64 -6.59 0 C -6.59 -3.64 -3.64 -6.59 0 -6.59 C 3.64 -6.59 6.59 -3.64 6.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},\hbox to9.93pt{\vbox to9.93pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-4.9644pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.59 0 C 6.59 3.64 3.64 6.59 0 6.59 C -3.64 6.59 -6.59 3.64 -6.59 0 C -6.59 -3.64 -3.64 -6.59 0 -6.59 C 3.64 -6.59 6.59 -3.64 6.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\big\|_{m-2-j,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\chi\partial_{\textbf{n}}(r_{0}^{-1})\omega_{r_{0}}^{j},r_{0}^{-1}\omega_{r_{0}}^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\omega_{r_{0}}^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}+\|\chi\partial_{\textbf{n}}(r_{0}^{-1}),(Zr_{0}^{-1})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The estimate of
1
requires some efforts since it contains four terms (see (
8.1
)).
In view of the identity (
7.15
), we have that
modulus some constants
C=C(R,C_{v},\gamma),

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}\approx-\kappa\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}r_{0}\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)-\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\varepsilon r_{0}(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)(\Gamma{\rm div\,}u).

Since
(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

it holds by Remark
1.5
that

\kappa\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}.
Denoting
\mathscr{P}
as a placeholder of

(\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta),u,Zr_{0},Z{\rm div\,}u,Z\Gamma),

one finds that:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\mathscr{P}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

It then follows from the generalized Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (in space) that:

\displaystyle\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3}}\lesssim\|\mathscr{P}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\mathscr{P}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We thus can control the term
\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}u^{j}((\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0})
as (note that
j\geq 1
):

(8.93)

\displaystyle\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}u^{j}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0})\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}|\!|\!|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}+\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3}}|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Since
j\geq 1
and
|\!|\!|(\sigma,\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}
is uniformly bounded, we control the next two terms
by attributing all the
L_{t,x}^{\infty}
norm on
r_{1}\Gamma,\,\varepsilon^{-1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})

(8.94)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\varepsilon r_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{j},(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(r_{1}\Gamma,\varepsilon^{-1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}))|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Finally, for the commutator term
\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u^{j},

one has by noticing the identity (
7.13
) that:

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u^{j}\approx\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla^{2}r_{0})u^{j}+\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla r_{0})\nabla u^{j},

which enables us to obtain:

(8.95)

\displaystyle\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{0}^{-1}(\nabla^{2}r_{0},\nabla r_{0})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3}}+\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}\big)\cdot

\displaystyle\big(|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{0}^{-1}\nabla^{2}r_{0}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla u,r_{0}^{-1}\nabla r_{0}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Note that by (
15.12
), (
15.13
),

\displaystyle\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{0}^{-1}(\nabla^{2}r_{0},\nabla r_{0})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3}}\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2})(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We can now conclude from (
8.93
)-(
8.95
) that

\|\hbox to9.93pt{\vbox to9.93pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-4.9644pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.59 0 C 6.59 3.64 3.64 6.59 0 6.59 C -3.64 6.59 -6.59 3.64 -6.59 0 C -6.59 -3.64 -3.64 -6.59 0 -6.59 C 3.64 -6.59 6.59 -3.64 6.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\|_{m-2-j,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

which, together with (
8.92
) and (
8.91
), yields the first inequality in (
8.82
).

The estimate for
{\mathcal{H}}_{4}^{j}
(defined in (
8.90
)) is a bit lengthy since it contains many terms. However, most of them can be dealt with in the similar manner as we performed before. We thus only detail one term appearing in
\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\big((1+\varepsilon r_{1}){\mathcal{C}}_{u}^{j}\big)

which may not be obvious, namely

\mu\lambda_{1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\big((1+\varepsilon r_{1})[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\Gamma]\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\big)=\mu\lambda_{1}[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\Gamma]\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u+\cdots,

where we denote
\cdots
as the terms which are similar or easier to control. We thus focus on the first term in the right hand side.
The equation
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{2}
can be rewritten

(8.96)

-\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u=\frac{1}{R\beta}(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)u+\nabla\tilde{\sigma}+\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})({\rm div\,}u)\nabla\Gamma,

from which we can find that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(u,\theta,\sigma,\varepsilon\mu{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(8.97)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu|\!|\!|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u|\!|\!|_{\frac{[m]}{2}-2,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\sigma,u,\theta)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-2,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Note that the properties (
4.19
), (
4.21
) for
\tilde{\sigma}
have been employed.
One thus obtains from the above two estimates that:

\displaystyle\mu\lambda_{1}\|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\big((1+\varepsilon r_{1})[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},\Gamma]\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\big)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\partial_{t}\Gamma,\mu\lambda_{1}\varepsilon\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We now finished the proof of the second estimate in (
8.82
) and thus the proof of the aspect
(3).

∎

8.2.
Uniform estimates for high spatial regularity-proof of (
8.31
)

In this subsection, we devote ourselves to proving the estimate (
8.31
) for
\zeta=\zeta^{0}=(\chi\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)\times\textbf{n})^{\Psi},
which relies again on a suitable splitting of the system (
8.27
).
Before doing so, we define

(8.98)

r_{2}=\frac{1}{R\beta(0)}\exp\big(-\theta+\frac{R}{C_{v}\gamma}\varepsilon\sigma\big),\quad b_{2}(t,y)=\big(\frac{1}{r_{2}}\big)^{\Psi}|_{z=0},\,\,b_{0}(t,y)=\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}|_{z=0}.

By (
7.1
), the following relation holds:

(8.99)

\displaystyle r_{0}=r_{2}\exp(-\varepsilon^{2}\mu\bar{C}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u)\quad\text{ with }\bar{C}=(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})R/{C_{v}\gamma}.

Let us write
\zeta=\zeta_{3}+\zeta_{4}+\zeta_{5},
where
\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4},\zeta_{5}

solve the following three problems:

(8.100)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}{\zeta}_{3}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}b_{2}\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{3}=0,\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\[2.5pt]
\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}=\big[\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{C_{v}\gamma}((\frac{r_{0}}{\beta}u\cdot\nabla)\textbf{n}\cdot u)|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi u+2r_{0}|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi\big((D\textbf{n})u-au\big)\big]^{\Psi}|_{z=0},\\[2.5pt]
{\zeta}_{3}|_{t=0}=0,\end{array}\right.

(8.101)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}{\zeta}_{4}+\mu(1-\Delta)\zeta_{4}=0,\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\[2.5pt]
\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}=\big[\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}r_{0}(\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}-\frac{1}{R\beta}(\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)\textbf{n}\cdot u)|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi u\big]^{\Psi}|_{z=0},\\[2.5pt]
{\zeta}_{4}|_{t=0}=0,\end{array}\right.

(8.102)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla){\zeta_{5}}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(\frac{1}{r_{0}})^{\Psi}(\partial_{z}^{2}+\Delta_{{g}})\zeta_{5}={\mathcal{S}}_{5},\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\[2.5pt]
\zeta_{5}|_{z=0}=0,\quad\zeta_{5}|_{t=0}=\zeta|_{t=0},\end{array}\right.

with the source term

(8.103)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{5}

\displaystyle=-(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla)(\zeta_{3}+\zeta_{4})+{\mathcal{S}}_{3}+{\mathcal{S}}_{4}+|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}(H^{0})^{\Psi}+H^{0,{\dagger}},

where
(H^{0})^{\Psi},H^{0,{\dagger}}
are defined in (
8.10
), (
8.28
) and

(8.104)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{3}=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\bigg(\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}\Delta_{{g}}\zeta_{3}+\big(\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}})^{\Psi}-b_{2}\big)\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg),

(8.105)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{4}=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}(\Delta_{{g}}+\partial_{z}^{2})\zeta_{4}-\partial_{t}\zeta_{4}.

Note that the system (
8.101
) and (
8.102
) satisfy the compatibility condition thanks to the assumption
u_{0}|_{\partial\Omega}=0.

Moreover,
by the definition of
\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{5},

it holds that:

\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}+\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}+\zeta_{5}|_{z=0}=\zeta|_{z=0}.

The desired estimate (
8.29
) is thus the consequence of the Proposition
8.9
, Proposition
8.11

and Proposition
8.16
for
\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4}
and
\zeta_{5}
respectively.

Proposition 8.9
.

Suppose that
m\geq 7
and the assumption (
1.34
) holds. We have the following estimates for
\zeta_{3}
which solves the system (
8.100
): for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

any
0<t\leq T,

(8.106)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad|\!|\!|\zeta_{3}|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),\quad

(8.107)

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{3}(t)\|_{m-2}\lesssim(\mu T)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},\quad\|\zeta_{3}\|_{m-1,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(8.108)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{3}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(8.109)

\displaystyle\quad\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}\zeta_{3}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

We apply (
15.46
) with

a_{1}=a_{2}=a_{3}=0
to obtain the following formula:

(8.110)

\displaystyle\zeta_{3}(t,y,z)

\displaystyle=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(\pi Y)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{z}{2Y}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}b_{2}(t^{\prime},y)\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}(t^{\prime},y)\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

where

\displaystyle b_{2}=(\frac{1}{r_{2}})^{\Psi}|_{z=0},\quad Y=Y(t,t^{\prime},y)=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}b_{2}(\tau,y)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

By the assumption (
1.34
) and the property (
1.33
), it holds that for any
0\leq t^{\prime}\leq t\leq T,y\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

(8.111)

c_{0}\leq b_{2}(t,y)\leq{1}/{c_{0}},\,\quad\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}c_{0}(t-t^{\prime})\leq Y(t,t^{\prime},y)\leq\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(t-t^{\prime})/c_{0}.

The first estimate (
8.106
) can be shown by following the similar arguments as in the proof of
(
8.35
) done in Proposition
8.5
,
we thus omit the details.

Let us now focus on the proof of (
8.107
).
Thanks to (
8.41
) and the properties (
15.29
), (
15.30
) in the appendix,
we find for any vector fields
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}
with
|\gamma|\leq m-2,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\zeta_{3}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}\lesssim\mu\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b_{2}|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t,y}^{\infty}}\big)\cdot

(8.112)

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{(\mu(t-t^{\prime}))^{\frac{3}{4}}}\bigg(\big(|\partial_{y}^{m-2}b_{2}(t^{\prime},\cdot)|+\big|\frac{1}{t-t^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\partial_{y}^{m-2}b_{2}(\tau,\cdot)\mathrm{d}\tau\big|\big)+\sum_{k\leq m-2}|\partial_{y}^{k}\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}|\bigg)(t^{\prime},\cdot)\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\bigg\|_{L_{y}^{2}}.

In view of the explicit expression of
\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}
in
\eqref{eqzeta3}_{2},
we
apply successively the Minkowski inequality,
the classical Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
to get that:

(8.113)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\zeta_{3}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\mu T)^{\frac{1}{4}}\big(|\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+|\partial_{y}b_{2}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\big)\cdot

\displaystyle\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b_{2}|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t,y}^{\infty}}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\mu T)^{\frac{1}{4}}|(u,r_{0},r_{0}^{-1})|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2}(\partial\Omega)}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Thanks to the trace inequality (
15.19
) and the estimate (
15.12
), we can conclude that:

(8.114)

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{31}(t)\|_{m-2}\lesssim(\mu T)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Let us proceed to control the
\|\cdot\|_{m-1,t}
norm.
We use (
8.111
) and properties (
15.29
), (
15.30
) in appendix to find that, for any vector field
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}
with
|\gamma|\leq m-1,

\displaystyle\qquad\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\zeta_{31}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}}{(t-t^{\prime})^{\frac{3}{4}}}\big(\big|\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}(t^{\prime})\big|_{{H}^{m-1}}+|\partial_{y}b_{2}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2}}\big)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b_{2}|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|({\rm Id}\,,{\mathcal{Z}})\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t,y}^{\infty}}\big).

Hereafter, we denote for simplicity
H^{k}=H^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{2}).

By the convolution inequality in time (after extending
\zeta|_{z=0}
by zero to
t\leq 0
and
t\geq T
), we get further

(8.115)

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{31}\|_{m-1,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\mu T)^{\frac{1}{4}}\big(|\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{2}{H}^{m-1}}+|\partial_{y}b_{2}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, one can apply the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality in

\mathbb{R}^{2}
and the trace inequality (
15.20
) to derive that:

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\zeta_{3}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{2}{H}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|(u,\beta^{-1},r_{0})|_{L_{t}^{2}{H}^{m-1}(\partial\Omega)}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla)(u,\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}|\partial_{y}b_{2}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{H}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla b_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\partial_{y}b_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Inserting these two estimates into (
8.115
),
we achieve the second estimate in (
8.107
).

Proof of (
8.108
)-(
8.109
)
.
Let us define

\tilde{\zeta}_{3}=\varepsilon(\zeta_{3}-h),\quad h=\colon\big[\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{C_{v}\gamma}\big((\frac{r_{0}}{\beta}u\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{n}\cdot u)|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi u+2r_{0}|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi\big((D\textbf{n})u-au\big)\big]^{\Psi}.

By equations (
8.100
), we find that
\tilde{\zeta}_{3}
solves the system:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}{\tilde{\zeta}}_{3}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}b_{2}(t,y)\partial_{z}^{2}\tilde{\zeta}_{3}=-\varepsilon\partial_{t}h+\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}b_{2}(t,y)\partial_{z}^{2}h=\colon h_{1},\\[2.0pt]
{\tilde{\zeta}}_{3}|_{z=0}=0,\quad{\tilde{\zeta}}_{3}|_{t=0}=-\varepsilon h|_{t=0}.\end{array}\right.

As one has:

\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}h\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

to prove (
8.108
),
it suffices to show:

(8.118)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}\tilde{\zeta}_{3}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim Y_{m}(0)+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The solution of the system (
8.2
) has the form

\displaystyle\tilde{\zeta}_{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(K_{-}-K_{+})(t,t^{\prime},y,z^{\prime},z)h_{1}(t^{\prime},y,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(K_{-}-K_{+})(t,0,y,z^{\prime},z)\varepsilon h|_{t=0}(y,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\colon\tilde{\zeta}_{31}+\tilde{\zeta}_{32}

where

K_{\pm}(t,t^{\prime},y,z^{\prime},z)=\frac{1}{(4\pi Y)^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\frac{|z\pm z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4Y}},\quad Y=Y(t,t^{\prime},y)=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}b_{2}(\tau,y)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

It can be verified that

(8.119)

\displaystyle\|h_{1}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla,\mu\varepsilon\nabla^{2})(u,\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

By taking benefits of the properties (
15.31
), (
15.33
), (
15.34
) proved in the appendix, we have that, for any vector fields
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}
with
|\gamma|\leq m-2,

(8.120)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\tilde{\zeta}_{31}=C_{\gamma_{1}}^{\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}C_{\gamma_{2}}^{\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}\sum_{0\leq\tilde{\gamma}_{j}\leq\gamma_{j},j=1,2,3}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(z^{\prime}\partial_{z^{\prime}})^{\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}\big(

\displaystyle F_{\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}^{{\gamma}_{3},\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}(t,t^{\prime},y,z-z^{\prime})-F_{\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}^{{\gamma}_{3},\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}(t,t^{\prime},y,z+z^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}^{{\gamma}_{1}-\tilde{\gamma}_{1}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}^{{\gamma}_{1}-\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}h_{1}(t^{\prime},y,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

where
F_{\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}^{{\gamma}_{3},\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}(t,t^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot)
are smooth functions that satisfy:

if

\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}=\tilde{\gamma}_{1}+\tilde{\gamma}_{2}=m-2,

(8.121)

\displaystyle\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}F_{\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}^{{\gamma}_{3},\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}(t,t^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L_{y}^{2}L_{z}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(t-t^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b_{2}|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)|b_{2}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{m-2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(t-t^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}

and if
\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}\leq m-3,

(8.122)

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}F_{\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}^{{\gamma}_{3},\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}(t,t^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L_{z}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(t-t^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b_{2}|_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(t-t^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Note that the following estimates for
b_{2}
have been employed to derive the second inequality in the above two estimates:

\displaystyle|b_{2}|_{m-3,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle|b_{2}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{m-2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

In view of the estimates (
8.120
)-(
8.122
), we find that by the convolution inequality in time variable (after zero extensions of
h_{1}
on
t\leq 0,
and
t\geq T
),

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\tilde{\zeta}_{31}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}F_{\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}^{{\gamma}_{3},\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}(t,t^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L_{y}^{2}L_{z}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}|\!|\!|h_{1}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\mathbb{I}_{\{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}=m-2\}}

\displaystyle+\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}F_{\tilde{\gamma}_{1},\tilde{\gamma}_{2}}^{{\gamma}_{3},\tilde{\gamma}_{3}}(t,t^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L_{y}^{\infty}L_{z}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\|h_{1}(t^{\prime})\|_{m-2}\mathbb{I}_{\{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}\leq m-3\}}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|h_{1}\|_{m-2,t}+{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Summing up for
|\gamma|\leq m-2,
and using (
8.119
), we can conclude that

\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}\tilde{\zeta}_{31}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, by integration by parts in
z
variable, we can verify that:

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}\tilde{\zeta}_{32}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}h|_{t=0}\|_{m-2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big).

We thus obtain (
8.108
) from the above two estimates.

Finally, as the proof of (
8.109
) can be reduced to the control
\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\zeta_{3}}\|_{0,t},
we multiply the system (
8.2
) by
\partial_{t}\tilde{\zeta}_{3},
integrate in space and time and use Young’s inequality
to obtain:

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\zeta}_{3}\|_{0,t}^{2}+\mu\|\partial_{z}\tilde{\zeta}_{3}(t)\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mu\|\partial_{z}\tilde{\zeta}_{3}(0)\|_{0}^{2}+\|h_{1}\|_{0,t}^{2}+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\partial_{t}b|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)\mu\|\partial_{z}\tilde{\zeta}_{3}\|_{0,t}^{2}.

The above estimate, together with the fact:

\displaystyle\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}h\|_{0,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

leads to (
8.109
).
∎

Corollary 8.10
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{3}
be defined in (
8.104
), the following estimate holds:

(8.123)

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{S}}_{3}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

By the assumption (
1.34
), the property (
1.33
)
and estimate (
8.107
), we have directly:

\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}\Delta_{{g}}\zeta_{3}\|_{0,t}\leq(\mu T)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}}\big)\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|\zeta_{3}\|_{2}\lesssim(\mu T)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, we write by using the relation (
8.99
), the equation
(
8.100
)
that:

\displaystyle\qquad\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}-b_{2}\big)\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{3}

(8.124)

\displaystyle=\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}-(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}|_{z=0}\big)\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{3}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big(\exp(\varepsilon^{2}\mu\bar{C}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u)-1\big)^{\Psi}|_{z=0}\partial_{t}\zeta_{3}.

Denote
K=K(\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})=\exp(-\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}),
we have by the definition (
7.1
) that

r_{0}^{-1}=R\beta(\theta)K(\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma}),
which gives rise to:

(8.125)

\displaystyle(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}-(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}|_{z=0}=R(\beta^{\Psi}-\beta^{\Psi}|_{z=0})K^{\Psi}+R\beta^{\Psi}|_{z=0}(K^{\Psi}-K^{\Psi}|_{z=0}).

It follows from the Taylor expansion and the fact

(\partial_{z}\theta^{\Psi})|_{z=0}=(\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta|_{\partial\Omega})^{\Psi}=0
that:

(8.126)

\displaystyle\beta^{\Psi}-\beta^{\Psi}|_{z=0}=z^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{z}^{2}(\beta^{\Psi})(t,y,\tau z)(1-\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,

\displaystyle K^{\Psi}-K^{\Psi}|_{z=0}=\varepsilon z\int_{0}^{1}\big((K^{\Psi})^{\prime}\partial_{z}\tilde{\sigma}^{\Psi}\big)(t,y,\tau z)\mathrm{d}\tau,

which, together with the estimate (
8.107
), yield:

(8.127)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\big((r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}-(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}|_{z=0}\big)\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{3}\|_{0,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\zeta_{3}\|_{2,t}+\|\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}\zeta_{3}\|_{1,t})\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\nabla(\theta,\tilde{\sigma}),\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}\theta|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, by again the Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle\exp(\varepsilon^{2}\mu\bar{C}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u)-1=\varepsilon^{2}\mu\bar{C}\int_{0}^{1}\exp(\tau\varepsilon^{2}\mu\bar{C}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u)\Gamma{\rm div\,}u\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

which, together with (
8.109
), enable us to control the second term in (
8.124
) as:

\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}\zeta_{3}\|_{0,t}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|({\rm div\,}u,\sigma)|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We thus finish the proof of (
8.123
) by collecting the above estimate and (
8.127
).
∎

Proposition 8.11
.

Let
\zeta_{4}
be the solution to the system (
8.101
). Under the same assumption as in Proposition
8.9
, it holds that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

any
0<t\leq T,

(8.128)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\zeta_{4}|\!|\!|_{*,1,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

(8.129)

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{4}(t)\|_{m-2}+\|\zeta_{4}\|_{m-1,t}+\|\partial_{t}\zeta_{4}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

We will only prove (
8.129
), since (
8.128
) can be shown by following the similar (and easier) arguments as in the proof of (
8.35
).
By
\eqref{eq-tiltasigma}_{2},
it holds that:

\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}-\frac{1}{R\beta}(\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)\textbf{n}\cdot u=-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})({\rm div\,}u)\partial_{\textbf{n}}\Gamma+\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}\text{ on }\partial\Omega.

Consequently, in light of the boundary condition:

\displaystyle\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}=\big[\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}r_{0}\chi\Pi u(\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}-\frac{1}{R\beta}(\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)\textbf{n}\cdot u)\big]^{\Psi}|_{z=0},

we have by using Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality in
\mathbb{R}^{2},
the estimates (
4.28
), (
4.29
) and the trace inequality (
15.20
) that:

\displaystyle|\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\big|\big(r_{0}\chi\Pi u(\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}-\frac{1}{R\beta}(\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)\textbf{n}\cdot u)\big)^{\Psi}\big|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}

(8.130)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\mu|(\chi r_{0},\chi\Gamma,u,{\rm div\,}u,\partial_{\textbf{n}}\Gamma,\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n})|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{m-\frac{3}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Accordingly, by noticing again the estimate (
4.29
), one can show that

(8.131)

|\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{m-2}}+|\partial_{t}\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{m-\frac{5}{2}}}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

The heat type equation (
8.101
) admits the explicit formula:

(8.132)

\displaystyle\zeta_{4}=2\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{1}{(4\pi\mu(t-t^{\prime}))^{\frac{3}{2}}}e^{-(t-t^{\prime})}\partial_{z}\big(e^{-\frac{z^{2}+|y-y^{\prime}|^{2}}{4\mu(t-t^{\prime})}}\big)\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}(t^{\prime},y^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}.

We can follow the arguments as in the proof of
(
8.113
) (by setting
b_{2}=1
) to obtain that:

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{4}(t)\|_{m-2}\lesssim(T\mu)^{\frac{1}{4}}\big|\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}\big|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon(T\mu)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

To control the quantity
\|\zeta_{4}\|_{m-1,t},
we need to take benefit of the horizontal smoothing effects. To do so, we first extend

\zeta_{4}
from
[0,T]
to
\mathbb{R}
by zero extension and then perform Fourier transform in both
t
and
y
variable. By denoting
\widehat{f}(\tau,\xi)={\mathcal{F}}_{t\rightarrow\tau,y\rightarrow\xi}(f),
it holds that:

\displaystyle\widehat{\zeta_{4}}(\tau,\xi,z)=e^{-z\sqrt{\mu(1+i\tau+|\xi|^{2}})}\widehat{\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}}(\tau,\xi).

Therefore, for any
0\leq k\leq m-1,

one has that:

\displaystyle\|(z\partial_{z})^{k}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{\frac{m-1-k}{2}}\widehat{\zeta_{4}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(1+|\xi|^{2})^{m-1-k}\widehat{\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}}(\tau,\xi)\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}\tau\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}P_{2k}\big(z\sqrt{\mu(1+i\tau+|\xi|^{2}})\big)e^{-z\sqrt{\mu(1+i\tau+|\xi|^{2}})}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{-\frac{1}{4}}\big|\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}\big|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{m-\frac{3}{2}-k}}

where
P_{2k}
is a polynomial with degree
2k.

We thus obtain by using (
8.2
) that

\|\zeta_{4}\|_{m-1,t}\lesssim|\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{m-\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\mu^{\frac{1}{4}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Finally, to control the quantity
\|\partial_{t}\zeta_{4}\|_{m-2,t},
we first take time derivative to the system and then follow the similar arguments as in the proof of
\|\zeta_{4}\|_{m-1,t}.
Doing so and using the estimate (
8.131
), we find that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\zeta_{4}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim|\partial_{t}\zeta_{4}|_{z=0}|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{m-\frac{5}{2}}}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

∎

Corollary 8.12
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{4}
be defined in (
8.105
), we have:

(8.133)

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{S}}_{4}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Proof.

It is just a matter to substitute
\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{4}
by

(\partial_{t}-1-\partial_{y^{1}}^{2}-\partial_{y^{2}}^{2})\zeta_{4}
in the expression of
{\mathcal{S}}_{4}
and to use the property (
8.129
).
∎

To finish the estimate for
\zeta,
it remains to control
\zeta_{5}
defined as the solution to the system (
8.102
). As a preparation, we begin with the following lemma:

Lemma 8.13
.

Let
\zeta_{5}
be the solution to the system (
8.102
)
and assume
\mu
and
\kappa
satisfy the relation
(
1.19
).
Then
\zeta_{5}
can be written as the sum of two functions:

\zeta_{5}=\zeta_{51}+\zeta_{52},
where
\zeta_{51}
and
\zeta_{52}
admit the properties:

(8.134)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{51}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\big(\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)\big),

(8.135)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\partial_{z}\zeta_{52}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim Y_{m}(0)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Remark 8.14
.

The estimates (
8.134
) (
8.135
) will be very useful later in order to close the energy estimates for
\zeta_{5}
.

Remark 8.15
.

Since
\zeta_{5}
solves a transport-diffusion equation (
8.102
) with the viscosity parameter
\mu,
one may expect
\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\zeta_{5}
to be uniformly bounded in
L_{t,x}^{\infty}
which is the case in the purely inviscid limit problem
[
41
,
49
,
58
]
.
Nevertheless, such an estimate is unlikely to be uniform with respect to the Mach number
\varepsilon.
Indeed, incorporated with the fast oscillation effects, the width of
the viscous boundary layer is expected to be
(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}},
which means that the best one can hope is
|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\zeta_{5}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}
being uniformly bounded, which is not useful here. Moreover, since the source term
{\mathcal{S}}_{5}
involve the high order derivatives of unknown
r_{0}=r_{0}(\theta,\tilde{\sigma}),
there will be some interactions between the thermal boundary layer and the viscous boundary layer. Consequently, we are forced to make some suitable assumption (see (
1.19
)) on
\kappa
and
\mu.

Let us comment that all the estimates established previously hold indeed for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in(0,1]^{2}
satisfying
\mu\sim\kappa.
The more restrictive assumption (
1.19
) on
\kappa
and
\mu
is made here to prove (
8.134
).

Proof.

We define
\zeta_{51}
and
\zeta_{52}
to be the solutions of the following two systems:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\big(\partial_{t}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}b_{0}\partial_{z}^{2}\big)\zeta_{51}={\mathcal{M}}_{1},\\
\zeta_{51}|_{z=0}=0,\quad\zeta_{51}|_{t=0}=0,\end{array}\right.\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}b_{0}\partial_{z}^{2})\zeta_{52}={\mathcal{M}}_{2},\\
\zeta_{52}|_{z=0}=0,\quad\zeta_{52}|_{t=0}=\zeta|_{t=0},\end{array}\right.

where
{\mathcal{M}}_{1},{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
are defined as:

(8.140)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\colon{\mathcal{S}}_{3}+{\mathcal{S}}_{4}+\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}}_{5}+\big(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits{\mathcal{Q}}\times\chi\textbf{n}\big)^{\Psi}

\displaystyle-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\bigg(\big(\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\omega_{r_{0}}\big)\times\chi\textbf{n}+2r_{0}^{-1}{\rm div\,}\big(\omega_{r_{0}}\times\nabla(\chi\textbf{n})\big)\bigg)^{\Psi},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\colon{\mathcal{S}}_{5}-{\mathcal{M}}_{1}-\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla\zeta_{5},

with
{\mathcal{S}}_{3},{\mathcal{S}}_{4}
being defined in (
8.103
)-(
8.105
)
and

\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}}_{5}=\colon\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\bigg(\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}\Delta_{g}\zeta_{5}+\big(\big(\frac{1}{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi}-b_{0}\big)\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta_{5}\bigg),

(8.141)

{\mathcal{Q}}=u\bigg((\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}+\frac{\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}\big(\exp\big({\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}}\big)\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}\big)\Delta\theta\bigg)+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u.

Let us prove (
8.134
), (
8.135
) and start with the estimate for
\zeta_{51}.

First,
corresponding to (
8.89
), we can find that the last two terms of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
can be written as the
gradient of some quantities

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{1}=\colon\partial_{y^{1}}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}+\partial_{y^{2}}{\mathcal{H}}_{2}+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{H}}_{3}

where
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}-{\mathcal{H}}_{3}
are linear combinations of the following typical terms (with the coefficients depending on
\chi,(D\Psi)^{-1}
and their first two derivatives):

(8.142)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}^{\Psi},\quad\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\chi\partial_{\textbf{n}}(r_{0}^{-1})\omega_{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi},\qquad 2\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(r_{0}^{-1}\omega_{r_{0}}\times\nabla(\chi\textbf{n})\big)^{\Psi},

plus a reminder
{\mathcal{H}}_{4}
which satisfy:

(8.143)

\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{H}}_{4}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},\quad\|{\mathcal{H}}_{4}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Now, taking the inner product with the system
(
8.2
) and
\mu^{-1}\zeta_{51},
we find,

(8.144)

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\zeta_{51}(t)\|_{0}^{2}+\iota c_{0}\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{51}\|_{0,t}^{2}\leq\big|\mu^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{51}\cdot{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\zeta_{51}\|_{0,t}\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathcal{S}}_{3},{\mathcal{S}}_{4},\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}}_{5},\partial_{y^{1}}{\mathcal{H}}_{1},\partial_{y^{2}}{\mathcal{H}}_{2},{\mathcal{H}}_{4})\|_{0,t}+\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{51}\|_{0,t}\|\mu^{-1}{\mathcal{H}}_{3}\|_{0,t}.

Analogues to (
8.123
), we can verify that,
by using (
8.124
)-(
8.126
),

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}}_{5}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\zeta_{5}\|_{2,t}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}\|_{1,t}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, combined with the estimates (
8.123
), (
8.133
), (
8.143
), (
8.144
) and Young’s inequality, gives rise to:

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}\|\zeta_{51}(t)\|_{0}^{2}+\iota c_{0}\|\nabla\zeta_{51}\|_{0,t}^{2}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\big(\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)\big)+\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathcal{H}}_{1},{\mathcal{H}}_{2})\|_{1,t}^{2}+\|\mu^{-1}{\mathcal{H}}_{3}\|_{0,t}^{2}.

The proof of (
8.134
) shall thus be finished once it is shown that:

(8.145)

\displaystyle\|\mu^{-1}{\mathcal{H}}_{3}\|_{0,t}+\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}({\mathcal{H}}_{1},{\mathcal{H}}_{2},{\mathcal{H}}_{3})\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which relies on the control
of three terms in (
8.142
). Note that the estimate of
\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{H}}_{3}\|_{m-2,t}
is not necessary here but rather useful later.
Let us estimate the last two terms in (
8.142
) as:

\displaystyle\|\big(\chi\partial_{\textbf{n}}(r_{0}^{-1})\omega_{r_{0}}\big)^{\Psi},\big(r_{0}^{-1}\omega_{r_{0}}\times\nabla(\chi\textbf{n})\big)^{\Psi}\|_{m-2,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\omega_{r_{0}},\nabla r_{0}^{-1})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

(8.146)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

It now remains to control
\|\mu^{-1}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\Psi}\|_{0,t}
which is the very place where the relation (
1.19
) is used. Let us look at the terms in (
8.141
) more carefully.
By the straightforward calculation,

\displaystyle[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u=-(\Delta{r_{0}})u+\big({\rm div\,}u\nabla r_{0}-(\nabla r_{0}\cdot\nabla)u+(u\cdot\nabla)\nabla r_{0}+\nabla r_{0}\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\big).

Moreover, by the definition (
7.1
),

-r_{0}^{-1}\Delta{r_{0}}=\Delta\theta-\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\Delta\tilde{\sigma}+\big|\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}-\nabla\theta\big|^{2},

which, together with the above identity, yields:

(8.147)

\displaystyle\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(\Delta\theta)u+l.o.t,

where hereafter we denote
l.o.t
for the terms that admit the following property:

(8.148)

\displaystyle\|\mu^{-1}(l.o.t)^{\Psi}\|_{0,t}+\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(l.o.t)^{\Psi}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Next, by the equation (
7.15
), and the
fact
R\beta r_{0}=\exp(\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}),

(8.149)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}=-\frac{\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}\exp\big(\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}\big)\Gamma\Delta\theta+l.o.t,

we thus obtained

(8.150)

\displaystyle u\bigg((\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}+\frac{\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}\big(\exp\big({\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}}\big)\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}\big)\Delta\theta\bigg)=-\frac{\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}\overline{\Gamma}u\Delta\theta+l.o.t,

which, combined with (
8.147
), leads to that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}=\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\mu-\frac{\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma\lambda_{1}}\big)u\Delta\theta+l.o.t.

Consequently, by the virtue of the fact:

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}(\Omega)}+\kappa\|\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

once the relation (
1.19
) holds, we can conclude that:

(8.151)

\displaystyle\mu^{-1}\|{\mathcal{Q}}^{\Psi}\|_{0,t}+\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{Q}}^{\Psi}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Collecting (
8.2
) and (
8.151
), one finds the estimate (
8.145
) and thus finishes the proof of the first estimate in (
8.134
).

Let us now switch to the estimate of
\zeta_{52}
stated in (
8.135
).
Defined as the solution of (
8.2
),
\zeta_{52}
has the following form:

(8.152)

\displaystyle\zeta_{52}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(K_{-}-K_{+})(t,0,y,z^{\prime},z)\zeta_{52}|_{t=0}(y,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(K_{-}-K_{+})(t,t^{\prime},y,z^{\prime},z){\mathcal{M}}_{2}(t^{\prime},y,z^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}=\colon\zeta_{521}+\zeta_{522}

where

\displaystyle K_{\pm}(t,t^{\prime},y,z^{\prime},z)=\frac{1}{(4\pi Y)^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\frac{|z\pm z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4Y}},\quad Y=Y(t,t^{\prime},y)=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}b_{0}(\tau,y)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

First, we integrate by parts to obtain that

(8.153)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\partial_{z}\zeta_{521}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}\zeta|_{t=0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lesssim Y_{m}(0).

Moreover, by applying the convolution inequality in
z
variable and the estimate (
15.34
), we find that

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\partial_{z}\zeta_{522}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}(K_{-}-K_{+})\|_{L_{y}^{\infty}L_{z}^{1}}(t,t^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}|\!|\!|{\mathcal{M}}_{2}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|{\mathcal{M}}_{2}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}.

By the definitions (
8.140
) and (
8.103
)

(8.154)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{2}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{S}}_{5}-{\mathcal{M}}_{1}-u\cdot\nabla\zeta_{5}=-(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla)\zeta+\bigg(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\big(\frac{\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}u\big(\exp\big({\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}}\big)\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}\big)\Delta\theta\big)\times\chi\textbf{n}\bigg)^{\Psi}

\displaystyle+|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\bigg(H^{0}+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\big(\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\omega_{r_{0}}\big)\times\chi\textbf{n}+2r_{0}^{-1}{\rm div\,}(\omega_{r_{0}}\times\nabla(\chi\textbf{n}))\big)\bigg)^{\Psi}+H^{0,{\dagger}}.

We have first by the definition of
\zeta,

|\!|\!|(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla)\zeta|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\big(u,\nabla(r_{0}u)\big)|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Next, by using the equation
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{3}
for
\theta,

it can be verified that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\kappa\|\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

We thus find that the
|\!|\!|\cdot|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}
norm of the first line in the right hand side of (
8.154
) can be controlled as
\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, in view of the definition of
H^{0}
in (
8.10
),
and
H^{0,{\dagger}}
in (
8.28
),
we find that the last line in (
8.154
) contains the terms which are the smooth functions of quantities:

\displaystyle\zeta,(\omega_{r_{0}})^{\Psi},\,(({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)u)^{\Psi},\,(\nabla(r_{0}u))^{\Psi},\,\big(({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)r_{1}\Gamma\big)^{\Psi},\,\varepsilon^{-1}\big(({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)r_{1}(\Gamma-\bar{\Gamma})\big)^{\Psi},\,\varepsilon\mu\big(({\rm Id}\,,\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits)\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\big)^{\Psi},\,

whose
L^{\infty}([0,t]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})
norm, by using the estimate (
8.97
) for the last quantity, can be controlled by
{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}.

The above arguments enable us to conclude that

|\!|\!|{\mathcal{M}}_{2}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),
and thus

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\zeta_{522}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with (
8.153
), yields the second estimate in (
8.134
).
∎

Proposition 8.16
.

Let
\zeta_{5}
be the solution to the system (
8.102
). There exists a constant
\vartheta_{5}\in(0,\frac{1}{2}],
such that
for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],
any
(\mu,\kappa)\in(0,1]^{2}
satisfying the relation (
1.19
),
any
0<t\leq T,

(8.155)

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{5}(t)\|_{m-2}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{5}\|_{m-2,t}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{5}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Remark 8.17
.

This property can be shown by performing energy estimates for
\zeta_{5},
however, such estimates relies
crucially on the Lemma
8.13
when dealing with some commutator terms.

Proof.

Taking a multi-index
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})
and applying
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}
on the equations (
8.102
), we find that
\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}=\colon{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\zeta_{5}
solves the following equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla)\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\partial_{z}({\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0})+\partial_{y^{k}}(({g^{kl}}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0})\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta_{5}^{\gamma})\big)={\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{S}}_{5}+\sum_{i=1}^{5}{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{\gamma},\\
\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}|_{z=0}=0,\quad\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}|_{t=0}={\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}(\zeta|_{t=0}),\end{array}\right.

where
{\mathcal{S}}_{5}
is defined in (
8.103
) and

(8.158)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle=-[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla]\zeta_{5},\quad{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{\gamma}=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\big(\partial_{z}^{2}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{5}\big),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{3}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle=-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}([{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\partial_{z}]+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma})\big(\partial_{z}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{5}\big),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{4}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big([{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\partial_{z}](\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0})+\partial_{z}([{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},1/r^{\Psi}_{0}]\partial_{z}\zeta_{5})\big),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\bigg(\partial_{y^{k}}\big([{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},{g^{kl}}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0}]\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta_{5}\big)-{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\big(\partial_{y^{k}}({|g|^{-\frac{1}{2}}}/{r}^{\Psi}_{0})g^{kl}|g|^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta_{5}\big)\bigg).

Before going into details, we summarize some useful properties for
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{\gamma}-{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{\gamma}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{S}}_{5}.

We first gather some estimates for
{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{\gamma}:

Lemma 8.18
.

Let
{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{\gamma}(i=1,\cdots 5)
be defined in (
8.158
). Suppose that
|\gamma|=m-2,
we have under the assumption (
1.34
)
the following estimates: for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in(0,1]^{2}
satisfying
\mu\sim\kappa
, any
0<t\leq T,

(8.159)

\displaystyle\|({\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{\gamma},{\mathcal{R}}_{2}^{\gamma})\|_{0,t}+\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}(1/r_{0}^{\Psi})\zeta_{5})\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

(8.160)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{\gamma}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{5}\|_{m-2,t}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, if
\mu
and
\kappa
satisfy the relation
(
1.19
), then it holds that:

(8.161)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}/r_{0}^{\Psi}\|_{m-3,t}+\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},1/r_{0}^{\Psi}]\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}\|_{0,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}\|_{m-3,t}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)+(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\big(\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)\big).

Remark 8.19
.

We remark that the estimate (
8.161
) can be derived with the help of the properties (
8.134
) and (
8.135
) in Lemma
8.13
, which is the place where
the relation (
1.19
) is used.

The following lemma states some estimates for
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{S}}_{5},|\gamma|=m-2:

Lemma 8.20
.

Suppose that
|\gamma|=m-2
and (
1.34
) is satisfied.
For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in(0,1]^{2}
satisfying the relation (
1.19
), any
0<t\leq T,
the following aspects hold:

(i) It holds that

(8.162)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla(\zeta_{3}+\zeta_{4})\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

(ii) Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{3},{\mathcal{S}}_{4}
be defined in (
8.104
), (
8.105
) respectively, then we have:

(8.163)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{3}+{\mathcal{S}}_{4}=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\partial_{y^{1}}{\mathcal{S}}_{341}+\partial_{y^{2}}{\mathcal{S}}_{342}+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{S}}_{343}\big)+{\mathcal{S}}_{344}

where
{\mathcal{S}}_{341}-{\mathcal{S}}_{344}
are some functions that admit
the properties:

(8.164)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{S}}_{34j}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{4}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},\quad\|{\mathcal{S}}_{344}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

(iii) There exist
{\mathcal{H}}_{51}-{\mathcal{H}}_{54},
such that:

(8.165)

\displaystyle|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}(H_{1}^{0})^{\Psi}+H^{0,{\dagger}}=\partial_{y^{1}}{\mathcal{H}}_{51}+\partial_{y^{2}}{\mathcal{H}}_{52}+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{H}}_{53}+{\mathcal{H}}_{54}

and

(8.166)

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|({\mathcal{H}}_{51},{\mathcal{H}}_{52},{\mathcal{H}}_{53})\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),\quad\|{\mathcal{H}}_{53}\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We will postpone the proof of the above two lemmas until the end of this section and proceed to prove (
8.155
).

Analogue to (
8.1
),
we carry out direct energy estimates for (
8.2
)
to obtain:

(8.167)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}|\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}|^{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x+\mu\iota c_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}|\nabla\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}|\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}|^{2}(0)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}2\mu[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\partial_{z}]\zeta_{5}\cdot\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}/r^{\Psi}_{0}+|\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}|^{2}{\rm div\,}\tilde{u}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\bigg|}_{\eqref{EI-zeta4}_{1}}+\underbrace{\bigg|2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}\cdot\big({\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{S}}_{5}+\sum_{i=1}^{5}{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{\gamma}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\bigg|}_{\eqref{EI-zeta4}_{2}}

where
\iota>0
is a universe constant.

First of all, corresponding to (
8.56
), we have by using (
8.25
), (
8.107
) and (
8.155
) that, for any
0\leq k\leq m-2,

(8.168)

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{5}\|_{k,t}\lesssim\|(\zeta,\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4})\|_{k,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|(\zeta,\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4})(s)\|_{k}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

This, together with the identity (
8.54
) and Young’s inequality, enables us to control
the term
\eqref{EI-zeta4}_{1}
as

(8.169)

\displaystyle\eqref{EI-zeta4}_{1}\leq\delta\mu c_{0}\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}\|_{0,t}^{2}+C_{\delta}\mu\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}\|_{m-3,t}^{2}+T\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Let us now focus on the estimate of
\eqref{EI-zeta4}_{2}.

By the virtue of favorable estimates established in Lemma
8.18
,
the integral involving the terms
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{\gamma}-{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{\gamma}
can be treated in the same way as we did in (
8.58
)-(
8.61
). More precisely, by using the estimate (
8.159
) for
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{\gamma}-{\mathcal{R}}_{3}^{\gamma},
(
8.161
) for
{\mathcal{R}}_{4}^{\gamma},
(
8.160
) for
{\mathcal{R}}_{5}^{\gamma},

we can obtain after suitable integration by parts
the following estimate corresponding to
(
8.1
):

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{5}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{R}}_{i}^{\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\delta c_{0}\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{5}\|_{k,t}^{2}+(T+\varepsilon)\big(\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)\big)

(8.170)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\delta}\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{5}\|_{k-1,t}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}^{2}\big).

Let us now control the term
\big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{S}}_{5}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\big|.

Thanks to the properties for the source term
{\mathcal{S}}_{5}
listed in Lemma
8.20
, we get after suitable integration by parts that:

(8.171)

\displaystyle\big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}\zeta_{5}^{\gamma}\cdot{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}{\mathcal{S}}_{5}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\zeta_{5}\|_{0,t}\sum_{l=1}^{3}\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|(\mu{\mathcal{S}}_{34l},{\mathcal{H}}_{5l})\|_{m-2,t}

\displaystyle+\|\zeta_{5}\|_{m-2,t}\|\big(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla(\zeta_{3}+\zeta_{4}),{\mathcal{S}}_{344},{\mathcal{H}}_{54}\big)\|_{m-2,t}

\displaystyle\leq\delta c_{0}\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{5}\|_{k,t}^{2}+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}^{2}.

Inserting (
8.169
)-(
8.171
) into
(
8.167
), we find, by summing up for
|\gamma|=k
and by choosing
\delta
small enough,

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{5}\|_{k,t}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{5}\|_{k,t}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}\|_{k-1,t}^{2}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big).

Consequently, by noticing (
4.42
), (
8.68
),
we can do induction on
k
to find that, there exists a constant
\vartheta_{5}\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
such that:

\|\zeta_{5}\|_{m-2,t}^{2}+\mu\|\nabla\zeta_{5}\|_{m-2,t}^{2}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{5}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

∎

Proof of Lemma
8.18
.

The estimates (
8.159
) (
8.160
) follow from the product estimate (
15.16
) and the commutator estimate (
15.17
). Let us show for instance the estimate for
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{\gamma}=-[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla]\zeta_{4}.
In view of the identity (
8.73
), we can apply the commutator estimate (
15.17
) to obtain that:

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{\gamma}\|_{0,t}=\|[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla]\zeta_{5}\|_{0,t}\lesssim\|(\tilde{u}_{1},\tilde{u}_{2},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)\|_{m-2,t}|\!|\!|\zeta_{5}|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}+\|\zeta_{5}\|_{m-2,t}|\!|\!|(\tilde{u}_{1},\tilde{u}_{2},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}.

Note that by (
8.106
), (
8.128
), one has:

|\!|\!|\zeta_{5}|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|(\zeta,\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4})|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with (
8.168
) (
8.75
) and the fact

\|(\tilde{u},\nabla\tilde{u})\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

gives rise to:

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}_{1}^{\gamma}\|_{0,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

We now prove (
8.161
), which relies crucially on (
8.134
).
By using (
8.78
), we can have directly:

(8.172)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}/r_{0}^{\Psi}\|_{m-3,t}+\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},1/r_{0}^{\Psi}]\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}-{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}(1/r_{0}^{\Psi})\partial_{z}\zeta_{4}\|_{0,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}\|_{m-3,t}|\!|\!|1/r_{0}^{\Psi}|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}\|_{m-3,t}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big).

It now remains to control
\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}(1/r_{0}^{\Psi})\partial_{z}\zeta_{4}\|_{0,t}
which is the most problematic one. As a matter of fact, in general, the term
