\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}(s)
cannot be uniformly bounded in
L^{P}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})
for
2<p<+\infty,
one is forced to attribute the
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})
norm on
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}(1/r_{0}^{\Psi}),
which is not uniformly (in
\mu
) bounded. To get around this difficulty, we assume the relation (
1.19
) holds and take benefit of (
8.134
)-(
8.135
) to obtain that:

(8.173)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}(1/r_{0}^{\Psi})\partial_{z}\zeta_{5}\|_{0,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}(1/r_{0}^{\Psi})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}|\!|\!|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}\zeta_{51}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}/r_{0}^{\Psi}|\!|\!|_{m-2,\infty,t}\|\partial_{z}\zeta_{52}\|_{0,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\big(\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)\big).

Note that by the definition (
7.1
) for
r_{0}

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}/r_{0}^{\Psi}|\!|\!|_{m-2,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{Z}})(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

We thus finish the proof of (
8.161
) by collecting (
8.172
) and (
8.173
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.20
.

We begin with the proof of the first aspect, namely the estimate (
8.162
).
It follows from the identity (
8.83
),
the commutator estimate (
15.16
) that:

\displaystyle\|(\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla)(\zeta_{3}+\zeta_{4})\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim\|(\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4})\|_{m-1,t}|\!|\!|(\tilde{u},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}+\|(\tilde{u},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)\|_{m-2,t}|\!|\!|(\zeta_{3},\zeta_{4})|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t},

which, combined with estimates (
8.106
), (
8.107
) for
\zeta_{3}
, (
8.128
), (
8.129
) for
\zeta_{4}
and the fact
\|(\tilde{u},\tilde{u}_{3}/\phi)\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(u,\nabla u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}},
yields (
8.162
).

Let us now sketch the second aspect concerning the fact
(
8.163
) which can be shown by following the similar arguments
as in the proof of (
8.80
). In view of the definitions (
8.104
), (
8.105
) for
{\mathcal{S}}_{3},{\mathcal{S}}_{4}
and the identity
(
8.124
), let us set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{34k}=(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\big(g^{kl}\partial_{y^{l}}(\zeta_{3}+\zeta_{4})-\partial_{y^{k}}\zeta_{4}\big),\,k=1,2,\quad

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{343}=\big((1/r^{\Psi}_{0})-(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})|_{z=0}\big)\partial_{z}\zeta_{3}-\partial_{z}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{3}+b_{2}\big(\exp(\varepsilon^{2}\mu\bar{C}\Gamma{\rm div\,}u)-1\big)^{\Psi}|_{z=0}\partial_{z}\zeta_{3},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{344}=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\bigg(-\partial_{y^{k}}(|g|^{-\frac{1}{2}}/r^{\Psi}_{0})g^{kl}|g|^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y^{l}}(\zeta_{3}+\zeta_{4})+\partial_{y^{k}}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\partial_{y^{k}}\zeta_{4}+\partial_{z}^{2}(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})\zeta_{3}+(1/r^{\Psi}_{0})(\partial_{t}\zeta_{4}-\zeta_{4})\bigg)-\partial_{t}\zeta_{4}.

The desired estimates listed in (
8.164
) are
the consequences of the product estimate (
15.16
),
Lemma
8.9
and Lemma
8.11
for

\zeta_{3}
and
\zeta_{4},
we omit the details.

Finally, for the third aspect, we check the terms appearing in the definitions (
8.10
), (
8.28
) for
(H^{0})^{\Psi}
and
H^{0,{\dagger}}
to find that:

(8.174)

\displaystyle|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}(H^{0})^{\Psi}+H^{0,{\dagger}}=l.o.t+{\mathcal{M}}_{1}-({\mathcal{S}}_{3}+{\mathcal{S}}_{4})

\displaystyle+\bigg(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\bigg(\frac{\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}u\big(\exp\big({\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}}\big)\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}\big)\Delta\theta+\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}\big(\big(r_{1}\Gamma+\varepsilon^{-1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\big)\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\big)\bigg)\times\chi\textbf{n}\bigg)^{\Psi}

where
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
is defined in (
8.140
)
and
l.o.t
stands for the terms that admit the property:

\displaystyle\|l.o.t\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

However, in the proof of Lemma
8.13
,
we found that there exists
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}-{\mathcal{H}}_{3}
such that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{1}-({\mathcal{S}}_{3}+{\mathcal{S}}_{4})=\partial_{y^{1}}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}+\partial_{y^{2}}{\mathcal{H}}_{2}+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{H}}_{3}+l.o.t.

Moreover, if the relation (
1.19
) holds, then (see (
8.145
)):

(8.175)

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|({\mathcal{H}}_{1},{\mathcal{H}}_{2},{\mathcal{H}}_{3})\|_{m-2,t}\lesssim(T^{\frac{1}{2}}+\varepsilon)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

By using the identity (
8.85
), we can show again that
there exists
{\mathcal{H}}_{5}-{\mathcal{H}}_{7}
, such that the last term of
(
8.174
)
can be rewritten in the following form:

\displaystyle\partial_{y^{1}}{\mathcal{H}}_{5}+\partial_{y^{2}}{\mathcal{H}}_{6}+\partial_{z}{\mathcal{H}}_{7}+l.o.t.,

where
{\mathcal{H}}_{5}-{\mathcal{H}}_{7}
are smooth functions with respect to the quantities:

\bigg(\kappa u\big(\exp\big({\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}}\big)\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}\big)\Delta\theta\big)\bigg)^{\Psi},\quad\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}\bigg(\big(r_{1}\Gamma+\varepsilon^{-1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\big)\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\bigg)^{\Psi}

we thus can conclude from the product estimate (
15.16
) that:

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{H}}_{5},{\mathcal{H}}_{6},{\mathcal{H}}_{7}\|_{m-2,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\varepsilon\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{3}}\|(\varepsilon^{\frac{2}{3}}\mu^{\frac{1}{2}})\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\sigma,\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

This, together with (
8.175
), enables us to
finish the proof of (
8.165
) and (
8.166
) by setting
{\mathcal{H}}_{51}={\mathcal{H}}_{1}+{\mathcal{H}}_{5},{\mathcal{H}}_{52}={\mathcal{H}}_{2}+{\mathcal{H}}_{6},{\mathcal{H}}_{53}={\mathcal{H}}_{3}+{\mathcal{H}}_{7}.

∎

9.
L_{t,x}^{\infty}
estimates for
\theta

In this section, we aim to bound the several
L_{t,x}^{\infty}
norms for
\theta,
which will be useful to recover higher order
L_{t}^{\infty}
type conormal regularities of
(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)
in the next section.

Lemma 9.1
.

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{m,t}(\theta):

(9.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{m,t}(\theta)

\displaystyle=|\!|\!|(\theta,\kappa\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\nabla\theta,\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla^{2}\theta|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}.

Under the assumption (
1.34
), there exists a constant
\vartheta_{6}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,
the following estimate holds:

(9.2)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}(\theta)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

First of all, it follows from the inequality (
4.42
) and the estimates (
4.5
), (
4.34
) that:

(9.3)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Similarly, by the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
) and the estimates (
2.27
), (
4.35
),

(9.4)

\displaystyle\kappa|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\kappa\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

It thus remains to control the last three terms in (
9.1
). We note however that away from the boundaries where the conormal Sobolev norm is equivalent to the usual Sobolev norm, these three terms can be bounded by the standard Sobolev embedding. Therefore, it suffices to control

(9.5)

|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},\quad\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}\theta|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t},\quad\kappa|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},

where
\chi_{i},(1\leq i\leq N)
are smooth functions compactly supported in
\Omega_{i}.

Let us start with the derivation of
\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta.
The equation
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{3}
together with
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{1},
allow us to rewrite the equation satisfied by
\theta:

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\theta-\frac{R\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}(\Gamma\beta)\Delta\theta=\frac{R}{C_{v}\gamma}\big(\varepsilon(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\sigma+\varepsilon\mathfrak{N}+\kappa\Gamma\nabla\beta\cdot\nabla\theta\big)=\colon\mathfrak{M}_{1}^{i}

where
\mathfrak{N}
is defined in (
1.18
).
Taking the gradient of the above equation and multiplying the resultant by
\chi_{i}\textbf{n},
we find that

\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta
solves the system:

(9.6)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+w\cdot\nabla)(\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta)-\frac{R\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}(\Gamma\beta)\Delta(\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta)=\mathfrak{M}_{2}^{i},\quad\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta|_{\partial\Omega}=0

where
w=u-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\beta\nabla\theta
and

(9.7)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{2}^{i}

\displaystyle=\big(\nabla\mathfrak{M}_{1}^{i}+\frac{R\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}\nabla(\Gamma\beta)\Delta\theta-\nabla u\cdot\nabla\theta\big)\cdot\chi_{i}\textbf{n}

\displaystyle+u\cdot\nabla(\chi_{i}\textbf{n})\cdot\nabla\theta+\frac{R\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}\Gamma\beta[\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}},\Delta]\theta-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\beta\nabla\theta\cdot\nabla(\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta).

The reason why we change the transport term
u\cdot\nabla
to
w\cdot\nabla
will be clear later.

The estimates of the three quantities in (
9.5
) will rely on the careful studies on the transport-diffusion equation (
9.6
). Such an equation, when transformed in half space by using the normal geodesic coordinates (
8.12
), has the similar form as (
15.40
), whose solution admits the ’explicit’ expression (
15.46
).
Denote
\eta_{i}=\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta\circ\Psi_{i},\,f^{\Psi_{i}}=f\circ\Psi_{i},
where

\Psi_{i}
is the normal geodesic coordinates defined in (
8.12
). By using (
8.13
)-(
8.15
),
\eta_{i}
solves:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{w}_{i}\cdot\nabla)\eta_{i}-\frac{R\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi_{i}}\partial_{z}^{2}\eta_{i}=\frac{R\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}\big({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi_{i}}(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\ln|g_{i}|\partial_{z}+\Delta_{g_{i}}\big)\eta_{i}+({\mathfrak{M}_{2}^{i}})^{\Psi_{i}},\\[3.0pt]
\eta_{i}|_{t=0}=\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta_{0}\circ\Psi_{i},\quad\eta_{i}|_{z=0}=0,\end{array}\right.

where
\nabla=(\partial_{y^{1}},\partial_{y^{2}},\partial_{y^{3}})
and
{\rm div\,}=(\nabla)^{*}

stand for the gradient and the divergence in the new coordinates and
\Delta_{g_{i}},
defined in (
8.16
), involves only tangential derivatives.
Moreover,
\tilde{w}_{i}
is defined as:

(9.10)

\tilde{w}_{i}=(\tilde{w}_{i,1},\tilde{w}_{i,2},\tilde{w}_{i,3})=(D\Psi_{i}|_{z=0})^{-*}(\chi_{i}w)^{\Psi_{i}}.

Note that on the boundary
z=0,

(9.11)

\tilde{w}_{i,3}|_{z=0}=\big[(D\Psi_{i}|_{z=0})^{-*}(\chi_{i}w)^{\Psi_{i}}\big]_{3}\big|_{z=0}=-((\chi_{i}w\cdot\textbf{N}^{i})|_{\partial\Omega})^{\Psi_{i}}=0.

In order to apply Lemma
15.9
in the appendix, let us rewrite the system (
9
) as:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{w}_{i,1}^{b}\partial_{y^{1}}+\tilde{w}_{i,2}^{b}\partial_{y^{2}}+z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{i,3})^{b}\partial_{z})\eta_{i}-\frac{R\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}[({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi_{i}}]^{b}\partial_{z}^{2}\eta_{i}={\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi_{i}}_{3},\\[3.0pt]
\eta_{i}|_{t=0}=\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta_{0}\circ\Psi_{i},\quad\eta_{i}|_{z=0}=0,\end{array}\right.

where we denote
f^{b}=f|_{z=0}
and
{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi_{i}}_{3}={\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi_{i}}_{3,1}+{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi_{i}}_{3,2}+{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi_{i}}_{3,3}+{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi_{i}}_{3,4}
with

(9.14)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi_{i}}_{3,1}=\frac{R\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi_{i}}\big(\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{z}\ln|g_{i}|)\partial_{z}+\Delta_{g_{i}}\big)\eta_{i},\quad{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi_{i}}_{3,2}=({\mathfrak{M}_{2}^{i}})^{\Psi_{i}},\,({\mathfrak{M}_{2}^{i}}\textnormal{ is defined in }\eqref{defm2})

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi_{i}}_{3,3}=-\sum_{l=1}^{2}(\tilde{w}_{i,l}-\tilde{w}_{i,l}^{b})\partial_{y^{l}}\eta_{i}-\big(\tilde{w}_{i,3}-\tilde{w}_{i,3}^{b}-z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{i,3})^{b}\big)\partial_{z}\eta_{i},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi_{i}}_{3,4}=\frac{R\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}\bigg(({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi_{i}}-[({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi_{i}}]^{b}\bigg)\partial_{z}^{2}\eta_{i}.

From now on, we will drop the subscript
i
for the sake of notational convenience.
Applying the properties (
15.41
), (
15.44
) in Lemma
15.9
by setting
a_{1}=\tilde{w}_{1}^{b},\,a_{2}=\tilde{w}_{2}^{b},\,a_{3}=(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b},\,b=[({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi}]^{b},\,\nu=\frac{R\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma},
we find that:

(9.15)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\eta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda_{1}^{m-5}\|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta(0)\|_{m-5,\infty}

\displaystyle+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda_{1}^{m-5}\bigg(\Lambda_{2}^{m-5}|\!|\!|\eta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3}\|_{m-5,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg),

\displaystyle\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\partial_{z}\eta|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda_{1}^{m-6}\|\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{m-6,\infty}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda_{1}^{m-6}\big(\Lambda_{2}^{m-6}|\!|\!|\eta|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3}|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}\big),

\displaystyle\kappa|\!|\!|\partial_{z}\eta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\kappa\Lambda_{1}^{m-5}\|\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{m-5,\infty}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda_{1}^{m-5}\Lambda_{2}^{m-5}|\!|\!|(\eta,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},

where we denote

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}^{k}=\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|T(\tilde{w}^{b},(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}),[({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi}]^{b}|_{k,\infty,t}\big),\quad\Lambda_{2}^{k}=\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|(\tilde{w}^{b},(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}),\partial_{t}[({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi}]^{b}|_{k,\infty,t}\big).

We derive from the definition of
\tilde{w}
in (
9.10
) that:

(9.16)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}^{m-5}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|T(w,\nabla w),\Gamma\beta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+T|\!|\!|u,\nabla u,\kappa(\nabla\theta,\nabla^{2}\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2}^{m-5}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\varepsilon\partial_{t},{\rm Id}\,)(\sigma,\theta),u,\nabla u,\kappa(\nabla\theta,\nabla^{2}\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

By using the inequality (
4.42
) and the fact that
\Lambda
is a polynomial with respect to its arguments, we can find a constant
\vartheta_{7}>0,
such that

(9.17)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}^{m-5}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{\vartheta_{7}}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Therefore, it holds that:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}^{m-5}\big(\|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta(0)\|_{m-5,\infty}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{m-6,\infty}+\kappa\|\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{m-5,\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{7}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

In view of (
9.15
)
and the fact
|\!|\!|\eta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),
it suffices for us to control the three terms involving
{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3}
which is done in the next lemma.
Inserting (
9.18
) into the above three inequalities and changing back to the original variable,
we achieve that, by defining
\vartheta_{6}=\min\{\vartheta_{7},\frac{1}{2},\frac{\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}}{2}\},

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,T}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}\theta|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,T}+\kappa|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,T}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{6}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with (
9.3
), (
9.4
), leads to (
9.1
).
∎

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3}={\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,1}+{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,2}+{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,3}+{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,4}
be defined in (
9.14
), the following estimates hold:

(9.18)

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3}\|_{m-5,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\!|\!|{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3}|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

First, in view of the very definitions of
{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,1},{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,2}
in (
9.14
), it is direct to verify that:

(9.19)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,1},{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,2}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},

\displaystyle\kappa|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}

\displaystyle+|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\sigma,u,\theta),\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta,\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

We now focus on the control of

{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,3},{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,4}.
For the term
{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,3},
we bound
(\tilde{w}_{l}-\tilde{w}_{l}^{b})\partial_{y^{l}}\eta,\,l=1,2,
as:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|(\tilde{w}_{l}-\tilde{w}_{l}^{b})\partial_{y^{l}}\eta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\!|\!|z^{-1}(\tilde{w}_{l}-\tilde{w}_{l}^{b})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}|\!|\!|z\eta|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\!|\!|\nabla w|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}.

Note that the last inequality results from the fundamental theorem of calculus and the definitions of
\tilde{w}
in (
9.10
),
as well as the fact
\eta=\chi^{\Psi}\partial_{z}\theta^{\Psi}.
Since by the definition of

{w}
in (
3.1
),

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\nabla w|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\nabla u,\theta,\nabla\theta,\kappa\Delta\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big),

we find that:

(9.20)

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{2}|\!|\!|(\tilde{w}_{l}-\tilde{w}_{l}^{b})\partial_{y^{l}}\eta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Next, let us control the second term in

{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,3}
in the following way:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\big(\tilde{w}_{3}-\tilde{w}_{3}^{b}-z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}\big)\partial_{z}\eta_{i}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\!|\!|z^{-2}(\tilde{w}_{3}-\tilde{w}_{3}^{b}-z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b})|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}|\!|\!|z^{2}\partial_{z}\eta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle+|\!|\!|z^{-1}(\tilde{w}_{3}-\tilde{w}_{3}^{b}-z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}|\!|\!|z\partial_{z}\eta|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\!|\!|\partial_{z}^{2}\tilde{w}_{3}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}.

By the virtue of the definition (
9.10
)
and the change of variable (
8.13
), one can write that:

{\rm div\,}\tilde{w}={\rm div\,}((D\Psi)^{-\star}(\chi w)^{\Psi})=({\rm div\,}(\chi w))^{\Psi}-\frac{\nabla d}{d}(D\Psi)^{-\star}(\chi w)^{\Psi},

where
d=\det(D\Psi).
Therefore,
using the identity
\partial_{z}^{2}\tilde{w}_{3}=\partial_{z}{\rm div\,}\tilde{w}-\sum_{l=1}^{2}\partial_{z}\partial_{y^{l}}\tilde{w}_{l},

we derive that:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\partial_{z}^{2}\tilde{w}_{3}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|\nabla w|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla{\rm div\,}w|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}.

In light of the equations
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{1},\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{3},
we can write:

\displaystyle{\rm div\,}w

\displaystyle={\rm div\,}\big(u-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\overline{\Gamma}\kappa\beta\nabla\theta\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\sigma+\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\varepsilon\mathfrak{N}+\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\frac{\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}}{\varepsilon\Gamma}\big[(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)\big(\frac{C_{v}\gamma}{R}\theta-\varepsilon\sigma\big)-\varepsilon^{2}\mathfrak{N}\big]

which leads to that:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\nabla{\rm div\,}w|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\sigma,u,\theta)|\!|\!|_{2,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla(\sigma,u,\theta,\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u)|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Consequently, we find
|\!|\!|\partial_{z}^{2}\tilde{w}_{3}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)
and thus

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\big(\tilde{w}_{3}-\tilde{w}_{3}^{b}-z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}\big)\partial_{z}\eta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

which, together with (
9.20
), finishes the control of
\mathfrak{M}_{3,3}^{\Psi}:

(9.21)

|\!|\!|\mathfrak{M}_{3,3}^{\Psi}|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Finally, let us detail the estimate of
\mathfrak{M}_{3,4}^{\Psi}
defined in (
9.14
). By the Taylor expansion and the Neumann boundary condition:
\partial_{z}\theta^{\Psi}|_{z=0}=(\partial_{\textbf{n}}\theta|_{\partial\Omega})^{\Psi}=0,
one can write:

\displaystyle({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi}-[({\Gamma\beta})^{\Psi}]^{b}=\varepsilon(\beta^{\Psi})^{b}z{\mathcal{U}}_{1}+\Gamma^{\Psi}z^{2}{\mathcal{U}}_{2},

where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{1}=\int_{0}^{1}((\Gamma^{\prime})^{\Psi}\partial_{z}\sigma^{\Psi})(t,y,\tau z)\mathrm{d}\tau,\qquad{\mathcal{U}}_{2}=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{z}^{2}(\beta)^{\Psi}(t,y,\tau z)(1-\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

It can be verified directly that:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|{\mathcal{U}}_{1}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\nabla\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle\kappa|\!|\!|{\mathcal{U}}_{2}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|{\mathcal{U}}_{2}|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}

(9.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\kappa|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla,\nabla^{2})\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla,\nabla^{2})\theta|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

We thus can bound
(\beta^{\Psi})^{b}{\mathcal{U}}_{1}(\varepsilon\kappa z)\partial_{z}^{2}\eta
as:

(9.23)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|(\beta^{\Psi})^{b}{\mathcal{U}}_{1}(\varepsilon\kappa z)\partial_{z}^{2}\eta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\sigma,\nabla\sigma)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+\varepsilon\kappa|\!|\!|\nabla^{2}\theta|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Note that by the equation (
4.3
), it holds that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\kappa|\!|\!|\nabla^{2}\theta|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\sigma,u,\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\kappa\nabla\theta,\varepsilon\mu\nabla u)|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

For the term
\kappa\Gamma^{\Psi}{\mathcal{U}}_{2}z^{2}\partial_{z}^{2}\eta,

it is not easy to control it in
|\!|\!|\cdot|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},
we thus need to control it in the three norms appearing in (
9.18
), which, thanks to (
9.22
), can be done in the following way:

(9.24)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\kappa\Gamma^{\Psi}{\mathcal{U}}_{2}z^{2}\partial_{z}^{2}\eta|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}\lesssim(1+|\!|\!|\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t})|\!|\!|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}|\!|\!|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{U}}_{2}|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Next, the other two norms can be dealt with

(9.25)

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\kappa\Gamma^{\Psi}{\mathcal{U}}_{2}z^{2}\partial_{z}^{2}\eta(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\kappa\Gamma^{\Psi}{\mathcal{U}}_{2}z^{2}\partial_{z}^{2}\eta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(1+|\!|\!|\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\bigg(|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{2,\infty,t}|\!|\!|\kappa{\mathcal{U}}_{2}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle+|\!|\!|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{U}}_{2}|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}\big(|\!|\!|\kappa\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}\theta(s)\|_{m-3,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We remark that again from Sobolev embedding,

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}\theta(s)\|_{m-3,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Collecting (
9.23
)-(
9.25
), we obtain that:

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}|\!|\!|{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,4}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\!|\!|{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,4}|\!|\!|_{m-6,\infty,t}+\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3,4}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with (
9.19
), (
9.21
),
yields (
9.18
).
∎

Remark 9.3
.

It follows from the property (
15.45
) that, for any
2\leq q<+\infty,

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}\eta\|_{m-5,\infty}^{q}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{q}}\Lambda_{1}^{m-5}\Lambda_{2}^{m-5}\bigg(\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{m-5,\infty}+\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|(\eta,{\mathfrak{M}}^{\Psi}_{3})(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg).

Therefore, as long as
\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\textbf{n}}^{2}\theta(0)\|_{m-5,\infty}
being bounded,
we have by (
9.16
), (
9.18
) that:

(9.26)

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}\theta\|_{m-5,\infty}^{q}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{q}}\big(Y_{m}(0)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)\big).

10.
Uniform estimates for the compressible part-II

Proposition 10.1
.

Under the assumption of (
1.34
), there exists
\vartheta_{8}>0,
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

any
0<t\leq T,
the following estimate holds:

(10.1)

\displaystyle\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{{\vartheta_{8}}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

The estimate can be obtained again by using the equations
(
1.17
)
and the induction arguments.
Nevertheless, as so far only the control of
\mathbb{P}(r_{0}u)
(rather than
\mathbb{P}u
) is shown, in the process of induction, we need to deal with some
product terms like
\nabla r_{0}\cdot\varepsilon\partial_{t}u
in the
L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}
norm. This requires the control of
|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t},
which have not been controlled.
Therefore, we need to do induction in the following two steps:

\bullet
Control
(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)
in a lower order regularity space
L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4},

\bullet
Control
(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)
in higher order regularity space
L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}.

We remark that after the first step, we can bound
|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\gtrsim|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t},

which enables us to proceed the second step.

As a preparation, before going into the detail for the first step, we show two estimates concerning the higher order time derivatives for
(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)

and the higher order conormal norms for
{\rm div\,}u.

Namely, for any
k\leq m-2,

(10.2)

\displaystyle\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k,0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\sigma,u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k+1,0}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle+\|\big(\mu\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma),\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k}},

and for any
j+l\leq m-2,l\geq 1,

(10.3)

\displaystyle\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}\lesssim\|\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1}}+\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\|\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{j+l}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

We start with the proof of (
10.2
).
By using the equation (
6.8
), one can obtain that:

\displaystyle\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k,0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k+1,0}}+\|\kappa\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k}}+\varepsilon\|\big(u\cdot\nabla\sigma,\mathfrak{N},\varepsilon^{-1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k}}.

By the product estimate (
15.1
), we find that the last term in the above can be bounded by
\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big){\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},
which, in turn together with the above estimate, yields (
10.2
) for
{\rm div\,}u.

Let us now control
\nabla\sigma
. We rewrite the equation (
4.22
) satisfied by
\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}){\Gamma}{\rm div\,}u
as:

(10.4)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta\tilde{\sigma}={\rm div\,}\big(f+\mu\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\big)\text{ in }\Omega,\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}=f\cdot\textbf{n}\text{ on }\partial\Omega,\end{array}\right.

where

f=-\frac{1}{R\beta}(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)u-\varepsilon\mu\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})({\rm div\,}u)\nabla\Gamma.

By using the elliptic estimate
(
15.24
), we find that:

(10.5)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k,0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f+\mu\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k,0}}+\mu\varepsilon\sum_{j\leq k}|(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}.

On the one hand, it can be verified that:

\displaystyle\|f+\mu\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u+\frac{1}{R\beta}\varepsilon\partial_{t}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2,0}}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

On the other hand, for any
1\leq k\leq m-2,

\displaystyle\big\|\big[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{k},\frac{1}{R\beta}\big]\varepsilon\partial_{t}u\big\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\big(\|\partial_{t}(\frac{1}{R\beta})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k-1,0}}+|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k,0}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Consequently, we obtain, for any
k\leq m-2,

\displaystyle\|f+\mu\varepsilon\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k,0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k+1,0}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Inserting this inequality and (
4.30
) into (
10.5
), one finds that:

\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k,0}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k+1,0}}+\mu\varepsilon\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Since
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}+\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}){\Gamma}{\rm div\,}u,
we derive further that

\|\nabla{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k,0}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k+1,0}}+\mu\varepsilon\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

By the equation
\eqref{newsys}_{1}
for

\varrho=\frac{\varepsilon}{\gamma}\sigma-\frac{(\gamma-1)C_{v}}{\gamma R}\theta,
we can bound

\mu\varepsilon\nabla{\rm div\,}u
in the following way:

(10.6)

\displaystyle\mu\varepsilon\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with the previous estimate, leads to that for any
0\leq k\leq m-2,

(10.7)

\|\nabla{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k,0}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k+1,0}}+\mu\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{k}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We thus finish the proof of (
10.2
) for
\nabla\sigma.

As for the proof of (
10.3
), we can use again the equation (
6.8
) to derive that for any
j+l\leq m-2,l\geq 1

\displaystyle\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}+\|\kappa\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{j+l}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, combined with the fact

\|\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}\lesssim\|\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1}}+\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}},

yields (
10.3
).

\bullet
Control of
\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}
.

We will show that: there exists
\vartheta_{9}>0,
such that:

(10.8)

\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{9}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

By noticing the estimates (
2.1
), (
3.1
), (
6.13
), (
6.14
), (
7.30
), (
4.42
), (
9.2
), we find that the above property is the consequence of the following estimate:

\displaystyle\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\cdot

\displaystyle\big(\|(\sigma,u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3,0}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|\big(\mu\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla\theta,\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}\big),

whose proof relies further on the following estimate:
for any
j+l\leq m-4,l\geq 1,

\displaystyle\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\|(\sigma,u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3,0}}+\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}\big)

(10.9)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\big(\mu\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla\theta,\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

In view of (
10.2
), (
10.3
),
to prove (
10
), it suffices to show the following estimate for
\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}:

\displaystyle\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}\lesssim\mu\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)

(10.10)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,0}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big),

which is the task of what follows.

Let us first notice that by
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{2}
,
\sigma
solves the following elliptic problem:

(10.11)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta{\sigma}={\rm div\,}\big(-\varepsilon\partial_{t}(r_{0}u)+\mu\varepsilon(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\nabla{\rm div\,}u+\varepsilon d\big)\text{ in }\Omega,\\[4.0pt]
\partial_{\textbf{n}}{\sigma}=(-\varepsilon\partial_{t}(r_{0}u)+\mu\varepsilon(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\nabla{\rm div\,}u+\varepsilon d)\cdot\textbf{n}-\mu\varepsilon\lambda\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}\text{ on }\partial\Omega,\end{array}\right.

where

r_{0}=\frac{1}{R\beta}\exp(\varepsilon R\sigma/C_{v}\gamma),\quad r_{1}=\varepsilon^{-1}(\exp(\varepsilon R\sigma/C_{v}\gamma)-1),

d=\colon u\partial_{t}r_{0}-r_{0}u\cdot\nabla u+r_{1}(-\nabla\sigma+\varepsilon\mu{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u)+\frac{\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma}}{\varepsilon}\varepsilon\mu{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u.

By using Proposition
15.1
, Corollary
15.3
as well as the definition of
{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}
(see (
1.20
)), one can verify that,

\|d\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We thus get from the elliptic estimate (
15.24
) that:
for any
j,l,
with
j+l\leq 4,l\geq 1,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}(r_{0}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}+\mu\varepsilon\|\nabla{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mu\varepsilon|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{m-\frac{9}{2}}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with the estimates (
4.30
) and (
10.6
),
yields that:

(10.12)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}+\mu\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

The proof of (
10
) is thus complete if we show that, for
j+l\leq m-4,

\displaystyle\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,0}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}\big)

(10.13)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big),

which can be done in the following way.
It follows from the elliptic estimate (
15.26
), the definition
v=\mathbb{P}(r_{0}u)
and the condition
r_{0}u\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=0
that:

(10.14)

\displaystyle\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|{\rm div\,}(r_{0}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|\nabla r_{0}\cdot u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}.

Noticing the fact

\|\partial_{t}(\nabla r_{0}/r_{0})\cdot r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

we can bound the last term in the last line as:

\displaystyle\|\nabla r_{0}\cdot u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\|\nabla\theta\|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Inserting the above estimate into (
10.14
), we find that

\displaystyle\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\|\nabla\theta\|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}

\displaystyle+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}.

Therefore, we eventually find (
10
) by induction on
l.

\bullet
Control of
\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}
.

In the previous step, we have controlled
\|({\rm div\,}u,\nabla\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}.
In this step, we recover the spatial conormal derivatives to the higher order
m-2.
Before going into the detail, let us first note that

|\!|\!|r_{0}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}
has essentially been bounded by the quantities that we have controlled.
Indeed, by using the identity (
2.15
) and the fact that near the boundaries

u^{j}\cdot\nabla=u_{1}^{j}\partial_{y^{1}}+u_{2}^{j}\partial_{y^{2}}+\frac{u^{j}\cdot\textbf{n}}{\phi}\phi\partial_{z},\text{ where}\,\,u^{j}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}u,

we find that: for any
0\leq j\leq m-4,0\leq k\leq m-4-j,

\displaystyle\|{\rm div\,}(r_{0}u^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}\big)\|{\rm div\,}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,k}}+\|u^{j}\cdot\nabla r_{0}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,k}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)(\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}+\|r_{0}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,k}})+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\big(\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}+\|{\rm div\,}(r_{0}u^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,k-1}}\mathbb{I}_{\{k\geq 1\}}+\|v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-4-j}}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-4,0}}\big),

where we have used the notation
v^{j}=\mathbb{P}(r_{0}u^{j}).

Therefore, one obtains by induction on
0\leq k\leq m-4-j
that: for any
0\leq j\leq m-4,

\displaystyle\|{\rm div\,}(r_{0}u^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-4-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\big(\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}+\|v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-4-j}}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-4,0}}\big).

Moreover, by again writing
r_{0}u^{j}=\mathbb{Q}(r_{0}u^{j})+v^{j},
we have:

\displaystyle\|r_{0}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-3-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|r_{0}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3-j,0}}+\|{\rm div\,}(r_{0}u^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-4-j}}+\|v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-3-j}}.

The previous estimates, together with the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
) and the fact
|\!|\!|\partial_{t}r_{0}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

enable us to get that

\displaystyle|\!|\!|r_{0}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{m-5}|\!|\!|r_{0}u^{j}|\!|\!|_{\star,m-5-j,\infty,t}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{m-5}\big(\|r_{0}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-3-j}}+\|({\rm div\,}(r_{0}u^{j}),\nabla v^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-4-j}}\big)+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\big(\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-4,0}}+\sum_{j=0}^{m-5}(\|\nabla v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-4-j}}+\|v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-3-j}})\big).

Note that we used a slightly different notation here
|\!|\!|\cdot|\!|\!|_{\star,l,\infty,t}
to stress that only spatial conormal derivatives involved in the norm. By (
3.1
), (
7.51
), (
8.1
), (
10.8
), (
4.42
), (
9.2
), we can thus find from the above estimate that, there exists a constant
\vartheta_{10}>0,
such that:

(10.15)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|r_{0}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{10}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We now are able to control
\|({\rm div\,}u,\nabla\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}.
Our ultimate goal is to show that
(
10.1
) holds, which relies on the following estimate:

\displaystyle\|(\nabla\sigma,

\displaystyle{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(r_{0}u,\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\cdot

(10.16)

\displaystyle\big(\|(\sigma,u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-1,0}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\big(\mu\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma),\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big).

Indeed, by (
2.1
), (
3.1
), (
6.13
), (
6.14
), (
7.30
); (
4.42
), (
9.2
), (
10.15
), one derives (
10.1
)
from the above estimate.
However, by induction, we find that to show (
10
), it suffices to prove that, for any
j+l\leq m-2,l\geq 1,

\displaystyle\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(r_{0}u,\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\big(\|(\sigma,u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2,0}}+\|(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\big(\mu\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma),\kappa{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta)\big)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

This, in turn, would be the consequence of (
10.2
), (
10.3
),
and the following estimate for
\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}:

\displaystyle\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(r_{0}u,\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

(10.17)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\big(\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,0}}+\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big).

The remaining paragraph is thus devoted to the proof of (
10
).

Similar to (
10.12
), one derives from (
4.30
), (
10.6
) and the elliptic estimate (
15.24
) that for any
j,l,
with
j+l\leq m-2,l\geq 1,

(10.18)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}+\mu\|\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla(\theta,\varepsilon\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

It thus remains to control the term
\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}},
which can be bounded by using the decomposition
r_{0}u=\mathbb{Q}(r_{0}u)+v:

(10.19)

\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,0}}+\|{\rm div\,}(r_{0}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Let us estimate
\|{\rm div\,}(r_{0}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}.

On the one hand, since
j+l\leq m-2,
and

\displaystyle\|(\varepsilon\partial_{t}r_{0}){\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|(\partial_{t}r_{0},{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\partial_{t}r_{0},{\rm div\,}u)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

we have that:

(10.20)

\displaystyle\|r_{0}{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

On the other hand, since
\nabla r_{0}=r_{0}(-\nabla\theta+\varepsilon\frac{R}{C_{v}\gamma}\nabla\tilde{\sigma})

and
[\frac{m}{2}]-1\leq m-5
by the assumption
m\geq 7,
the term
\|\nabla r_{0}\cdot u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}
can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\|\nabla r_{0}\cdot u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\theta\cdot r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)

(10.21)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(r_{0}u,\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)(\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}})+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Inserting estimates (
10.20
) and (
10
) into (
10.19
), we find that:

\displaystyle\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(r_{0}u,\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)(\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}})

\displaystyle\qquad+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Using the above estimate recursively
l
times, we obtain
that:

(10.22)

\displaystyle\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(r_{0}u,\nabla\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\big(\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1}}+\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{j+1,l-1}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\big).

Plugging the above estimate into (
10.18
), we eventually get (
10
) and thus finish the proof.
∎

Remark 10.2
.

Thanks to the relation
\frac{1}{R\beta r_{0}}=\exp({-\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}})

and the fact
\|\partial_{t}r_{0}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

it holds that:

\displaystyle\big\|\frac{1}{R\beta}\varepsilon\partial_{t}u\big\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|(\varepsilon\partial_{t})(r_{0}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

inserting which into (
5.20
), and using the estimates (
5.2
), (
6.13
), (
10.1
), (
10.22
), we can find a constant
\vartheta_{11}
such that:

(10.23)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{11}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Remark 10.3
.

In view of estimates (
6.27
), (
6.13
)
and (
10.1
),
we have, by choosing
\vartheta_{12}=\min\{\frac{\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}}{2},\vartheta_{8},\frac{1}{12}\}
that

(10.24)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{12}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proposition 10.4
.

Assume (
1.34
) holds true, then there exists a constant
\vartheta_{13}>0,
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

any
0<t\leq T:

(10.25)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{13}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

(10.26)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{13}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big)+\|\theta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\nabla u|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\big).

Proof.

For any
0\leq j\leq m-2,
we write
(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}u=u^{j}=\mathbb{P}u^{j}+\mathbb{Q}u^{j}.

First, by the elliptic estimate (
15.26
) and the estimate (
10.1
), it holds that for any
j\leq k\leq m-2,

(10.27)

\|\nabla\mathbb{Q}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k-j}}\lesssim\|{\rm div\,}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{{\vartheta_{8}}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Note that we denote
{\mathcal{H}}^{l}={\mathcal{H}}^{0,l}
for spaces that involves only spatial conormal derivatives.
It remains to control
\nabla\mathbb{P}u^{j}.
We will show later that for

0\leq j\leq k\leq m-2,

(10.28)

\displaystyle\|r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla v^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k-j}}+\|\nabla r_{0}(u^{j},\mathbb{Q}u^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k-j}}

where
v^{j}=\mathbb{P}(r_{0}u^{j}).
The last term in the above estimate can be bounded as:

\displaystyle\|(\theta,\nabla\theta,r_{0}u,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\nabla\theta,r_{0}u,{\rm div\,}u)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big).

We observe that the quantities
|\!|\!|r_{0}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}
have been controlled in (
10.15
) and (
10.22
), the quantity
|\!|\!|\mathbb{Q}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}
can be estimated thanks to the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
) by
\|{\rm div\,}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-4}}
which has been controlled in (
10.1
).
These facts, together with the estimate (
8.1
),
enable us to find a constant
\vartheta_{14}>0

(10.29)

\displaystyle\|r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{k-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{14}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Therefore, by noticing
(
4.42
), (
8.68
), we have by by choosing
\vartheta_{14}>0
smaller if necessary,
for any
1\leq j\leq m-2,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\mathbb{P}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|r_{0}^{-1}|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{14}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

and for any
0\leq j\leq m-3,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\mathbb{P}u^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-3-j}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{14}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

The last estimate, together with (
10.27
), yields
(
10.25
) by letting
\vartheta_{13}=\min\{\vartheta_{8},\vartheta_{14}\}.

To prove (
10.26
), we still need to control
\nabla\mathbb{P}u
in
L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}:

\displaystyle\|\nabla\mathbb{P}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}\lesssim\|r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|r_{0}^{-1}|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)+\|Zr_{0}^{-1}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2}}|\!|\!|r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}.

Since
|\!|\!|r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|(\nabla u,\nabla\mathbb{Q}u)|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|\nabla u|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}+\|(u,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{2}},
we can use (
6.13
), (
10.1
), (
10.27
), (
10.29
) to derive (
10.26
) (choosing
\vartheta_{13}
smaller if necessary).

It now remains to show (
10.28
). Let us write:

(10.30)

\displaystyle r_{0}\nabla\mathbb{P}u^{j}-\nabla\mathbb{P}(r_{0}u^{j})=-\mathbb{P}u^{j}\cdot\nabla r_{0}+\nabla{\mathcal{W}}^{j},\quad{\mathcal{W}}^{j}=\colon\mathbb{Q}(r_{0}u^{j})-(r_{0}\mathbb{Q}u^{j}),

By the definition of
\mathbb{Q},
one has
{\mathcal{W}}^{j}\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=0.
Consequently, it follows from the div-curl lemma that:

\displaystyle\|\nabla{\mathcal{W}}^{j}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|({\rm div\,}{\mathcal{W}}^{j},\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits{\mathcal{W}}^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla r_{0}\times\mathbb{Q}u^{j},\nabla r_{0}\cdot(u^{j},\mathbb{Q}u^{j})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{m-2-j}},

which, together with (
10.30
), yields (
10.28
).
∎

We are now in position to summarize the obtained estimates concerning the energy norms
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,T}(\sigma,u):

Proposition 10.5
.

Denote

(10.31)

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u)={\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u)-\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}.

Suppose that the assumption (
1.34
) holds,
then there exists
\vartheta_{15}>0,
such that:
any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

any
0<t\leq T,

(10.32)

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{15}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

Collecting (
2.1
), (
5.2
), (
10.23
) for the control of
\varepsilon-
dependent norms appearing in the first two line of
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}
(see definition (
1.21
))
and (
3.1
) (
6.4
), (
6.18
) (
6.19
), (
7.48
),
(
10.1
), (
10.24
), (
10.25
) for the uniform estimates:

\|(\sigma,u)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}},\quad\|\nabla(\sigma,u)\|_{L^{2}H_{co,\sqrt{\mu}}^{m-1}},\quad\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(\nabla\sigma,{\rm div\,}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}},\quad\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}},\quad\|(\nabla u,\nabla^{2}\sigma)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}},

we can find a constant
\vartheta_{15}>0,
such that (
10.32
) holds.
∎

11.
L_{t,x}^{\infty}
estimates for
(\sigma,u)

In this section, we focus on the a-priori estimates for
{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u):

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{m,t}(\sigma,u)=\colon|\!|\!|(\sigma,u)|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\nabla\sigma,(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u)|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\nabla u,\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}(\sigma,u)\big)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}.

Before going into the details, we begin with the following lemma which will be useful later.

Lemma 11.1
.

Define the following non-uniform
L^{\infty}_{t,x}
norms:

(11.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{m,t}(u)=\colon|\!|\!|\varepsilon\mu\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}.

Then it holds that:

(11.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{m,t}(u)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

By the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
),

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu|\!|\!|\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu\big(\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\big)\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t}.

Moreover, we use successively the equation
\eqref{newsys}_{1},
the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
) to get that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu|\!|\!|\nabla{\rm div\,}u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu|\!|\!|\nabla(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu|\!|\!|\nabla(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Finally, it follows from (
5.22
) and the
local expression of the Laplacian (
4.26
) that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu|\!|\!|\nabla^{2}u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\mu\nabla u,u)|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\mu\nabla{\rm div\,}u,\nabla\sigma,\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

∎

Proposition 11.2
.

Assume that (
1.34
) holds true.
There exists a constant
\vartheta_{16}>0,
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

the following property holds:

(11.3)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{16}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

We first observe that it suffices to show that there exists a constant
\vartheta_{17}>0,
such that:

(11.4)

{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}-|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}-|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{17}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Note that
|\!|\!|\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}
is contained in
the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}.

Inserting this estimate into
(
10.26
), we can find, by using
(
4.2
),
a constant
\vartheta_{18}>0
such that:

(11.5)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{18}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We then get by the Sobolev embedding
(
15.18
)
and the estimate (
10.32
) that

\displaystyle|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{16}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

where
\vartheta_{16}=\min\{\vartheta_{15},\vartheta_{17},\vartheta_{18}\}.

This, combined (
11.4
), gives rise to (
11.3
).

Let us now prove (
11.4
). Thanks to the
Sobolev embedding (
15.18
), some quantities
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u)
can be controlled directly
by
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{m,t}
which is defined in (
10.31
). For instance:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u),

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}\lesssim\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u),

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\nabla\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla^{2}\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}\lesssim\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u).

Consequently, in view of (
10.32
), we are left to control
|\!|\!|(\nabla u,\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}u)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,T}.

As before, in the interior domain
\Omega_{0}
, by using the Sobolev embedding as well as the equivalence of the conormal spaces and
usual Sobolev spaces, we have directly that:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\chi_{0}(\nabla u,\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}u,(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}\sigma)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\|\chi_{0}(u,\sigma)\|_{L^{\infty}H^{m-1}}\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

where
\chi_{0}
is a smooth function supported on
\Omega_{0}.
Therefore, we focus on the estimate of

|\!|\!|\chi_{i}(\nabla u,\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}u,(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}\sigma)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},

where
\chi_{i}(i=1,\cdots,N)
are the cut-off functions supported on the charts
\Omega_{i}.

It can be deduced from the identity (
2.15
), the fact

\Pi(\partial_{\textbf{n}}u)=\Pi(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\textbf{n})+\Pi\nabla(u\cdot\textbf{n})+\Pi(-(D\textbf{n})u)

and the local expression of the Laplacian (
4.26
) that:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|{\rm div\,}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\textbf{n}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\nabla^{2}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|(\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}+\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla{\rm div\,}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\textbf{n})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\nabla^{2}\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}+\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\Delta\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}.

In light of (
6.8
),
we control the
L_{t,x}^{\infty}
of
{\rm div\,}u
by those having been controlled:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|{\rm div\,}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\!|\!|\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}+\kappa|\!|\!|\Delta\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+\kappa|\!|\!|\nabla\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}^{2}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, by using
\eqref{newsys}_{1},

we also see that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla{\rm div\,}u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla(\varepsilon\partial_{t}+\varepsilon u\cdot\nabla)(\varepsilon\sigma,\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla\varepsilon\theta|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

It thus remains for us to control

|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\textbf{n}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},\quad\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\partial_{\textbf{n}}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\textbf{n})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},\quad\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\Delta\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},

which will be presented in the following three lemmas.
∎

Lemma 11.3
.

Under the same assumption as in Proposition
11.2
, there exists a constant
\vartheta_{19}>0
such that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\textbf{n}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{19}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),\qquad\forall\,i=1,\cdots N.

Proof.

Let us write:

\displaystyle\omega\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n}=\colon\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n}=\frac{1}{r_{0}}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n}-\frac{\nabla r_{0}\times u}{r_{0}}\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n},

where
r_{0}
is defined in (
7.1
). Consequently,
we have that:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\omega\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim|\!|\!|r_{0}^{-1}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}|\!|\!|\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|(r_{0}^{-1}\nabla r_{0},u)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}^{2}.

It can be derived from the definition (
7.1
) for
r_{0},
the estimate (
4.21
) for
\tilde{\sigma}

that

\displaystyle|\!|\!|(r_{0}^{-1},r_{0}^{-1}\nabla r_{0})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma},\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)+{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}(\theta)\big)

where
{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}(\theta)
has been controlled already in (
9.2
). Therefore, it suffices to prove that, there exists a constant
\vartheta_{20}>0,
such that:

(11.6)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\chi_{i}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)\times\textbf{n}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{20}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),\qquad\forall\,i=1,\cdots N.

Denote
\zeta_{i}=|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n}\big)^{\Psi_{i}},
where
\Psi_{i}
is the transformation associated to the normal geodesic coordinates defined in (
8.12
), we are lead to control
|\!|\!|\zeta_{i}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},\,i=1,\cdots N.
In what follows, we shall drop the subscript
i
for the notational clarity.

By (
8.27
),
\zeta
solves the system:

(11.7)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}+\tilde{u}\cdot\nabla){\zeta}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(\frac{{1}}{r_{0}})^{\Psi}(\partial_{z}^{2}+\Delta_{{g}})\zeta=|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}H^{\Psi}+H^{{\dagger}},\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\[2.5pt]
\zeta|_{z=0}=\big[\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}r_{0}\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\sigma}|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi u+2r_{0}|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\chi\Pi\big((D\textbf{n})u-au\big)\big]^{\Psi}|_{z=0},\\[2.5pt]
\zeta|_{t=0}=|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}\big(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)\times\chi\textbf{n}\big)^{\Psi}|_{t=0},\end{array}\right.

where
H^{{\dagger}}
is defined in (
8.28
),
H^{\Psi}=H^{0}\circ\Psi
with
H^{0}
defined in (
8.10
),
\Delta_{g}
defined in (
8.16
) involves only tangential derivatives. Moreover, the definitions of
r_{0},\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{u}
can be found in (
7.1
), (
8.19
).
In order to take benefit of the property (
15.43
)
for the diffusion-transport equation (
15.40
),
let us rewrite the system in the following way:

(11.8)

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{t}+\tilde{w}_{1}^{b}\partial_{y^{1}}+\tilde{w}_{2}^{b}\partial_{y^{2}}+z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}\partial_{z}\big){\zeta}-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}[(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}]^{b}\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta

\displaystyle=|g|^{\frac{1}{4}}H^{\Psi}+H^{{\dagger}}-\sum_{l=1}^{2}(\tilde{w}_{l}-\tilde{w}_{l}^{b})\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta-\big(\tilde{w}_{3}-\tilde{w}_{3}^{b}-z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}\big)\partial_{z}\zeta

\displaystyle+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\bigg((r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}\Delta_{g}\zeta+((r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}-[(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}]^{b})\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta\bigg)-\kappa\overline{\Gamma}(D\Psi)^{-*}(\beta\nabla\theta)^{\Psi}\cdot\nabla\zeta

where
\tilde{w}=\tilde{u}-\kappa\overline{\Gamma}(D\Psi)^{-*}(\beta\nabla\theta)^{\Psi}
is defined in (
9.10
).
To control
|\!|\!|\zeta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},
it suffices to
show the following proposition:

Proposition 11.4
.

The right hand side of (
11.8
) can be written into the form
\mathfrak{F}_{1}+\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}\mathfrak{F}_{2},
where

\mathfrak{F}_{1},\mathfrak{F}_{2}
admit the property:

(11.9)

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathfrak{F}_{1}(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathfrak{F}_{2}(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Indeed, once this proposition is proved, it then follows from (
15.43
) (by setting
\nu=\mu,b=[(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}]^{b}
) that,

(11.10)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\zeta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda_{3}^{m-5}\bigg(\|\zeta|_{t=0}\|_{m-5,\infty}+|\zeta|_{z=0}|_{m-5,\infty,t}+T\Lambda_{4}^{m-5}|\!|\!|\zeta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathfrak{F}_{1}(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+T^{\frac{1}{4}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathfrak{F}_{2}(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\bigg)

where we denote

\displaystyle\Lambda_{3}^{k}=\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|T(\tilde{w}^{b},(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}),[(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}]^{b}|_{k,\infty,t}\big),\quad\Lambda_{4}^{k}=\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|(\tilde{w}^{b},(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}),\partial_{t}[(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}]^{b}|_{k,\infty,t}\big).

Analogues to the estimate (
9.16
) and
(
9.17
), we can find a positive constant
\vartheta_{21}>0,
such that:

(11.11)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{3}^{m-5}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\frac{\vartheta_{21}}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),\,\qquad\Lambda_{4}^{m-5}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

which yields that:

(11.12)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{3}^{m-5}\|\zeta|_{t=0}\|_{m-5,\infty}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{21}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, by the definition
\eqref{eqzetainfty}_{2}
and the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
),
we can have that:

\displaystyle|\zeta|_{z=0}|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,u)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+\varepsilon|\!|\!|(\nabla\sigma,\varepsilon\mu\nabla{\rm div\,}u)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{m,t}+\varepsilon{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

which, combined with (
10.32
) for
\tilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{m,t}
, allows us to find a constant
\vartheta_{22}>0
such that:

\displaystyle|\zeta|_{z=0}|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{22}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Collecting (
11.11
) and the above estimate, we then find a constant
\vartheta_{23}>0,
such that:

(11.13)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{3}^{m-5}|\zeta|_{z=0}|_{m-5,\infty}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{23}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Inserting now (
11.12
), (
11.13
),
(
11.11
) and (
11.9
) into (
11.10
), we can ensure the existence of some constant
\vartheta_{20}>0,
such that:

(11.14)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\zeta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{20}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, by the change of variable, lead to (
11.6
) and thus finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
11.4
.

Let us come back to prove Proposition
11.4
.
We sort up the right hand side of the above equations and clarify the definition of
\mathfrak{F}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{F}_{2}.
First of all, as
\zeta=(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)\times\chi\textbf{n})^{\Psi},
we can rewrite the term

\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}\Delta_{g}\zeta
as:

(11.15)

\displaystyle\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}\Delta_{g}\zeta=\mu\partial_{z}\mathfrak{R}_{1}\bigg((r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi},(\partial_{y}^{2},\partial_{y},{\rm Id}\,)(r_{0}u)^{\Psi}\bigg)+\mu\mathfrak{R}_{2}\bigg((\partial_{z},{\rm Id}\,)(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi},(\partial_{y}^{3},\partial_{y}^{2},\partial_{y},{\rm Id}\,)(r_{0}u)^{\Psi}\bigg)

where
\mathfrak{R}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{2}
are smooth functions concerning their arguments with coefficients depending on
(\chi\textbf{n})^{\Psi},D\Psi
and their derivatives up to the second order.
Similarly, let us split the term
\kappa\overline{\Gamma}(D\Psi)^{-*}(\beta\nabla\theta)^{\Psi}\cdot\nabla\zeta
as

(11.16)

\displaystyle\kappa\overline{\Gamma}(D\Psi)^{-*}(\beta\nabla\theta)^{\Psi}\cdot\nabla\zeta=\kappa\partial_{z}\mathfrak{R}_{3}\big((\beta\nabla\theta)^{\Psi},\nabla(r_{0}u)^{\Psi}\big)+\kappa\mathfrak{R}_{4}\big(({\rm Id}\,,\partial_{z})(\beta\nabla\theta)^{\Psi},(\nabla,\partial_{y}^{2})(r_{0}u)^{\Psi}\big).

Next, let us write:

\displaystyle\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big((r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}-[(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}]^{b}\big)\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta=\mu\partial_{z}\mathfrak{R}_{5}+\mu\mathfrak{R}_{6}

with

(11.17)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{5}=\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\bigg(\big((r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}-[(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}]^{b}\big)\partial_{z}\zeta-\partial_{z}(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}\zeta\bigg),\quad\mathfrak{R}_{6}=\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\partial_{z}^{2}(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}\zeta.

Finally, for the term
H^{\Psi}
, there are also some
terms that may involve one more normal derivative than what can be controlled, namely the terms
(1)^{\Psi},(2)^{\Psi}:

\displaystyle(1)=\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits G\times\chi\textbf{n},\qquad(2)=-\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\big(\nabla(r_{0}^{-1})\times\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\omega_{r_{0}}\big)\times\chi\textbf{n}-2\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}{\rm div\,}\big(\omega_{r_{0}}\times\nabla(\chi\textbf{n})\big),

where
\omega_{r_{0}}=\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits(r_{0}u)
and
G
is defined in (
7
). These two terms could be split into the following forms respectively:

(11.18)

\displaystyle(1)^{\Psi}=\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}\mathfrak{R}_{7}\big(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}G^{\Psi}\big)+\mathfrak{R}_{8}\big(({\rm Id}\,,\partial_{y})G^{\Psi}\big),

(11.19)

\displaystyle(2)^{\Psi}=\mu\partial_{z}\mathfrak{R}_{9}\big(\omega_{r_{0}}^{\Psi},\partial_{y}(r_{0}u)^{\Psi},({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}\big)+\mu\mathfrak{R}_{10}\big(\omega_{r_{0}}^{\Psi},(\partial_{y},\partial_{y}^{2})(r_{0}u)^{\Psi},({\rm Id}\,,\nabla,\nabla^{2})(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}\big).

We now can define
\mathfrak{F}_{1},\mathfrak{F}_{2}
as:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{1}=\big(H-(1)-(2)\big)^{\Psi}+H^{{\dagger}}+\mu(\mathfrak{R}_{2}+\mathfrak{R}_{6}+\mathfrak{R}_{10})+\kappa\mathfrak{R}_{4}+\mathfrak{R}_{8}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{l=1}^{2}(\tilde{w}_{l}-\tilde{w}_{l}^{b})\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta-\big(\tilde{w}_{3}-\tilde{w}_{3}^{b}-z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}\big)\partial_{z}\zeta,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{2}=\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathfrak{R}_{1}+\mathfrak{R}_{5}+\mathfrak{R}_{9})+\kappa\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{R}_{3}+\mathfrak{R}_{7}(\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}G),

where
\mathfrak{R}_{1}-\mathfrak{R}_{10}
are defined just before in
(
11.15
)- (
11.19
)
and

\displaystyle H-(1)-(2)=-\sum_{l=1}^{3}\nabla u_{l}\times\partial_{l}(r_{0}u)\times\chi\textbf{n}+\omega_{r_{0}}\times(u\cdot\nabla(\chi\textbf{n}))+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}\omega_{r_{0}}\times\Delta(\chi\textbf{n}),

see also (
8.28
) for the definitions of
H^{{\dagger}}.

It now remains for us to show that
\mathfrak{F}_{1},\mathfrak{F}_{2}
satisfy the property (
11.9
).
Most of the terms appearing in
\mathfrak{F}_{1},\mathfrak{F}_{2}
can be controlled directly in view of their expressions. For instance, by using the assumption

\kappa\approx\mu,

(11.20)
