\displaystyle|\!|\!|(H-(1)-(2))^{\Psi},H^{{\dagger}},\mathfrak{R}_{1},\kappa\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{R}_{3},\kappa\mathfrak{R}_{4},\mu\mathfrak{R}_{6},\mathfrak{R}_{9},\mu\mathfrak{R}_{10})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, it follows similarly from the derivation of (
9.21
) that:

(11.21)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\sum_{l=1}^{2}(\tilde{w}_{l}-\tilde{w}_{l}^{b})\partial_{y^{l}}\zeta+\big(\tilde{w}_{3}-\tilde{w}_{3}^{b}-z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}\big)\partial_{z}\zeta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\nabla u,\nabla\tilde{w})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\partial_{z}^{2}\tilde{w}_{3}|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

It remains to control
\mu\mathfrak{R}_{2},\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{R}_{5},\mathfrak{R}_{7},\mathfrak{R}_{8}
which are the tasks of the following.
First, by the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
),
it holds that:

(11.22)

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{R}_{2}(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|(r_{0}u)(s)\|_{m-2,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|\nabla(r_{0}u)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|r_{0}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}\underline{H}_{co}^{m}}\big)+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Let us proceed to estimate
\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{R}_{5}
defined in (
11.17
). Thanks to (
8.125
), (
8.126
), we have that:

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\big((r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}-[(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}]^{b}\big)\partial_{z}\zeta=\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}(\varepsilon z\mathcal{V}_{1}\partial_{z}\zeta+z^{2}\mathcal{V}_{2}\zeta)

where, by the property (
9.26
) (for
p=4
),

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\mathcal{V}_{1}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),\quad\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{V}_{2}\|_{m-5,\infty}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\lesssim\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}({\rm Id}\,,\nabla,\nabla^{2})\theta\|_{m-5,\infty}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Therefore, by noting the following estimates:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}z\partial_{z}\zeta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|z^{2}\zeta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}+\ |\!|\!|u|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\partial_{z}(r_{0}^{-1})^{\Psi}\zeta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla)(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma},u)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

we can conclude that:

(11.23)

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{R}_{5}\|_{m-5,\infty}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Next, we control
\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{R}_{7},\mathfrak{R}_{8}
as:

(11.24)

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathfrak{R}_{7}(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\lesssim\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}G(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{4}},\quad\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathfrak{R}_{8}(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|G(s)\|_{m-4,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

where
G=G_{1}+G_{2}
with

\displaystyle G_{1}=u(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u,

\displaystyle G_{2}=-\varepsilon\mu r_{1}\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u-\mu\lambda_{1}(\Gamma-\overline{\Gamma})\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u-\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})(1+\varepsilon r_{1}){\rm div\,}u\nabla\Gamma.

By the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}
and the estimate (
11.2
), we see that:

(11.25)

\displaystyle\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|G_{2}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\sigma,\nabla\sigma,\varepsilon\mu{\rm div\,}u,\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}u)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

\displaystyle|\!|\!|G_{2}|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\sigma,\nabla\sigma,\varepsilon\mu{\rm div\,}u,\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u)|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Moreover, by (
8.147
), (
8.149
),

\displaystyle u(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)r_{0}=-\frac{\kappa}{C_{v}\gamma}\exp\big(\frac{R\varepsilon}{C_{v}\gamma}\tilde{\sigma}\big)\Gamma u\Delta\theta+l.o.t,\quad\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}r_{0}^{-1}[\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits,r_{0}]u=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}(\Delta\theta)u+l.o.t.\,,

where we denote
l.o.t.
for the terms which satisfy:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(l.o.t)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|(l.o.t)(s)\|_{m-4,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Noticing the property (
9.26
) and the fact

(\mu+\kappa)|\!|\!|\Delta\theta|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),\quad\kappa\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\Delta\theta(s)\|_{m-4,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\kappa(\|\nabla\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}+\|\Delta\theta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{m-2}})\lesssim{\mathcal{E}}_{m,t},

we can conclude that:

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mu^{-\frac{1}{2}}G_{1}(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}+\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|G_{1}(s)\|_{m-4,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),

which, combined with (
11.25
) and (
11.24
), yields

(11.26)

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathfrak{R}_{7}(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{4}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}+\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathfrak{R}_{8}(s)\|_{m-5,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

We now finish the proof of (
11.9
) by
collecting (
11.20
)-(
11.23
) and (
11.26
).
∎

Lemma 11.5
.

Under the same assumption as in Proposition
11.2
, there exists a constant
\vartheta_{24}>0
such that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\partial_{\textbf{n}}(\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{24}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),\qquad\forall\,i=1,\cdots N.

Proof.

Since
|\!|\!|\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}
has been controlled,
it suffices for us to control
\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\partial_{\textbf{n}}\omega_{\textbf{n}i}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t},
where

\omega_{\textbf{n}i}=\colon\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u\times\chi_{i}\textbf{n}-2\Pi(\chi_{i}(-au+D\textbf{n}\cdot u))

solves the equations:

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+u\cdot\nabla)\omega_{\textbf{n}i}-\lambda_{1}R\mu(\beta\Gamma)\Delta\omega_{\textbf{n}i}=F_{i}^{\omega}+L_{i},\,\text{ in }\Omega_{i}\cap\Omega\quad\omega_{\textbf{n}i}|=0,\,\text{ on }{\partial(\Omega_{i}\cap\Omega)},

we refer to (
5.7
) for the definitions of

F_{i}^{\omega}
and
L_{i}.

As before, we define
\varsigma_{i}=\colon\omega_{\textbf{n}i}\circ\Psi_{i},

(
\Psi_{i}
is defined in (
8.12
)) and extend it by zero from

\Psi^{-1}(\overline{\Omega_{i}\cap\Omega})
to
\overline{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}}
without changing the notation.
Thanks to (
8.13
)-(
8.15
), we can find that
\varsigma_{i}
is governed by the system: (the subscript is dropped)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\big(\partial_{t}+\tilde{w}_{1}^{b}\partial_{y^{1}}+\tilde{w}_{2}^{b}\partial_{y^{2}}+z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b})\partial_{z}\big)\varsigma-\mu\lambda_{1}R[(\Gamma\beta)^{\Psi}]^{b}\partial_{z}^{2}\varsigma=F^{\varsigma}\text{ in }\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3},\\[2.5pt]
\varsigma|_{t=0}=\omega_{\textbf{n}}|_{t=0}\circ\Psi,\quad\varsigma|_{z=0}=0,\end{array}\right.

where
\tilde{w}
is defined in (
9.10
) and

(11.27)

\displaystyle F^{\varsigma}

\displaystyle=(F^{\omega}+L)^{\Psi}-\sum_{l=1}^{2}(\tilde{w}_{l}-\tilde{w}_{l}^{b})\partial_{y^{l}}\varsigma-\big(\tilde{w}_{3}-\tilde{w}_{3}^{b}-z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}\big)\partial_{z}\varsigma

\displaystyle+\mu\lambda_{1}R\bigg((\Gamma\beta)^{\Psi}(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{z}\ln|g|\partial_{z}+\Delta_{g})\varsigma+((\Gamma\beta)^{\Psi}-[(\Gamma\beta)^{\Psi}]^{b})\partial_{z}^{2}\varsigma\bigg)-\kappa\overline{\Gamma}(D\Psi)^{-*}(\beta\nabla\theta)^{\Psi}\cdot\nabla\varsigma.

We can now apply (
15.44
) in appendix (setting
\nu=\mu
)
to obtain that:

(11.28)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\partial_{z}\varsigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda_{3}^{m-5}\|\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\varsigma|_{t=0}\|_{m-5,\infty}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda_{3}^{m-5}\Lambda_{4}^{m-5}|\!|\!|\varepsilon F^{\varsigma}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

where
\Lambda_{3}^{m-5},\Lambda_{4}^{m-5}
admit the property (
11.11
). Similar to (
11.12
), we have directly by (
11.11
) that:

(11.29)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{3}^{m-5}\|\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\varsigma|_{t=0}\|_{m-5,\infty}\lesssim\Lambda_{3}^{m-5}Y_{m}(0)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{21}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

It thus remains to control
|\!|\!|\varepsilon F^{\varsigma}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}.
By checking every term in (
5.7
) where
F^{\omega},L
are defined, one can have that:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\varepsilon(F^{\omega}+L)^{\Psi}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\big(({\rm Id}\,,\nabla,\varepsilon\partial_{t})(\sigma,u,\theta),\varepsilon\mu\nabla^{2}u\big)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big).

Next, it follows almost identically as in (
11.21
) that:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\sum_{l=1}^{2}(\tilde{w}_{l}-\tilde{w}_{l}^{b})\partial_{y^{l}}\varsigma+\big(\tilde{w}_{3}-\tilde{w}_{3}^{b}-z(\partial_{z}\tilde{w}_{3})^{b}\big)\partial_{z}\varsigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Finally, it is directly to verify from their expression that the multiplication of
\varepsilon
and the last line in (
11.27
) can be controlled as:

\displaystyle|\!|\!|\varepsilon(\textnormal{last line of \eqref{defFvarsigma}})|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\varepsilon\mu(|\!|\!|\nabla u|\!|\!|_{m-3,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla^{2}u|\!|\!|_{m-4,\infty,t})+|\!|\!|\nabla(\sigma,\theta)|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Note that the intermediate estimate (
11.2
) has been used in the derivation of the second inequality.
The previous three estimates now give rise to:

|\!|\!|\varepsilon F^{\varsigma}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),
inserting which and (
11.29
) into
(
11.28
) stems

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\partial_{z}\varsigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{24}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big),\quad

where
\vartheta_{24}=\min\{\vartheta_{21},\frac{1}{2}\}.

The proof of this lemma is thus completed.
∎

Lemma 11.6
.

Under the assumption (
1.34
), it holds that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
any
0<t\leq T,

(11.30)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\Delta\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim Y_{m}(0)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,t}\big).

Proof.

It has been computed in Section 6 that
\Delta\sigma
solves the damped transport equation (
6.23
). One can verify, by using the similar arguments as in [Lemma 3.3,
[
43
]
] that:

(11.31)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\Delta\sigma|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Delta\sigma(0)\|_{m-5,\infty}+\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\mathfrak{H}_{3}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}+\varepsilon\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

where
\mathfrak{H}_{3}={\rm div\,}\mathfrak{H}_{2}-\varepsilon^{2}\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\big(\nabla\Gamma\cdot\partial_{t}\nabla\sigma+\nabla(\Gamma u)\cdot\nabla\sigma\big)

with
\mathfrak{H}_{2}
defined in
(
6.22
). By checking every term in the expression of
\mathfrak{H}_{3},
we find that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|\mathfrak{H}_{3}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,t}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}\big),

which, together with (
11.31
), yields (
11.30
).
∎

12.
Uniform estimates-proof of Proposition
1.10

The estimates established in the previous sections allow us to give the proof of Proposition
1.10
:

Proof of
1.10
.

In light of the estimates (
10.32
), (
11.5
), we find that

(12.1)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{m,T}(\sigma,u)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{25}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}\big),

where
\vartheta_{25}=\min\{\vartheta_{15},\vartheta_{18}\}>0.

This estimate, together with the estimate (
6.13
) for
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,T}(\theta),
yields that:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{m,T}(\sigma,u,\theta)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{26}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}\big),

where
\vartheta_{26}=\min\{\vartheta_{25},\frac{\tilde{\vartheta}_{0}}{2}\}>0.

Moreover, by the virtue of (
9.2
), (
11.3
), we conclude the following estimate for

{\mathcal{A}}_{m,t}(\sigma,u,\theta):

(12.2)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{m,T}(\sigma,u,\theta)\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{27}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}\big),

where
\vartheta_{27}=\min\{\vartheta_{6},\vartheta_{16}\}>0.

By choosing
\vartheta_{0}=\min\{\vartheta_{26},\vartheta_{27}\},
the desired estimate (
1.35
) then follows from (
12.1
) and (
12.2
).
∎

13.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
1.1
, which relies on Proposition
1.10
and
the local well-posedness result stated in below:

Theorem 13.1

(Local existence for fixed
\varepsilon,\mu,\kappa
)
.

Given the initial data
(\sigma_{0}^{\varepsilon},u_{0}^{\varepsilon},\theta_{0}^{\varepsilon})
satisfying the compatibility condition up to order 2 and

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{l}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})(0)\in H^{2-l}(\Omega),\quad l=0,1,2,

-\bar{c}\leq\varepsilon\sigma_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x),\,\theta_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x)\leq\bar{c},\quad\forall x\in\Omega,

where
\bar{c}>0
is a fixed constant. Then there exists
T_{\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}>0,
such that
system (
1.17
), (
1.16
) has a unique strong solution

(13.1)

(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\in C^{l}\big([0,T_{\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}],H^{2-l}\big),\quad\partial_{t}^{l}(u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}\big([0,T_{\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}],H^{3-l}\big),\quad l=0,1,2.

Moreover, the following property holds:

-3{\bar{c}}\leq\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon}(t,x),\,\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\leq 3\bar{c},\quad\forall(t,x)\in[0,T_{\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}]\times\Omega.

Such a result can be established in a similar way (Galerkin method and fixed point arguments) as in
[
59
]
or
[
26
]
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

On the one hand, by the assumption
(\sigma_{0}^{\varepsilon},u_{0}^{\varepsilon},\theta_{0}^{\varepsilon})\in Y_{m}(0)\,(\text{defined in}\eqref{initialnorm}),
we have that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mu u_{0}^{\varepsilon},\sigma_{0}^{\varepsilon},\kappa\theta_{0}^{\varepsilon}\in H^{2}(\Omega),\,\quad\varepsilon\partial_{t}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})(0),\,\in H^{1}(\Omega),\quad(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{2}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})(0),\,(\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon})(0)\in L^{2}(\Omega).

Therefore, by Theorem
13.1
, one can find some
T_{\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}>0
such that there is a unique solution of (
1.17
), (
1.16
) satisfying the (
13.1
) and (
1.34
).
On the other hand, as
(\sigma_{0}^{\varepsilon},u_{0}^{\varepsilon},\theta_{0}^{\varepsilon})\in Y_{m},

a higher regularity space,
by propagation of regularity arguments (for example based on applying finite difference instead of derivatives)
in the estimates of Section 2-11, we find that
the estimates of Proposition
1.10
hold on
[0,T_{\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}]
.
More specifically, we can find
two increasing polynomials
\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1}
that are independent of
\varepsilon
and
T_{\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon},
and a constant
\vartheta_{0}>0,

such that for any
0\leq T\leq\min\{1,T_{\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}\},0<\varepsilon\leq 1,(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

(13.2)

\mathcal{N}_{m,T}^{\mu,\kappa}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\leq\Lambda_{0}\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},Y_{m,\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}(0)\big)+(T+\varepsilon)^{\vartheta_{0}}\Lambda_{1}\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},\mathcal{N}_{m,T}^{\mu,\kappa}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\big).

Moreover, by using the characteristics method, we have that
\varepsilon\sigma
can be expressed as,

(13.3)

\displaystyle\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\varepsilon\sigma_{0}^{\varepsilon}\big(X^{-1}(t,x)\big)-\int_{0}^{t}S_{\sigma^{\varepsilon}}\big(X(s,X^{-1}(t,x))\big)\,\mathrm{d}s,

(13.4)

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\theta_{0}^{\varepsilon}\big(X^{-1}(t,x)\big)-\int_{0}^{t}S_{u^{\varepsilon}}\big(X(s,X^{-1}(t,x))\big)\,\mathrm{d}s,

where
X(t,\cdot)
is the flow associated to
u^{\varepsilon}

and

S_{\sigma^{\varepsilon}}=\gamma{\rm div\,}u^{\varepsilon}-(\gamma-1)\big(\kappa\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon})+\mathfrak{N}\big),\qquad S_{u^{\varepsilon}}=\frac{R}{C_{v}}\big({\rm div\,}u^{\varepsilon}-\kappa\Gamma{\rm div\,}(\beta\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon})-\varepsilon\mathfrak{N}\big).

From the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}_{m,T}^{\mu,\kappa}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})
in (
1.24
), we can find a polynomial

\Lambda_{2},
such that as long as (
1.34
) and (
1.33
) hold,

(13.5)

\displaystyle\|(S_{\sigma^{\varepsilon}},S_{u^{\varepsilon}})\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\Omega)}\leq\Lambda_{2}\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{m,T}^{\mu,\kappa}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\big).

Let us define for any
\varepsilon\in[0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

T^{\varepsilon}_{*,\mu,\kappa}=\sup\big\{T\big|(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\in C^{l}([0,T],H^{2-l}),\,\partial_{t}^{l}(u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}([0,T],H^{3-l}),\,l=0,1,2\big\},

\displaystyle T_{0,\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}=\sup\big\{

\displaystyle T\leq\min\{T^{\varepsilon}_{*,\mu,\kappa},1\}\big|{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}^{\mu,\kappa}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})\leq 2\Lambda_{0}\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},M\big),

\displaystyle-2{\bar{c}}\leq\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon}(t,x),\,\varepsilon(\sigma^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon}){\rm div\,}u^{\varepsilon})(t,x),\,\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\leq 2\bar{c},\,\,\forall(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\Omega\big\}

where
M>\sup_{(\mu,\kappa)\in A,\varepsilon\in(0,1]}Y_{m,\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}(0).

We now choose successively two constants
0<\varepsilon_{0}\leq 1
and
0<T_{0}\leq 1
(uniform in
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
) which are small enough, such that:

\displaystyle(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\frac{\varepsilon_{0}^{2}}{c_{0}}\Lambda_{0}\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},M\big)\leq\frac{1}{4}\bar{c},

\displaystyle({T_{0}}+\varepsilon_{0})^{\vartheta_{0}}\Lambda_{1}\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},2\Lambda_{0}

\displaystyle\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},M\big)\big)<\frac{1}{2}\Lambda_{0}\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},M\big),\quad T_{0}\Lambda_{2}\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},2\Lambda_{0}\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},M\big)\big)<\frac{1}{2}\bar{c}.

In order to prove Theorem
1.1
, it suffices to show that
T_{0,\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}\geq{T_{0}}
for every
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0},(\mu,\kappa)\in A.
Suppose otherwise
T_{0,\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}<{T_{0}}
for some
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0},(\mu,\kappa)\in A,
then in view of the inequality
(
13.2
), the formulas (
13.3
), (
13.4
) and the estimate (
13.5
),
we have by the definition of
\varepsilon_{0}
and
T_{0}
that, for any
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0},

(13.6)

{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}^{\mu,\kappa}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\leq\frac{3}{2}\Lambda_{0}\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},M\big),\qquad\forall\,T\leq\tilde{T}=\min\{T_{0},T^{\varepsilon}_{*,\mu,\kappa}\},

(13.7)

-2{\bar{c}}\leq\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon}(t,x),\,\varepsilon(\sigma^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}){\Gamma}(\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon}){\rm div\,}u^{\varepsilon})(t,x),\,\theta^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\leq 2\bar{c},\quad\forall\,(t,x)\in[0,\tilde{T}]\times\Omega.

We will prove that
\tilde{T}=T_{0}\leq T_{*,\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}.
This fact, combined with the definition of
T_{0,\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}
and the estimates (
13.6
), (
13.7
),
yields
T_{0,\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}\geq T_{0},
which is a contradiction with the assumption
T_{0,\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}<T_{0}.
To continue, we shall need the claim stated and proved below.
Indeed, once the following claim holds, we have by (
13.6
) that

\|\partial_{t}^{l}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})(T_{0})\|_{H^{2-l}(\Omega)}<+\infty,\quad\forall\,l=0,1,2,

which, combined with the local existence result stated in
Theorem
13.1
, yields that
T_{*,\mu,\kappa}^{\varepsilon}>T_{0}.

∎

Claim.

For all
\varepsilon\in(0,1],(\mu,\kappa)\in A,

if
{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}^{\mu,\kappa}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})<+\infty,
then

(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{l}(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\in C^{l}([0,T],H^{2-l}),\quad(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{l}(u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}([0,T],H^{3}),\quad l=0,1,2.

Proof of the claim.

We see from the definition of
{\mathcal{N}}_{m,T}^{\mu,\kappa}
in (
1.20
) and Remark
5.4
that:

\displaystyle(\varepsilon\mu u^{\varepsilon},\sigma^{\varepsilon},\kappa\theta^{\varepsilon})\in L^{\infty}([0,T],H^{2}),\quad(\varepsilon\partial_{t},(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{2})(u^{\varepsilon},\sigma^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\in L^{\infty}([0,T],H^{1}),

\displaystyle(\varepsilon\mu u^{\varepsilon},\kappa\theta^{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}([0,T],H^{3}),\quad\varepsilon\partial_{t}((\varepsilon\mu)^{\frac{1}{2}}u^{\varepsilon},\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\theta^{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}([0,T],H^{2}),\quad\varepsilon\partial_{t}^{2}(\varepsilon u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}([0,T],H^{1}).

Moreover, as
m\geq 7,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{3}(\varepsilon^{3}u^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{3}\sigma^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}),\quad\varepsilon\partial_{t}\nabla^{2}\sigma^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}),

one deduces from the interpolation that

\partial_{t}^{2}(\varepsilon^{3}u^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{3}\sigma^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon})\in C([0,T],L^{2}),\quad\,\partial_{t}(\varepsilon^{2}u^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon^{2}\sigma^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon\theta^{\varepsilon})\in C([0,T],H^{1}),\quad(\varepsilon\mu u^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon\kappa\theta^{\varepsilon})\in C([0,T],H^{2}).

This ends the proof of the claim.
Note that at this stage we do not require the norms listed in the last line to be bounded uniformly in
\varepsilon,\mu,\kappa
.
∎

14.
Proof of Theorem
1.11

In this section, we sketch the proof of the convergence result stated in Theorem
1.11
, which is essentially adapted from
[
1
,
45
]
.

Before going into details, we first verify,
based on the uniform estimates for
\theta^{\varepsilon},
that:

(14.1)

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\theta^{0}\quad\text{ in }C([0,T_{1}],H_{loc}^{1}(\Omega))\cap C([0,T_{1}]\times\Omega).

Indeed, it follows from the uniform estimate (
1.32
) that

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T_{1}}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-1}}+\|(\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon},\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}\theta^{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{T_{1}}^{\infty}H_{co}^{m-2}}+\|\partial_{t}\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{L_{T_{1}}^{2}H_{co}^{m-3}}<+\infty,

which stems the convergence (up to extraction of the subsequences) of
(\theta^{\varepsilon},\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon})
to
(\theta^{0},\nabla\theta^{0})
in

C([0,T_{1}],H_{co,loc}^{m-3}(\Omega)).

By using further the Sobolev embedding (
15.18
),
we have also that
|\!|\!|\theta^{\varepsilon}-\theta^{0}|\!|\!|_{m-5,\infty,T_{1}}\rightarrow 0
and thus finish the proof of (
14.1
)
by remembering the assumption
m\geq 7.

The delicate part concerning the proof of Theorem
1.11

is the first half which is re-summarize in the following proposition for convenience:

Proposition 14.1
.

Let
(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\in C([0,T_{1}],L^{2})
be the solution to system (
1.17
), (
1.16
) satisfying the uniform estimates (
1.32
). Under the assumptions made in Theorem
1.11
, the following strong convergence for the penalized terms holds:

(14.2)

\sigma^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0,\,\quad{\rm div\,}u^{\varepsilon}-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\kappa\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon}){\rm div\,}(\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon})\rightarrow 0\qquad\text{ in }\,L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)).

Let us first assume that the above proposition holds true and verify that the limit
(u^{0},\theta^{0})
of the sequences
(u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})
solves the equations (
1.36
). To proceed, we need the following claim:

Claim
: Let
(\sigma^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})
be the solution to system (
1.17
), (
1.16
) satisfying the uniform estimates (
1.32
). The following convergence properties hold:
up to extraction of the subsequences,

(14.3)

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow u^{0}\text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)),\quad\mathbb{Q}u^{0}=\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\beta(\theta^{0})\nabla\theta^{0},

where
\mathbb{Q}
is the projection defined in (
6.1
) and
\overline{\Gamma}=1/\overline{P}.
Note that by the definition of
\beta
in (
1.18
), the term
\beta(\theta^{0})\nabla\theta^{0}=\nabla(\beta(\theta^{0})-\beta(0)).

Let us postpone the proof of this claim and justify the convergence of the system (
1.17
) to (
1.36
). Thanks to the facts (
14.1
), (
14.3
),
the uniform estimates (
1.32
),
it is easy to verify from the equation of

\theta^{\varepsilon}
in
\eqref{NCNS-S2}_{3}
that

\displaystyle\frac{C_{v}}{R}(\partial_{t}+u^{0}\cdot\nabla)\theta^{0}-\frac{1}{\gamma}\kappa\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}(\beta(\theta^{0})\nabla\theta^{0})=0

holds in the sense of distribution. However, as every term in the above equation belongs to
C([0,T_{1}],L^{2}(\Omega)),
it also holds in the strong sense. It now remains to find
\nabla\pi^{0}\in L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L^{2}(\Omega))

and show that
(u^{0},\nabla\pi^{0})
solves
\eqref{NINS-1}_{1},
(
1.37
) in the sense of (
1.41
). To start, it is convenient to look at the equations satisfied by
w^{\varepsilon}=\colon u^{\varepsilon}-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon},
which, in light of
\eqref{sys-uni}_{2},
takes the form:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}(\partial_{t}+u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla)w^{\varepsilon}+\nabla\pi^{\varepsilon}-\mu\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u^{\varepsilon}-F(\theta^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})=\varepsilon\tilde{F}_{w}^{\varepsilon}

where

\pi^{\varepsilon}=\sigma^{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon-\frac{(\gamma-1)}{C_{v}\gamma}\overline{\Gamma}\kappa\big(-{\rm div\,}w^{\varepsilon}+\frac{\kappa}{\gamma}\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}(\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon})\big),

F(\theta^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})=\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\frac{\gamma-1}{R\gamma}(\nabla u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon}-\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{t}+u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla)\theta^{\varepsilon}),\quad\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}]}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\tilde{F}_{w}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L^{2}(\Omega))}<+\infty.

With the help of (
14.1
), (
14.3
),
and the uniform estimates (
1.32
), we deduce by testing the divergence-free, compactly supported test function that

(14.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta^{0})}(\partial_{t}+u^{0}\cdot\nabla)w^{0}-F(\theta^{0},u^{0})+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{0}\bigg)=0

holds in the sense of distribution.
That is, for any
\psi\in C^{\infty}([0,T_{1}],C_{c}^{\infty}(\ \overline{\Omega}))
with

{\rm div\,}\psi=0,\,\psi\cdot\textbf{n}|_{\partial\Omega}=0,
the following identity holds: for every
0<t\leq T_{1},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{R}\int_{\Omega}\big(\frac{w^{0}}{\beta(\theta^{0})}\cdot\psi\big)(t,\cdot)\ \mathrm{d}x+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits w^{0}\cdot\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\psi\ \mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{R}\int_{\Omega}\big(\frac{w^{0}}{\beta(\theta^{0})}\cdot\psi\big)(0,\cdot)\ \mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\bigg(F(\theta^{0},u^{0})-\frac{u^{0}}{R\beta(\theta^{0})}\cdot\nabla w^{0}\bigg)\cdot\psi\ \mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{R}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{w^{0}}{\beta(\theta^{0})}\cdot\partial_{t}\psi+\partial_{t}(\frac{1}{\beta(\theta^{0})})w^{0}\cdot\psi\ \mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s+\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}2\Pi\big(-au^{0}+(D\textbf{n})u^{0}\big)\cdot\psi\ \mathrm{d}S_{y}\mathrm{d}s.

The identity (
14.4
) lead us to find
a
\nabla\tilde{\pi}^{0}\in L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L^{2}(\Omega))
by solving the elliptic problem:

(14.5)

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{\pi}^{0}={\rm div\,}H^{0}\text{ in }\Omega\qquad\qquad\partial_{\textbf{n}}\tilde{\pi}^{0}|_{\partial\Omega}=H^{0}\cdot\textbf{n}-\mu\overline{\Gamma}\lambda_{1}\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{0}\cdot\textbf{n},\quad\tilde{\pi}^{0}\xlongrightarrow{|x|\rightarrow+\infty}0

with

H^{0}=-\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta^{0})}(\partial_{t}+u^{0}\cdot\nabla)w^{0}+F(\theta^{0},u^{0}).
Indeed, as

{\rm div\,}(\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta^{0})}\partial_{t}w^{0})=\nabla\big(\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta^{0})}\big)\partial_{t}w^{0},

it holds that
{\rm div\,}H^{0}\in L^{2}([0,T_{1}],H^{-1}(\Omega)).
Moreover, it follows from (
4.31
), (
4.32
), (
14.3
) that:

\displaystyle\mu\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{0}\cdot\textbf{n}\in L^{2}\big([0,T_{1}],H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)\big).

Next, direct computations show that:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta^{0})}(\partial_{t}+u^{0}\cdot\nabla)w^{0}-F(\theta^{0},u^{0})=\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta^{0})}(\partial_{t}+u^{0}\cdot\nabla)u^{0}-\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\frac{\gamma-1}{R\gamma}\nabla(\partial_{t}+u^{0}\cdot\nabla)\theta^{0},

which, together with (
14.4
), implies that the following holds in the sense of distribution:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta^{0})}(\partial_{t}+u^{0}\cdot\nabla)u^{0}-\mu\overline{\Gamma}{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u^{0}\bigg)=0

Define
\nabla\pi^{0}=\nabla\big(\tilde{\pi}^{0}-\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\frac{\gamma-1}{R\gamma}(\partial_{t}+u^{0}\cdot\nabla)\theta^{0}\big)\in L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L^{2}(\Omega)),
we thus find that
(u^{0},\nabla\pi^{0})
solves

\eqref{NINS-1}_{2}
in the sense of (
1.41
). Finally, it results from the uniform bounds

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}]}\big(\|(u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{T_{1}}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-1}}+\|(\nabla u^{0},\nabla\theta^{0},\partial_{t}\theta^{0})\|_{L_{T_{1}}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}}^{0,m-2}\cap L^{\infty}([0,T_{1}]\times\Omega)}\big)<+\infty

that
(u^{0},\theta^{0})
satisfy the additional regularity (
1.40
). The uniqueness of the solution to the limit system stems from the boundedness of the
Lipschitz norm, which in turn, means that the convergence of
(u^{\varepsilon},\theta^{\varepsilon})
holds for the whole sequence.

Proof of the claim:

Let us prove (
14.3
). Recall that
w^{\varepsilon}=u^{\varepsilon}-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon}.
The property
(
14.2
) together with
uniform boundedness of
{\rm div\,}\big(\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon}\big)

in
L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L^{2}(\Omega))
imply that
{\rm div\,}w^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0
in
L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)),
which combined with the weak convergence of
\mathbb{Q}w^{\varepsilon},
yields
\mathbb{Q}w^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0
in
L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)).
Therefore, in view of (
14.1
),
we find that

\mathbb{Q}u^{\varepsilon}
converges strongly in
L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega))
to
\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\kappa\overline{\Gamma}\beta(\theta^{0})\nabla\theta^{0}.

It thus remains to show
\mathbb{P}u^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}u^{0}\text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)).
As
\theta^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon}
are uniformly bounded in
(L^{2}\cap L^{\infty})([0,T_{1}]\times\Omega),
we easily see that

(14.6)

\displaystyle\frac{u^{\varepsilon}}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\rightharpoonup\frac{u^{0}}{\beta(\theta^{0})}\Rightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{u^{\varepsilon}}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\big)\rightharpoonup\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{u^{0}}{\beta(\theta^{0})}\big)\quad\text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{1}]\times\Omega).

On the other hand, it follows from the identity

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{u^{\varepsilon}}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\big)=\mathbb{P}\bigg(u^{\varepsilon}\partial_{t}\big(\frac{1}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\big)-\frac{u^{\varepsilon}}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon}+\mu\Gamma{\rm div\,}{\mathcal{L}}u^{\varepsilon}\bigg)

that
\partial_{t}\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{u^{\varepsilon}}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\big)
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}([0,T_{1}],H^{-1}(\Omega)),
which, combined with the uniform boundedness of

\frac{u^{\varepsilon}}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}
in
L^{\infty}([0,T_{1}],H^{1}(\Omega))
and the Aubin-Lions Lemma that
\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{u^{\varepsilon}}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\big)
converges in

C([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)).
We thus find by noticing (
14.6
) that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{u^{\varepsilon}}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\big)\rightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{u^{0}}{\beta(\theta^{0})}\big)\text{ in }C([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)).

Next, we derive from
the convergence results (
14.1
) (
14.3
) and the uniform estimates in
L^{\infty}([0,T_{1}]\times\Omega)

for
(\theta^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon})
that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{\mathbb{Q}u^{\varepsilon}}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\big)\rightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{\mathbb{Q}u^{0}}{\beta(\theta^{0})}\big)\text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)).

The previous two properties yield further that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{\mathbb{P}u^{\varepsilon}}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\big)\rightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(\frac{\mathbb{P}u^{0}}{\beta(\theta^{0})}\big)\text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)),

which, combined with the fact

\frac{1}{\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\rightarrow\frac{1}{\beta(\theta^{0})}
in
L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)),
imply that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{P}(u^{\varepsilon}-u^{0})}{\beta(\theta^{0})}\bigg)\rightarrow 0\text{ in }L^{2}\big([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)\big).

Consequently,
we find that, by noticing
\beta(\theta^{0})(t,x)\geq c_{0}
for all
(t,x)\in[0,T_{1}]\times\Omega,

\mathbb{P}(u^{\varepsilon})
strongly converges to
\mathbb{P}(u^{0})
in
L^{2}\big([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)\big).
The proof of (
14.3
) is now complete.
∎

To finish the proof of Theorem
1.11
, it now remains to show Proposition
14.1

which is based on the following Theorem:

Theorem 14.2
.

Assume that

(14.7)

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon},\partial_{t}\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon}\text{ are uniformly bounded respectively in }C^{1}([0,T_{1}]\times\Omega),L^{2}([0,T_{1}]\times\Omega)

\displaystyle\theta^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\theta^{0}\quad\text{ in }C([0,T_{1}],H_{loc}^{1}(\Omega))\cap C([0,T_{1}]\times\Omega),

where
\theta^{0}
enjoys the decay property (
1.39
). Let
q^{\varepsilon}
be a bounded sequence in
C([0,T_{1}],H^{2}(\Omega))
satisfying
\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{1}]}\|\nabla^{2}q^{\varepsilon}\|_{H_{co}^{1}}<+\infty
and
solving the equations

(14.8)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma}(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{2}q^{\varepsilon}-{\rm div\,}\big(R\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})\nabla q^{\varepsilon}\big)=G_{1}^{\varepsilon}+{\rm div\,}\big(R\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})G_{2}^{\varepsilon}\big)=\colon G^{\varepsilon},\quad\partial_{\textbf{n}}q^{\varepsilon}=g^{\varepsilon},

where

(14.9)

G^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0\text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{1}]\times\Omega),\qquad g^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0\text{ in }\,L^{2}([0,T_{1}],H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)),

then it holds that:

q^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0\text{ in }\,L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L_{loc}^{2}(\Omega)).

Proof.

This theorem has essentially been proved in Proposition 3.1 of
[
1
]
. For the reader’s convenience, we outline the proof detailed in
[
1
]
and more importantly, make some comments on the assumptions imposed in (
14.7
), (
14.9
) on

\theta^{\varepsilon}
and
G^{\varepsilon},g^{\varepsilon}
as they are slightly different from those in
[Prop 3.1,
[
1
]
].
To get the strong compactness in time, the idea is to construct the defect measures associated to the sequence
q^{\varepsilon}
and then prove it vanishes. More precisely, one first extends the functions to
t\in\mathbb{R},
(without changing the notation) and then uses the wave packet transform:

\displaystyle W^{\varepsilon}f(t,\tau,x)=(2\pi^{3})^{-\frac{1}{4}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{3}{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\big(i(t-s)\tau-(t-s)^{2}\big)/\varepsilon}f(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s

to write the equation (
14.8
) in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega:

\displaystyle P^{\varepsilon}(t,\tau,\nabla)(W^{\varepsilon}q^{\varepsilon})=W^{\varepsilon}G^{\varepsilon}+{\rm div\,}\big([W^{\varepsilon},R\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})]\nabla q^{\varepsilon}\big)-\frac{1}{\gamma}\big(W^{\varepsilon}\big((\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{2}q^{\varepsilon}\big)+\tau^{2}W^{\varepsilon}q^{\varepsilon}\big)

where
P^{\varepsilon}(t,\tau,\nabla)=\colon-\frac{1}{\gamma}\tau^{2}\cdot-{\rm div\,}(R\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})\nabla\cdot).

Next, by defining
\Theta^{\varepsilon}=({\rm Id}\,-\Delta)W^{\varepsilon}q^{\varepsilon}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{t,\tau}^{2}\times\Omega)

following the similar arguments in [Lemma 4.3,
[
45
]
], one shows the existence of the defect measure
\mu
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
associated to the sequence
\Theta^{\varepsilon}
and the existence of
M\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathcal{L}_{+}^{1},\mu),
such that, up to subsequence:
for any
A\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathcal{K}),

(14.10)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(A(t,\tau)\Theta^{\varepsilon},\Theta^{\varepsilon})_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\tau\xlongrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\text{tr}\big(A(t,\tau)M(t,\tau)\big)\mu(\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\tau)

Note that in the above
\mathcal{K},\mathcal{L}_{+}^{1}
denote the spaces of compact operators and non-negative trace class operators in
L^{2}(\Omega).

The remaining task is to show the following two facts:

(14.11)

\displaystyle P^{0}(t,\tau,\nabla)(1-\Delta_{N})^{-1}M(t,\tau)=0\quad\mu-a.e,

(14.12)

\displaystyle\text{Ker}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big(P^{0}(t,\tau,\nabla)(1-\Delta_{N})^{-1}\big)={0},\,\,\forall(t,\tau)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}

where

P^{0}(t,\tau,\nabla)=\frac{1}{\gamma}\tau^{2}\cdot-{\rm div\,}(R\beta(\theta^{0})\nabla\cdot)
and
(1-\Delta_{N})^{-1}
is defined as:

\displaystyle u=(1-\Delta_{N})^{-1}f\Leftrightarrow(1-\Delta)u=f,\quad\partial_{\textbf{n}}u=0.

Under the assumption (
1.39
) on
\theta^{0},
(
14.12
) has been shown more or less in [Lemma 5.1,
[
45
]
]. The matter is thus reduced to the justification of (
14.11
) where the assumptions (
14.7
), (
14.9
) on

\theta^{\varepsilon}
and
G^{\varepsilon},g^{\varepsilon}
are used. Thanks to (
14.10
), it suffices to show that for any
\varphi\in C_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),K\in\mathcal{K},

\displaystyle\varphi KP^{0}(t,\tau,\nabla)(1-\Delta_{N})^{-1}\Theta^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0\text{ in }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega),

which is in turn the consequence of the following:

(14.13)

\displaystyle\varphi K(P^{\varepsilon}-P^{0})(t,\tau,\nabla)(1-\Delta_{N})^{-1}\Theta^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow 0\text{ in }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega),

(14.14)

\displaystyle\quad\varphi KP^{\varepsilon}(t,\tau,\nabla)\big(W^{\varepsilon}q^{\varepsilon}-(1-\Delta_{N})^{-1}N(W^{\varepsilon}g^{\varepsilon})\big)\rightarrow 0\text{ in }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega),

where
N(h)
is defined as:

\displaystyle v=N(h)\Leftrightarrow(1-\Delta)u=0,\quad\partial_{\textbf{n}}u=h.

By the definition of
P^{\varepsilon}(t,\tau,\nabla)
and
P^{0}(t,\tau,\nabla),
we have that:

\displaystyle\|\varphi K(P^{\varepsilon}-P^{0})(t,\tau,\nabla)(1-\Delta_{N})^{-1}\Theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\theta^{\varepsilon}-\theta^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}\big([0,T_{1}],\,H^{1}_{loc}\cap L^{\infty}(\Omega)\big)}\|(1-\Delta_{N})^{-1}\Theta^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}\big(\mathbb{R}^{2},\,W^{1,\infty}\cap H^{2}(\Omega)\big)},

which tends to
0
. Therefore, by noticing (
14.7
) and
the fact
W^{\varepsilon}
commutes with the spatial derivatives,
and the properties

\displaystyle\|W^{\varepsilon}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2},L^{p}(\Omega))}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L^{p}(\Omega))},\,(p=2,+\infty),\quad\|(1-\Delta_{N})^{-1}f\|_{W^{1,\infty}\cap H^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|f\|_{H_{co}^{1}(\Omega)},

one finds (
14.13
).
Next, taking the benefits of the properties on
\theta^{\varepsilon},q^{\varepsilon}
we can follow the similar arguments as in [Lemma 3.3,
[
1
]
] that:

\displaystyle\varphi K{\rm div\,}\big([W^{\varepsilon},R\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})]\nabla q^{\varepsilon}\big),\quad\varphi K\big(W^{\varepsilon}\big((\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{2}q^{\varepsilon}\big)+\tau^{2}W^{\varepsilon}q^{\varepsilon}\big)\longrightarrow 0\text{ in }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\Omega),

which, combined with the assumption (
14.9
), leads to (
14.14
).
Note that in
[
1
]
, it is assumed that

\theta^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\theta^{0}
in
C([0,T_{1}],H^{s}),s>1+{d}/{2}
and
\|G_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([0,T_{1}]\times H^{1}(\Omega))}\rightarrow 0.
But it turns out from the above computations that (
14.7
), (
14.9
) are enough to obtain the desired convergence result.
∎

Proof of Proposition
14.1
.

Let
\tilde{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}=\sigma^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon}){\rm div\,}u^{\varepsilon},
we derive from the equations (
1.17
) that:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma}\varepsilon\partial_{t}\tilde{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}+{\rm div\,}u^{\varepsilon}=F_{1}^{\varepsilon},\quad\frac{1}{R\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}\varepsilon\partial_{t}u^{\varepsilon}+\nabla\tilde{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}=F_{2}^{\varepsilon},

where

\displaystyle F_{1}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\kappa\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon}){\rm div\,}(\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon})+\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\mu\varepsilon^{2}\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma^{\varepsilon}){\mathcal{L}}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\mathbb{S}u^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\gamma}\big(\varepsilon u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\sigma^{\varepsilon}+\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\varepsilon\partial_{t}(\Gamma{\rm div\,}u^{\varepsilon})\big),

\displaystyle F_{2}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\varepsilon}{R\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})}u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla u^{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\mu\big(\lambda_{1}\Gamma\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits\mathop{\rm curl}\nolimits u^{\varepsilon}+\nabla\Gamma{\rm div\,}u^{\varepsilon}\big).

Consequently, we find that
\tilde{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}
solves (
14.8
) with
G_{1}^{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon\partial_{t}F_{1}^{\varepsilon},G_{2}^{\varepsilon}=F_{2}^{\varepsilon},g^{\varepsilon}=\partial_{\textbf{n}}\sigma^{\varepsilon}|_{\partial\Omega}.

By the virtue of Remark
4.4
, we see that by assuming (
1.38
),
(\varepsilon\kappa)^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}\nabla^{2}\theta^{\varepsilon}
are uniformly bounded in
L_{T_{1}}^{2}H_{co}^{1},
which, combined with the uniform estimate (
1.32
), yields that:

\displaystyle\|(\varepsilon\partial_{t}F_{1}^{\varepsilon},F_{2}^{\varepsilon},{\rm div\,}F_{2}^{\varepsilon})\|_{L_{T_{1}}^{2}H_{co}^{1}}\xlongrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}0.

In light of the uniform boundedness of

|\!|\!|(\theta^{\varepsilon},\nabla\theta^{\varepsilon})|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,T_{1}},
we find that,

\displaystyle G^{\varepsilon}=\colon\varepsilon\partial_{t}F_{1}^{\varepsilon}+{\rm div\,}\big(R\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon})F_{2}^{\varepsilon}\big)\xlongrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}0\ \text{ in }L_{T_{1}}^{2}H_{co}^{1}.

For the boundary term, it has been justified in (
4.20
) that:

\displaystyle({\rm Id}\,,\varepsilon\partial_{t})\partial_{\textbf{n}}\sigma^{\varepsilon}|_{\partial\Omega}\xlongrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}0\,\text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{1}]\times\partial\Omega).

Furthermore, on can verify by using the uniform estimate (
1.32
) that:

\displaystyle\varepsilon{\rm div\,}\big(\partial_{t}(R\beta(\theta^{\varepsilon}))\nabla\tilde{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}\big)\xlongrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}0\ \text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{1}]\times\Omega).

The previous four estimates enable us to apply Theorem
1.11
to obtain that

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{\varepsilon},\varepsilon\partial_{t}\tilde{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}\xlongrightarrow{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}0\ \text{ in }L^{2}([0,T_{1}],L^{2}_{loc}(\Omega)),

which implies readily (
14.2
).

∎

15.
Appendix

In this appendix, we gather some useful estimates/facts and prove some technical lemmas. We begin with the product and commutator estimates
which are used throughout the paper:

Proposition 15.1
.

For each
0\leq t\leq T,
and for any integer
k\geq 2,
one has the (rough) product estimates:

(15.1)

\|(fg)(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k}}\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k}}|\!|\!|g|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+\|g(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k}}|\!|\!|f|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k+1}{2}],\infty,t},

(15.2)

\|(fg)(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k}}\lesssim\|(f,g)(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k}}|\!|\!|(f,g)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k}{2}]-1,\infty,t},

and commutator estimates:

(15.3)

\|[Z^{I},f]g(t)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|Zf(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k-1}}|\!|\!|g|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+\|g(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k-1}}|\!|\!|Zf|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k-1}{2}],\infty,t},\quad\,|I|=k,

or

(15.4)

\|[Z^{I},f]g(t)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|Zf(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k-1}}|\!|\!|g|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k}{2}],\infty,t}+\|g(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k-1}}|\!|\!|Zf|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k}{2}]-1,\infty,t},\quad\,|I|=k.

Moreover, on the boundary the following estimate also holds

(15.5)

\displaystyle|(fg)(t)|_{\tilde{H}^{k}}\lesssim|(f,g)(t)|_{\tilde{H}^{k}}|\!|\!|(f,g)|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k}{2}],\infty,t}.

Proof.

This proposition follows from simply counting the derivatives hitting on
f
or
g.
For instance, to prove the product estimate (
15.1
) and the commutator estimate (
15.3
),
one can use the following expansion:

(15.6)

\displaystyle Z^{I}(fg)

\displaystyle=\big(\sum_{1\leq|J|\leq[k/2]-1}+\sum_{1\leq|I-J|\leq[(k+1)/2]}\big)(C_{I,J}Z^{J}gZ^{I-J}f)+fZ^{I}g+gZ^{I}f,\quad|I|=k.

We can indeed have the more precise product estimate: for
j+l\leq k,

(15.7)

\|(fg)(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}\lesssim\|g(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}|\!|\!|f|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}+\|g(t)\|_{H_{co}^{m-1}}|\!|\!|Zf|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k-1}{2}],\infty,t}+\|f(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}}|\!|\!|g|\!|\!|_{[\frac{k}{2}]-1,\infty,t}.

∎

As a corollary of Proposition
15.1
,
the following composition estimates hold:

Corollary 15.2
.

Suppose that
h\in C^{0}([0,T]\times\Omega)\cap L_{t}^{p}H_{co}^{m}\,(p=2,+\infty)
with

A_{1}\leq h(t,x)\leq A_{2},\quad\forall(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\Omega.

Let

F(\cdot):[A_{1},A_{2}]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function satisfying

\sup_{s\in[A_{1},A_{2}]}|F^{(m)}|(s)\leq B.

Then we have the composition estimate:

\|F(h(\cdot,\cdot))-F(0)\|_{L_{t}^{p}H_{co}^{m}}\leq\Lambda(B,|\!|\!|h|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t})\|h\|_{L_{t}^{p}H_{co}^{m}},

where
\Lambda(B,|\!|\!|h|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t})
is a polynomial with respect to

B
and
|\!|\!|h|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}.

This Corollary, combined with Proposition
15.1
, leads to the following estimates:

Corollary 15.3
.

Let
\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma),\beta(\theta)
be defined in (
1.18
),

r_{0},r_{1}
be defined as:

r_{0}=\frac{1}{R\beta(0)}\exp(-\theta+\varepsilon R\tilde{\sigma}/C_{v}\gamma),\quad r_{1}=\varepsilon^{-1}(\exp(\varepsilon R\tilde{\sigma}/C_{v}\gamma)-1),

where
\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma-\varepsilon\mu(2\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2})\Gamma{\rm div\,}u.

Assume that (
1.34
), (
1.33
) hold.
Then one has the following
estimates: for
j=1,2,\,p=2,+\infty,X^{m}=H_{co}^{m}\text{ or }\underline{H}_{co}^{m},

\tilde{Z}=Z\text{ or }\nabla
or
\partial_{t},

(15.8)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Z}\beta\|_{L_{t}^{p}X^{m-1}}\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\tilde{Z})\theta\|_{L_{t}^{p}X^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\tilde{Z}\theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big),

\displaystyle\kappa\|\nabla^{2}\beta\|_{L_{t}^{P}X^{m-1}}\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla,\kappa\nabla^{2})\theta\|_{L_{t}^{P}X^{m-1}}

(15.9)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|({\rm Id}\,,\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla)\theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m-1}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\kappa\nabla^{2}\theta|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big),

(15.10)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma(\varepsilon\sigma)-\Gamma(0)\|_{L_{t}^{p}X^{m}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{p}X^{m}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\sigma|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}\big),

(15.11)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Z}\Gamma\|_{L_{t}^{p}X^{m-1}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|({\rm Id}\,,\tilde{Z})\sigma\|_{L_{t}^{p}X^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\sigma|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\tilde{Z}\sigma|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big),

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Z}(r_{0},r_{0}^{-1})\|_{L_{t}^{P}X^{m-1}}

(15.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\tilde{Z})(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{P}X^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\tilde{Z}(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}\big),

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}(r_{0},r_{0}^{-1})\|_{L_{t}^{P}X^{m-2}}\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2})(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})\|_{L_{t}^{P}X^{m-2}}

(15.13)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}(\theta,\varepsilon\tilde{\sigma})|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-2,\infty,t}\big),

(15.14)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Z}r_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{P}X^{m-1}}\lesssim\|({\rm Id}\,,\tilde{Z})\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{P}X^{m-1}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\tilde{Z}\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t})\big),

\displaystyle\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}r_{1}\|_{L_{t}^{P}X^{m-2}}\lesssim\varepsilon\|({\rm Id}\,,\nabla,\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2})\tilde{\sigma}\|_{L_{t}^{P}X^{m-2}}

(15.15)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}],\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-1,\infty,t}+|\!|\!|\nabla^{2}\tilde{\sigma}|\!|\!|_{[\frac{m}{2}]-2,\infty,t}\big).

When there is no time derivatives involved in the norm, we have also the following inequalities proved in
[
24
]
,

Lemma 15.4
.

Using the notation (
8.21
)-(
8.23
), we have the following product estimate and commutator estimate

(15.16)

\displaystyle\|(fg)(t)\|_{m}\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{m}|\!|\!|g|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}+\|g(t)\|_{m}|\!|\!|f|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t},

(15.17)

\displaystyle\|[{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma},f]g(t)\|_{0}\lesssim\|g(t)\|_{m-1}|\!|\!|f|\!|\!|_{1,\infty,t}+\|f(t)\|_{m}|\!|\!|f|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\qquad\text{ for }|\gamma|=m.

We will use often the following Sobolev embedding inequality whose proof is sketched in
[
43
]
.

Proposition 15.5
.

Let
\Omega
be a smooth bounded domain or an exterior domain, we have the following Sobolev embedding inequality: by using the notation (
1.11
),

(15.18)

\|f(t)\|_{k,\infty}\lesssim\|\nabla f(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k+1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k+2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|f(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k+2}}.

The following trace inequalities are also used:

Lemma 15.6
.

For multi-index
I=(I_{0},\cdots,I_{M})
with

|I|=k,
we have the following trace inequalities:

(15.19)

\displaystyle|Z^{I}f(t)|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla f(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k}}\|f(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k}}+\|f(t)\|_{H_{co}^{k}}^{2},

(15.20)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|Z^{I}f(s)|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\|\nabla f\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}\|f\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}+\|f\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}^{2},

(15.21)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|Z^{I}f(s)|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\|\nabla f\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}^{2}+\|f\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{co}^{k}}^{2}.

In the next proposition, we state some elliptic estimates which are used frequently.

Proposition 15.7
.

Given a bounded or an exterior domain
\Omega
with
C^{k+2}
boundary.
Consider the following elliptic equation with Neumann boundary condition:

(15.22)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta q={\rm div\,}f\quad\text{in}\quad\Omega,\\
\partial_{\textbf{n}}q=f\cdot\textbf{n}+g\quad\text{on}\quad\partial\Omega,\\
\int_{\Omega}q\,\mathrm{d}x=0,\text{ if }\Omega\text{ is bounded, }\quad q\xlongrightarrow{|x|\rightarrow+\infty}0\text{ if }\Omega\text{ is an exterior domain. }\end{array}\right.

The system (
15.22
) has a unique solution in
H^{1}(\Omega)
which satisfies the following gradient estimate:

(15.23)

\|\nabla q(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+|g(t)|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}.

Moreover, for
j+l=k,

(15.24)

\displaystyle\|\nabla q(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}(\Omega)}+|g(t)|_{\tilde{H}^{k-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)},

(15.25)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}q(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|f(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l+1}}+\|{\rm div\,}f(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}(\Omega)}+|g(t)|_{\tilde{H}^{k+\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)},

(15.26)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}q(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|{\rm div\,}f(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{j,l}(\Omega)}+|(f\cdot\textbf{n},g)(t)|_{\tilde{H}^{k+\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)}.

Lemma 15.8
.

Let
Y
and
K_{\pm}
be defined as:

(15.27)

Y=Y(t,t^{\prime},y)=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}b(\tau,y)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad K_{\pm}=\frac{1}{(4\pi{Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\frac{|z\pm z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4{Y}}}

where
b=b(t,y)
is such that

(15.28)

c_{0}<b(t,y)<\frac{1}{c_{0}}\qquad\forall(t,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}.

Recall the definition of the vector fields in
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}:

{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}=\partial_{y^{1}},\,{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}=\partial_{y^{2}},\,{\mathcal{Z}}_{3}=\frac{z}{1+z}\partial_{z}.

Let multi-index
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}),
there exists polynomial
P_{2|\gamma|+1}
with degree
2|\gamma|+1,

such that if
|\gamma|\leq m-3,

(15.29)

\bigg|{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}^{\gamma_{2}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{3}^{\gamma_{3}}\bigg(\frac{1}{(\pi Y)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{z}{2Y}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}b(t^{\prime},y)\bigg)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{1}{Y}P_{2|\gamma|+1}\bigg(\frac{z}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b|_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\infty,t}\big),

and if
m-2\leq|\gamma|\leq m-1,

(15.30)

\displaystyle\qquad\bigg|{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}^{\gamma_{2}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{3}^{\gamma_{3}}\bigg(\frac{1}{(\pi Y)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{z}{2Y}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}b(t^{\prime},y)\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m-2\leq k\leq|\gamma|}\frac{1}{Y}P_{2|\gamma|+1}\bigg(\frac{z}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}\big(1+|\partial_{y}^{k}b(t^{\prime},y)|+\big|\frac{1}{t-t^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\partial_{y}^{k}b(\tau,y)\mathrm{d}\tau\big|\big)\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big).

Moreover, we have the following property for
K_{-}-K_{+}:
there exist smooth functions
F^{\gamma_{3},j}_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}=F_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}^{\gamma_{3},j}(t,t^{\prime},y,\cdot),j=0,\cdots\gamma_{3}
such that:

(15.31)

{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}^{\gamma_{2}}(z\partial_{z})^{\gamma_{3}}(K_{-}-K_{+})=\sum_{i=0}^{\gamma_{3}}(z^{\prime}\partial_{z^{\prime}})^{j}\big(F_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}^{\gamma_{3},j}(t,t^{\prime},y,z-z^{\prime})-F_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}^{\gamma_{3},j}(t,t^{\prime},y,z+z^{\prime})\big).

In addition,
F^{\gamma_{3},j}_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}
admits the following properties:
if
\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}\leq m-3,

(15.32)

\sup_{0\leq t^{\prime}\leq t\leq T,y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\|F_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}^{\gamma_{3},k}(t,t^{\prime},y,\cdot)\|_{L_{z}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b|_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\infty,t}\big),

(15.33)

\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}F_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}^{\gamma_{3},k}(t,t^{\prime},y,\cdot)\|_{L_{z}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+})}\lesssim\frac{1}{(t-t^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b|_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\infty,t}\big),\forall\,0\leq t^{\prime}<t\leq T

and if
m-2\leq|\gamma|\leq m-1,

(15.34)

\|\mu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}F_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}^{\gamma_{3},k}(t,t^{\prime},y,\cdot)\|_{L_{y}^{2}L_{z}^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3})}\lesssim\frac{1}{(t-t^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b|_{m-3,\infty,t}\big)(1+|\partial_{y}b|_{L_{t}^{\infty}\tilde{H}^{|\gamma|-1}}),\forall\,0\leq t^{\prime}<t\leq T.

Proof.

By induction, we can find a polynomial with degree
2\gamma_{3}+1,

such that:

(15.35)

\partial_{z}^{\gamma_{3}}\bigg(\frac{1}{(\pi Y)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{z}{2Y}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}\bigg)=\frac{1}{Y}P_{2\gamma_{3}+1}\bigg(\frac{z}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}z^{-\gamma_{3}}.

Moreover, it follows from the basic facts:

(15.36)

\partial_{y^{j}}\big(\frac{1}{Y}\big)=-\frac{1}{Y}\frac{\partial_{y^{j}}Y}{Y},\quad\partial_{y^{j}}\bigg(\frac{z}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\bigg)=-\frac{\partial_{y^{j}}Y}{2Y}\frac{z}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}},\quad j=1,2

and induction arguments that, there are some (multi-variable) polynomials
F_{l,J_{1l},J_{2l}}^{\gamma_{3}},l=0,\cdots(\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}),J_{jl}=(\gamma_{j1},\cdots\gamma_{jl}),j=1,2
, such that:

\displaystyle\partial_{y^{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}\partial_{y^{2}}^{\gamma_{2}}\big(\frac{1}{Y}P_{2\gamma_{3}+1}\big(\frac{z}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}\big)

(15.37)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\sum_{{|J_{1l}|=\gamma_{1},|J_{2l}|=\gamma_{2}}}\frac{1}{Y}F_{l,J_{1l},J_{2l}}^{\gamma_{3}}\bigg(\frac{z}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}},\frac{\partial_{y^{1}}^{\gamma_{11}}\partial_{y^{2}}^{\gamma_{21}}Y}{Y},\cdots\frac{\partial_{y^{1}}^{\gamma_{1l}}\partial_{y^{2}}^{\gamma_{2l}}Y}{Y}\bigg)e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{4Y}}.

By the definition
Y=\mu\lambda_{1}\overline{\Gamma}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}b(\tau,y)\mathrm{d}\tau,
we see that:

\frac{\partial_{y^{1}}^{\gamma_{1k}}\partial_{y^{2}}^{\gamma_{2k}}Y}{Y}=\frac{\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\partial_{y^{1}}^{\gamma_{1k}}\partial_{y^{2}}^{\gamma_{2k}}b(\tau,y)\mathrm{d}y}{\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}b(\tau,y)\mathrm{d}\tau},\quad k=1,\cdots l,

which, by noticing (
15.28
),
can be bounded from above by
\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|\!|\!|b|\!|\!|_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\infty,t}\big)
if
\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}\leq m-3
and by

\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}}\big)\sum_{m-2\leq k\leq|\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}|}\big|\frac{1}{t-t^{\prime}}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\partial_{y}^{k}b(\tau,y)\mathrm{d}\tau\big|

if
m-2\leq\gamma_{1k}+\gamma_{2k}\leq m-1.

We thus finish the proof of (
15.29
) and (
15.30
)
by gluing (
15.35
) and (
15
).

Let us switch to the proof of (
15.31
). By induction, one can verify that there exist polynomials
P_{2l},l=0,\cdots\gamma_{3}
with degree
2j,
such that:

(15.38)

\displaystyle(z\partial_{z})^{\gamma_{3}}(K_{-}-K_{+})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(4\pi Y)^{\frac{1}{2}}}(z\partial_{z})^{\gamma_{3}}\big(e^{-\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4Y}}-e^{-\frac{|z+z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4Y}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(4\pi Y)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\sum_{j=0}^{\gamma_{3}}(z^{\prime}\partial_{z^{\prime}})^{j}\bigg(P_{2(\gamma_{3}-j)}\big(\frac{z-z^{\prime}}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)e^{-\frac{|z-z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4Y}}-P_{2(\gamma_{3}-j)}\big(\frac{z+z^{\prime}}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)e^{-\frac{|z+z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4Y}}\bigg).

Using again the fact (
15.36
), on can find some (multi-variable) polynomials
F_{l,J_{1l},J_{2l}}^{\gamma_{3},j},
such that:

(15.39)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}}_{1}^{\gamma_{1}}{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}^{\gamma_{2}}\bigg(\frac{1}{(4\pi Y)^{\frac{1}{2}}}P_{2(\gamma_{3}-j)}\big(\frac{z\pm z^{\prime}}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)e^{-\frac{|z\pm z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4Y}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}}\sum_{l=0}^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\sum_{{|J_{1l}|=\gamma_{1},|J_{2l}|=\gamma_{2}}}F_{l,J_{1l},J_{2l}}^{\gamma_{3},j}\bigg(\frac{z\pm z^{\prime}}{Y^{\frac{1}{2}}},\frac{\partial_{y^{1}}^{\gamma_{11}}\partial_{y^{2}}^{\gamma_{21}}Y}{Y},\cdots\frac{\partial_{y^{1}}^{\gamma_{1l}}\partial_{y^{2}}^{\gamma_{2l}}Y}{Y}\bigg)e^{-\frac{|z\pm z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4Y}}

\displaystyle=\colon F_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}^{\gamma_{3},j}(t,t^{\prime},y,z\pm z^{\prime}).

We thus finish the proof of (
15.31
). The properties (
15.32
)- (
15.34
) then follow from the direct computations.
∎

Lemma 15.9
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be the solution to the system:

(15.40)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}f+a_{1}\partial_{y^{1}}f+a_{2}\partial_{y^{2}}f+za_{3}\partial_{z}f-\nu b\partial_{z}^{2}f=F\quad\,(t,y,z)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{+},\\[2.5pt]
f|_{z=0}=\underline{f}(t,y),\quad f|_{t=0}=f_{0},\end{array}\right.

where
(a,b)=(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},b)=(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},b)(t,y),

b(t,y)>c_{0},
for all
(t,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
and the constant
\nu>0
is a placeholder of
\mu
or
\kappa.
Using the notation (
8.24
),
the following uniform in
\nu
estimates hold: for any
0<t\leq T,

(15.41)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|f|\!|\!|_{k,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda_{a,b}^{k}\bigg(\|f_{0}\|_{k,\infty,t}+|\underline{f}|_{k,\infty,t}+T{\Lambda}_{a,\partial_{t}b}^{k}|\!|\!|f|\!|\!|_{k,\infty,t}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|F(s)\|_{k,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg),

where we denote for simplicity

(15.42)

\Lambda_{a,b}^{k}=\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|(T(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}),b)|_{k,\infty,t}\big)e^{4(k+1)T|a_{3}|_{0,\infty,t}},\quad{\Lambda}_{a,\partial_{t}b}^{k}=\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},\partial_{t}b|_{k,\infty,t}\big).

Moreover, if
F=F_{1}+\nu\partial_{z}F_{2},
then for any
2<p\leq+\infty,

(15.43)

\displaystyle|\!|\!|f|\!|\!|_{k,\infty,t}\lesssim_{p}\Lambda_{a,b}^{k}

\displaystyle\bigg(\|f_{0}\|_{k,\infty}+|\underline{f}|_{k,\infty,t}++T{\Lambda}_{a,\partial_{t}b}^{k}|\!|\!|f|\!|\!|_{k,\infty,t}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{1}(s)\|_{k,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+T^{\frac{p-2}{2p}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}F_{2}(s)\|_{k,\infty}^{p}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\bigg).

Finally, if
\underline{f}=0,
it holds also that for
\alpha\geq\frac{1}{2},

(15.44)

\displaystyle\nu^{\alpha}|\!|\!|\partial_{z}f|\!|\!|_{k,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda_{a,b}^{k}\bigg(\nu^{\alpha}\|f_{0}\|_{k,\infty}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}{\Lambda}_{a,\partial_{t}b}^{k}\nu^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|(f,F)|\!|\!|_{k,\infty,t}\bigg),

and for
2\leq q<+\infty,

(15.45)

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{z}f\|_{k,\infty}^{q}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim_{q}T^{\frac{1}{q}}\Lambda_{a,b}^{k}\bigg(\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{0}\|_{k,\infty}+{\Lambda}_{a,\partial_{t}b}^{k}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|(f,F)(s)\|_{k,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg).

Proof.

We first show that the solution to (
15.40
) can be expressed in the following way:

(15.46)

\displaystyle f(t,y,z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}({K}_{-}-{K}_{+})(t,0,y,z,z^{\prime})f_{0}\big(X(0,t,y),z^{\prime}e^{-\Theta(t,0,y)}\big)\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}

\displaystyle+2\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{z}{K}_{+}(t,t^{\prime},y,z,0)(b\underline{f})\big(t^{\prime},X(t^{\prime},t,y)\big)e^{2\Theta(t,t^{\prime},y)}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}({K}_{-}-{K}_{+})(t,t^{\prime},y,z,z^{\prime})F(t^{\prime},X(t^{\prime},t,y),z^{\prime}e^{-\Theta(t,t^{\prime},y)})\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

where

\displaystyle K_{\pm}(t,t^{\prime},y,z,z^{\prime})=\frac{1}{(4\pi{Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\frac{|z\pm z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4{Y}}}

(15.47)

\displaystyle\Theta(t,t^{\prime},y)=\int_{\tau}^{t}a_{3}

\displaystyle(\tau,X(\tau,t,y))\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad Y(t,t^{\prime},y)=\nu\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}b(\tau,X(\tau,t,y))e^{2\Theta(t,\tau,y)}\mathrm{d}\tau,

and
X:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
is the characteristic associated to the vector
(a_{1},a_{2})^{t}:

\partial_{t}X(t,\tau,y)=(a_{1},a_{2})(t,X(t,\tau,y)),\quad X(\tau,\tau,y)=y.

Let

\tilde{b}(\tilde{t},y)=b(\tilde{t},X(\tilde{t},0,y)),\quad\tilde{\Theta}(\tilde{t},y)=\int_{0}^{\tilde{t}}a_{3}(\tau,X(\tau,0,y))\,\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad z=\tilde{z}\exp(-{\tilde{\Theta}(\tilde{t},y)}),\quad t=\nu\int_{0}^{\tilde{t}}\tilde{b}e^{-2\tilde{\Theta}}(\tau,y)\mathrm{d}\tau

It follows from the direct computation that
the function
\tilde{f}(t,y,z)=\colon f(\tilde{t},X(\tilde{t},0,y),\tilde{z})

solves the heat equation with constant variable in the half line (
y
is now considered to be a parameter):

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\tilde{f}-\partial_{z}^{2}\tilde{f}=\big((\nu\tilde{b})^{-1}e^{2\tilde{\Theta}}\big)(\tilde{t},y)F(\tilde{t},X(\tilde{t},0,y),\tilde{z}),\\[2.5pt]
\tilde{f}|_{z=0}(t,y)=\underline{f}(\tilde{t},X(\tilde{t},0,y)),\quad\tilde{f}|_{t=0}(y,z)=f_{0}(y,z),\end{array}\right.

which can be solved explicitly:

\displaystyle\tilde{f}(t,y,z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(E_{-}-E_{+})(t,0,y,z,z^{\prime})f_{0}(y,z^{\prime})\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}+2\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{z^{\prime}}E_{+}(t,t^{\prime},y,z,0)\underline{f}(\tilde{t}^{\prime},X(\tilde{t}^{\prime},0,y))\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(E_{-}-E_{+})(t,t^{\prime},y,z,z^{\prime})F(\tilde{t}^{\prime},X(\tilde{t}^{\prime},0,y),\tilde{z}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

where
E_{\pm}(t,t^{\prime},y,z,z^{\prime})=\frac{1}{(4\pi(t-t^{\prime}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\frac{|z\pm z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4(t-t^{\prime})}}.

Changing back to the variable
(\tilde{t},X(\tilde{t},0,y),\tilde{z})
and
still denoting it by
(t,X(t,0,y),z),
we find that:

(15.48)

\displaystyle f(t,X(t,0,y),z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tilde{K}_{-}-\tilde{K}_{+})(t,0,y,z,z^{\prime})f_{0}(y,z^{\prime}e^{-\tilde{\Theta}(t,0,y)})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}

\displaystyle+2\nu\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{z}\tilde{K}_{+}(t,t^{\prime},y,z,0)\underline{f}(t^{\prime},X(t^{\prime},0,y))\tilde{b}(t^{\prime},y)e^{2\tilde{\Theta}(t,t^{\prime},y)}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(\tilde{K}_{-}-\tilde{K}_{+})(t,t^{\prime},y,z,z^{\prime})F(t^{\prime},X(t^{\prime},0,y),z^{\prime}e^{-\tilde{\Theta}(t,t^{\prime},y)})\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

where

\tilde{\Theta}(t,t^{\prime},y)=\tilde{\Theta}(t,y)-\tilde{\Theta}(t^{\prime},y)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{K}_{\pm}(t,t^{\prime},y,z,z^{\prime})=\frac{1}{(4\pi\tilde{Y})^{\frac{1}{2}}}e^{-\frac{|z\pm z^{\prime}|^{2}}{4\tilde{Y}}},\text{ with }\tilde{Y}(t,t^{\prime},y)=\nu\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}\tilde{b}(\tau,y)e^{2\tilde{\Theta}(t,\tau,y)}\mathrm{d}\tau.

The explicit formula (
15.46
) then follows readily from (
15.48
) by changing variable
X(t,0,y)
to
y.

We are now in position to sketch the proof of (
15.41
)-(
15.44
). Changing the definition of
Y
in (
15.27
) by (
15
), we can check that the properties (
15.29
), (
15.31
)-(
15.33
) still hold after minor revision-just replacing
\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|b|_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\infty,t}\big)
by
\Lambda_{a,b}^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}
defined in (
15.42
). Indeed, the identities (
15.35
)-(
15.39
) used in the derivation of these properties remain formally unchanged. However, the properties for
Y
used to derive (
15.29
), (
15.31
)-(
15.33
) from (
15.35
)-(
15.39
) are now:

\displaystyle Y\gtrsim\frac{\nu}{c_{0}}e^{-2T|a_{3}|_{0,\infty,t}},\quad\partial_{y^{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}\partial_{y^{2}}^{\gamma_{1}}Y\lesssim\Lambda\big(\frac{1}{c_{0}},|T(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}),b|_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\infty,t}\big)e^{2T|a_{3}|_{0,\infty,t}}.

These properties in hand, one can verify that, by using
the convolution inequality in
z
and
t
variables,
for
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}=\partial_{y^{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}\partial_{y^{2}}^{\gamma_{2}}(z\partial_{z})^{\gamma_{3}},

(15.49)

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}|\!|\!|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}f|\!|\!|_{0,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda_{a,b}^{k}\bigg(\|f_{0}\|_{k,\infty}+|\underline{f}|_{k,\infty,t}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|F(s)\|_{k,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)

and if
F=F_{1}+\nu\partial_{z}F_{2},

(15.50)

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}|\!|\!|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}f|\!|\!|_{0,\infty}\lesssim_{p}\Lambda_{a,b}^{k}

\displaystyle\bigg(\|f_{0}\|_{k,\infty,t}+|\underline{f}|_{k,\infty,t}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{1}(s)\|_{k,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+T^{\frac{p-2}{2p}}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}F_{2}(s)\|_{k,\infty}^{p}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\bigg),\quad\forall\,2<p\leq+\infty,

and if
\underline{f}=0,

(15.51)

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}\nu^{\alpha}|\!|\!|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\partial_{z}f|\!|\!|_{k,\infty,t}\lesssim\Lambda_{a,b}^{k}\bigg(\nu^{\alpha}\|f_{0}\|_{k,\infty}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}|\!|\!|F|\!|\!|_{k,\infty,t}\bigg),\,\alpha\geq\frac{1}{2},

(15.52)

\displaystyle\big(\int_{0}^{t}\big(\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}\partial_{z}f\|_{0,\infty}\big)^{q}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim_{q}T^{\frac{1}{q}}\Lambda_{a,b}^{k}\bigg(\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}f_{0}\|_{k,\infty}+\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|F(s)\|_{k,\infty}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg),\,2\leq q<+\infty.

To show (
15.41
)-(
15.44
), it remains to control the weighted time derivatives. To do so, we
apply
(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},1\leq j\leq k
on the equation (
15.40
) and obtain the following equation for
f^{j}=\colon(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}f:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}f^{j}+a_{1}\partial_{y^{1}}f^{j}+a_{2}\partial_{y^{2}}f^{j}+za_{3}\partial_{z}f^{j}-\nu b\partial_{z}^{2}f^{j}=F^{j}\quad\,(t,y,z)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}_{+},\\[2.5pt]
f|_{z=0}=\underline{f}(t,y),\quad f|_{t=0}=f_{0},\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle F^{j}=(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j}F-\sum_{l=1}^{2}[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},a_{l}]\partial_{y^{l}}f-[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},a_{3}](z\partial_{z}f)+\nu[(\varepsilon\partial_{t})^{j},b]\partial_{z}^{2}f.

The term
\varepsilon\nu\partial_{z}^{2}f
can be expressed by using the equation (
15.40
) as:

\frac{1}{b}(\varepsilon\partial_{t}f)+\varepsilon(a_{1}\partial_{y^{1}}f+a_{2}\partial_{y^{2}}f+za_{3}\partial_{z}f),

which allows us to bound the term
F^{j}
as:

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k-j}\|{\mathcal{Z}}^{\gamma}F^{j}(s)\|_{0,\infty}\lesssim\Lambda_{a,b}^{k}\big(\Lambda_{a,\partial_{t}b}^{k}\|f(s)\|_{k,\infty}+\|F(s)\|_{k,\infty}\big),\,\forall\,0<s\leq t.

Applying (
15.49
)-(
15.52
), we achieve eventually (
15.41
)-(
15.45
).
∎
